FAMILY HISTORY SECTION -

Families and individuals who submitted histories and pictures of their family
will find them printed in this section.  It was impossible to put them in
alphabetical order because they had to be processed and printed as received, and
to fit space requirements.  You will find many family histories that were not in
the old jubilee book, and histories of the younger and branches of families that
have stayed in Kulm.  No effort was made to include information carried in the
1957 jubilee book, since this is a supplement to the previous book.  [Family
histories have been rearranged so that they will be in alphabetical order]


001.   ALWIN, FRED & DENA (Steen)

[picture]  Fred Alwin, a son of Fred and Mary Joes Alwin, was born April 24,
1892 near Prairie De Sac, Wisc.  He came to No. Dak. in 1925.  During the years
1927 to 1933, he spent some time in California and Nebraska after which time he
came to the Kulm area and worked on several farms.

On October 3, 1958, Fred was united in marriage to Dena Steen, daughter of
Ludwig and Laura Larson Steen.  She was born February 22, 1905 and has lived her
whole life on the farm one mile northwest of Kulm.  Mr. Alwin is a nature lover
and spends much time planting and caring for trees which he has added to the
"Steen Farm".

Mrs. Alwin has been active in 4-H Club work and this year will complete 25 years
as the leader of the Kulm 4-H Club.  She also has been a member of The Kulm
Homemakers Club since 1930.


002.   ANDERSON, ARLEY & JOYCE (Bjur)

[picture]  Arley, son of Mr. and Mrs. Gust Anderson of Kulm, was born December
9, 1903.  On June 1, 1952, he married Joyce Bjur, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Caleb
Bjur.  They have five children: Stuart, Vicky, Crystal, Grace and Scott.

Arley is a farmer.  He farmed with his father three years, then moved to his
Uncle Carl's farm, lived there ten years and recently purchased the Allen Ogren
farm 9 miles north of Kulm.


003.   ANDERSON, ARTHUR & MILDRED (Lundgren)

[picture]  Arthur Anderson, son of Mr. and Mrs. Peter Anderson of Willmar,
Minnesota, was married to Mildren Lundgren, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Signar
Lundgren, at LaMoure on June 26, 1937.

Mr. Anderson has been a depot agent for over 35 years, working in Minnesota and
South Dakota.  Mrs. Anderson was assisting postmistress while living at Kidder,
So. Dak.  In 1961, they moved to Willow Lake, South Dakota and have lived there
since.  They are active members of the Grace Lutheran Church and community
affairs of Willow Lake.

The Andersons have two children: Marlys (Mrs. Layton Arnald) a nurse, her
husband is a coach at the Langford, So. Dakota school and Arthur Jr., who taught
science at Selby, So. Dak., is presently working in the science dept. of
Honeywell's at Hopkins, Minn.


004.   ANDERSON, GUST & HELGA (Arvidson)

Mr. Anderson was born in Vestergotland, Sweden, September 30, 1895.  He came to
this country in August, 1914.  He came right to Kulm and the first years he
worked at the John Arvidson farm.  1918, Mr. Anderson started farming on his
own.  He bought school land in Logan County about 15 miles northwest of Kulm.
He cleared off the rocks and broke up this land and built the buildings on this
farm.

June 1, 1922, he married Helga Arvidson in Napoleon, North Dakota.  Mrs.
Anderson is also a native of Sweden and came here together with her sister Gerda
in 1910.

Mr. Anderson ran John Arvidson's threshing rig for many years.  The year 1936,
there was no crop because of drought, so that fall, Gust and his nephew Ralph
went to Sheyenne, North Dakota to work during threshing, where they had a good
crop.  In 1941, the Andersons moved to the John Arvidson farm which they later
bought.  In 1960, Mr. and Mrs. Anderson quit farming and moved to Kulm where
they purchased a home.

Mr. and Mrs. Anderson were charter members of the Gospel Tabernacle north of
Kulm and, since the Tabernacle moved in with the Assembly Church, they are
attending there.

Eight children were born to this union: Ardis, Eugene, Vincent and Arley of
Kulm, North Dakota; Judith, (Mrs. Ray Loven), Bismarck, North Dakota; June,
(Mrs. LaMoine Beaver), Rolette, North Dakota; Joseph of Minneapolis, Minnestoa;
and Rose Ella, deceased.  The Andersons have 21 grandchildren.


005.   ARVIDSON, JOHN & ANNA (Johnson)

[picture & supplements 1957 book]  Mr. Arvidson was born in Hundshult Staaland,
Swede, September 19, 1873, and came to the U.S. in 1891, and worked for the Soo
Line at Bismarck.

In the summer of 1892, he was sent to Merricourt to work, because the Soo was
extending their line West.  John drove spikes from Merricourt to where Kulm is
now.  When the line was finished that fall, he noticed there were quite a few
Swedes around here so he decided to stay here.

He married Anna Johnson in 1894 and spent the first winter at the home of his
wife's brother, J.A. Hedlund.

Mr. and Mrs. Arvidson filed on a homestead, and that farm is now the Vincent
Anderson home.

Mrs. Arvidson died in 1941.  Mr. Arvidson retired in 1944 and then moved to Kulm
where he died in 1950.


006.   BEICH, BERBERT & ALMA (Patzer)

[picture]  Herbert Beich came to Kulm from St. Paul, Minnesota in 1928.  Here he
and his brother, Otto, operated a barber shop for seven years.

On October 9, 1938, he and Alma Patzer were married at Eureka, So. Dak., where
they owned and operated the Gamble Store from 1939 to 1942, after which they
moved to the farm south of Kulm, where they are presently living.

The Beichs have two children: Larry, who is married to Linda Baily and lives in
Murfreesboro, Tennessee and Melody, who is presently attending college.


007.   BEICH, REINHOLD

[Refer to 1957 book]  Mr. and Mrs. Reinhold Beich, who at present own the Kulm
Bowling Lanes, celebrated their fiftieth wedding anniversary in 1966, have 4
children.  Arlene, Mrs. Wilbert Hildebrand, who lives in Panorama City, Calif.,
has 3 children.

Dean, a science teacher in Devils Lake, N. Dak.; their children are Doni Jo,
Kent and Jay.  Dennis recently completed his Masters Degree in music.

Joyce, Mrs. Bart Johnson, Canoga Park, Calif.  Joyce and her husband will
receive their degree in music in June.

Ellyne Beich, Mrs. Everett Esgate lives in North Hollywood with [her] 2
children, Susan and Michael.  Ellyne is a registered nurse in the country
hospital in Los Angeles.

Orion Beich, an optometrist, practices in Thief River Falls, Minn.  They have 3
children; Barbara, Steven and Terry.

Marian Beich, Mrs. Erwin Dennis and their children; JoAnne, RN, in Colorado,
Darrel and Sarah.  Marion is teaching in Omaha, Nebr.


008.   BERNTSON, LESTER & PHYLLIS (Nilson)

[picture]  Lester Berntson was born September 30, 1929, to Mr. and Mrs. L.H.
Berntson, who farmed 6 miles north of Kulm.  In 1931, they moved to the farm 2
miles north of Kulm, where Lester grew to manhood.

On June 30, 1956, Lester was united in marriage to Phyllis Nilson of Orleans,
Minnesota.  They took over the home farm and continue to make it their home.

They have three children: Cleo, Lowell and Neal.

They live on the former Louis Berntson farm.

Cathryn is at Binghamton, New Jersey, where she is advising counselor and
secretary of SNY State Health and Nutrition Association.


009.   BILLIGMEIER, VICTOR & ANNA (Enslen)

[picture]  Victor Billigmeier, son of August Billigmeier and Adelia Zink
Billigmeier, was born in McIntosh County, September 25, 1914.  He was married to
Anna Marie Knopp Enslen, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Sam Enslen of Fredonia, No.
Dak.  Their children are Marjorie Ann (Mrs. R. John Holmgren), who lives in
Karlstead, Minn.  They have a daughter, Lisa.  Harris Victor Billigmeier is
serving with the U.S. Army Engineers in Germany.

Mr. Billigmeier is presently working for the Isaak Elevator since 1954.  They
have lived in Kulm since 1950.


010.   BJORNSON, ANNA

[picture]  Annie Bjornson worked in the Kulm Post Office for 30 years, serving
as clerk for 8 years and becoming Postmistress in 1912.  She served under the
Republican and Democratic Administrations, until 1934, when Oscar Lange became
the Postmaster.  After the death of her husband Nordahl, called Nordie, in 1936
she bought his partner, Henry Kempf's share of the business.  [Mrs.] C.W. Ware
was the pharmacist for 18 years, until 1954 when she sold the Drug Store to Milo
Knudsen of Edgeley.

Annie has been Secretary and Treasurer of the Health Center since 1953, and has
also been active in club work for the betterment of the city.  She is a member
of the EUB Church, serving the past years as pianist, Sunday School teacher, and
as a member of the women's group of the church.  Annie celebrated her eightieth
birthday on April 1, 1967.


011.   BJUR, ALVIN & LILLIE (Johnson)

[picture]  Alvin, son of Mr. and Mrs. Jacob Bjur, was born in Swede Township on
December 7, 1904.  On November 25, 1925, Alvin married Lillie Johnson, daughter
of Mr. and Mrs. John Johnson, also of Swede Township.  To this union four
children were born: June (Mrs. Alvin Land), Arleth (Mrs. Vincent Anderson),
Clifton (married to Violet Doering), and Lyle.  All of the children live in the
Kulm community.

The Bjurs have been farming northwest of Kulm since 1932.  They are members of
the Assembly of God church.


012.   BJUR, CALEB & ELEANOR (Nyland)

[picture]  Caleb Bjur, son of Jacob Bjur, was born in Sweden, July 24, 1899.  He
was married to Eleanor J. Nyland, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Bottolf Nyland, who
lived 4 miles north of Kulm, on March 22, 1930.

Their children are: Verlye Bjur, who lives at home; Joyce, now Mrs. Arley
Anderson, who lives on a farm 8 miles north of Kulm; Arlene, Mrs. Arlene Hamlin,
who lives in Kulm; Virgil, who is married to Darlene Woehl, daughter of Henry
Woehl, and lives on a farm 4 miles NE of Kulm; Harriet, Mrs. Calvin Schlecht,
who lives in Canby, Minn., on a dairy farm; and Elaine, Mrs. Lloyd Gackle, who
lives in Fargo, No. Dak.

Mr. and Mrs. Caleb Bjur purchased the farm they are now living on in 1941.


013.   BLOMQUIST, ERLAND & HARRIET (Ogren)

[picture]  Erland Blomquist was born July 17, 1916 to August and Henny
Blomquist.  On November 28, 1943, he was married to Harriet Doris Ogren, the
daughter of John and Tilda (Malm) Ogren of Kulm, now deceased.

The Blomquists reside in Swede Township, where he has been farming since 1940.

They have one daughter, Glenna Marie, who is at home.


014.   BOHNET, JACOB & MARIE (Fode)

[picture]  Jacob Bohnet was born May 27, 1861, at Postal, South Russia.  In
1887, the family moved to the state of Kersan [Cherson], Roumania, where they
resided for 20 years.  In 1907, they arrived in this country and located in
Kulm.

Mr. Bohnet married Marie Fode in 1885.  She was born December 11, 1864, in
Postal, South Russia.

Although a blacksmity by trade, Mr. Bohnet was occupied here as a farmer with
the exception of nine or ten years, when he was employed by the Soo Line.  The
couple also lived in Fredonia for a short period of time.

The Bohnets were blessed with six children: Jacob and Rynold, four daughters;
Mrs. William Hiller, Mrs. W.F. Lee, Mrs. Harry Miller, and Mrs. Harold Rooney.

Mrs. Bohnet died in 1941 and Mr. Bohnet in 1954.


015.   BOHNET, REINHOLD & JUSTINA

[picture & supplement to 1957 book]  Mr. Reinhold Bohnet passed away May 27,
1958, at the Veterans Hospital in Fargo.  Leland Bohnet, oldest son of Mrs.
Bohnet, is Athletic Director at the University of North Dakota.  They have three
daughters: Madeline, now Mrs. Miles Midstokke, lives at Finley, North Dakota,
and her husband is assistant manager of the Farmers Elevator.  They have two
boys and one girl.  Marilyn, Mrs. Palmer Vareburg, lives in The Dells, Oregon
and has 3 boys and two girls.  Her husband is employed at an Aluminum Plant and
she is employed with a cannery.  Marilyn and Madeline are twins.


016.   BORTH, JOHN & LORENE (Isaak)

[picture[  John Borth, son of Mr. and Mrs. Michael Borth, was born February 6,
1909, on a farm northwest of Fredonia, No. Dak.  In 1914, he came to Kulm with
his parents, who had traded their homestead for a livery barn.  At the age of
eleven, he started working as a stable boy for his dad.  He attended school at
Kulm and started to play high school basketball while in the grades.

In 1926, he began buying cream and eggs until 1929 when John a Otto Kosanke
operated a meat market for two years, located at the present Dell Palm
residence.  After selling the meat market, John again went into the cream
business until he entered the service (Air Force) in May of 1942.

On June 2, 1942, he was united in marriage to Lorene Isaak, daughter of the late
Edward and Mrs. Isaak of rural Kulm.  During the three years in the service,
they lived in the following States: Nebraska, California and Florida.  While
living in Florida, a son, James O. Borth was born to them on the twelfth day of
January, 1945, at Winter Haven.  He is married and has a daughter, Cara Jo, and
they live in Valley City, No. Dak.

In May of 1945, John received an honorable discharge from the Army Air Force and
returned to Kulm.

In 1946, he was employed by the city of Kulm as street commissioner, water and
sewer supervisor.  In 1948, he again went into the cream and produce business
and kept the water and sewer supervisor job for about ten years, after which he
resigned.  At the present, John still owns and operates a cream station, and is
also employed at Fredonia, in the bank which is a branch of the Kulm State Bank.

John always has been, and still is, active in civic affairs and sports, serving
in such positions as school board, city council, civic improvement board, legion
commander, vice commander, service officer, athletic officer, legion baseball
manager and coach, and fire chief and assistant.

John and Lorene are members of the First Congregational Church of Kulm.


017.   BRANDENBURGER, FRED & ADELINE (Franz)

[picture]  Fred Brandenburger, son of Mr. and Mrs. Henry Brandenburger of
Forbes, was born February 25, 1917.

Fred spent 4 years in the Armed Service from 1941 to 1945.  He spent much of
this time in the southwest Pacific.  Upon leaving the service, Fred went into
farming.

On June 28, 1947, he took Adeline Franz, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. John Franz, of
Kulm, as his bride, and in 1952, they moved to their present home 11 miles south
and 4 miles west of Kulm.  This farm was homesteaded by Mrs. Brandenburger's
grandfather, John Franz, Sr., in the year of 1880.

The Brandenburgers have three children: Connie Jean, Anthony Fredrick and Steven
John.


018.   BRAUER, WILLIAM & AMELIA (Widmer)

[picture]  William Brauer, son of Mr. and Mrs. Christ Brauer, was born November
14, 1910.  He married Amelia Widmer, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Jacob Widmer of
Gackle, on October 19, 1933.

Mr. Brauer operated the Brauer Plumbing and Heating Shop from September, 1948
until February, 1967, at which time he sold out the business.  He is now self-
employed.

The Brauers have three daughters: Mrs. George (Fayne) Oberlander, Watertown, So.
Dak.; Mrs. Robert (Janet) Berg, Harvey, No. Dak.; and Mrs. Keith (LaVon)
Mattson, St. Paul, Minn.


019.   BROST, HILMER & HILDEGARD (Ross)

[picture]  Hilmer Brost was born on February 4 to the late Dave and Mrs. Brost,
at Kulm, where he graduated from high school, and later attended Dakota Business
College at Fargo.  He was employed in his father's business.

On August 13, 1944, he was married to Hildegard Ross, the daughter of Mr. and
Mrs. Carl Ross, of Medina, No. Dak.  In 1946, Hilmer became a partner with his
brother, Wilbert, in the automobile and implement dealership and they have been
successful in business for 21 years.

They have been active members in the E.U.B. Church, Lions, Firemen and various
organizations of the community.

The Brosts have three children: Allen H., a senior; Elaine H., eighth grade; and
Laura Beth, in the fourth grade.


020.   BROST, JOHN & MATHILDA (Herrmann)

[picture]  Mathilda Brost, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Freidrich Herrmann, Sr., was
born in Beresina, Bessarabia, South Russia on November 16, 1889.  At the age of
eight, she came with her parents to the United States, where they settled on a
farm near Kulm, in 1898.

On December 6, 1909, she married John P. Brost and they lived on a farm five
miles SE of Kulm.  On December 28, 1939, Mr. Brost passed away and Mrs. Brost
continued farming with her children until 1952, when she moved to Kulm at her
present location.

Mr. and Mrs. Brost were blessed with six children: Theophil Brost, Kulm.  He is
deaf and went to the School for the Deaf at Devils Lake, N. Dak. for five years.
Esther, Mrs. John Goehner, Dryden, Wash., has three children: Donald, Elvera and
Eldora.  All are married and live in California.  Lillian, Mrs. John Heinrich,
Dryden, Wash., has two children: Gorden and Theresa.  Rosalie, Mrs. Delbert
Merkel, Ellendale, No. Dak., has two children: Marcia and Mavis.  Ronald Brost,
Kulm, served in the U.S. Navy during World War II, lived in the Seattle, Wash.
Area for nine years and at present is teaching in the Kulm High School.  ElRoy
Brost, Kulm, spent five years working in a saw mill in Peshastin, Wash. And is
now farming the old homestead.

Mathilda Brost, ElRoy and Ronald are members of the Congregational Church at
Kulm, and Mathilda Brost is a charter member of the Dorcas Society,.


021.   BROST, WILBERT & IDA (Gehring)

[picture]  Wilbert was born in Kulm, January 20, 1911, the son of the late Mr.
David and Mrs. Kathrina (nee Ruff) Brost.

Wilbert attained his education, both grade and high school, in Kulm.  He
graduated in 1929, after which time he went to Chicago and worked in the
advertising business for three and a half years.  He returned to Kulm in 1932
and went into business with his father, which was then known as Kulm Hardware
Company.

On July 14, 1935, Wilbert was married to Ida Gehring, daughter of Mrs. Christina
Gehring of Garrison, No. Dak.  Ida attended Kulm High School and graduated here.
Wilbert and Ida have the honor of being the first couple, and all of their
children, to be graduates from the same high school.

Patricia is the oldest daughter and she is married to Wayne Hieb, son of Mrs.
Edwin Hieb and the late Ed Hieb of Kulm.  They have three children: Jonathan,
Jean and Julie.  Wayne is employed with the Kulm State Bank.  Brenda is married
to Donald Wendland, son of Mr. and Mrs. Otto Wendland of Kulm.  Donald teaches
industrial arts and Brenda is a medical technologist at the Mayo Clinic at
Rochester, Minn.  Frederick is married to Carole Bobzien, daughter of Mr. and
Mrs. LaRoy Bobzien of Ellendale.  Carole is a teacher and Frederick is employed
with Brost Bros.

Wilbert started in business in Kulm with his father in 1932 until 1946, when his
father sold his share to a younger son, Hilmer.  The name of the firm was then
changed to Brost Bros.  Wilbert has been in business for 35 years.

Community work has been of great interest to Wilbert.  He served on the school
board for twelve years, of which he was president for four years.  He was a
member of the City Council for twelve years and is now serving his second term
as Mayor of the city of Kulm.  Wilbert is a charter member of the Lions Club,
which was organized twenty-five years ago.  He served as president and zone
chairman of this group.  He also has been Fire Chief and president of the Kulm
Volunteer Fire Dept., and has belonged to the Kulm Alumni Association since it
was organized.  He served as president and secretary of this organization.  He
has always belonged to the Evangelical United Brethren Church.

Ida has been active in different organizations of the community and has been a
member of the Homemakers Club for 33 years.  She is also active in church work.


022.   BROSZ, EDWIN & LYDIA (Miller)

Edwin Brosz, son of Mr. and Mrs. John Brosz, was born at Kulm, No. Dak., June
17, 1917.  On October 5, 1940, he was married to Lydia Miller, daughter of the
late Mr. and Mrs. John H. Miller.

Their children are Karen, who lives in Seattle, Wash.; Dennis at Gwinner, No.
Dak.; and Rodney, Jarvis, and Gina at home.

The Brosz's have farmed since 1938, moving to their present farm in 1941.


023.   BROSZ, JOHN & EMILY (Haase)

[picture]  John Brosz was born in Mannsburg, South Russia, October 30, 1888, and
came to the United States with his parents in 1889.  His parents, Martin and
Helena Brosz, homesteaded 20 miles SW of Eureka, So. Dak. and later moved to
Leola, where they operated a hotel and general store.

John was united in marriage to Emily Haase, February 6, 1913.  She was the
daughter of Jurina and Martin Haase and was born, September 4, 1894, on their
homestead SE of Kulm.

Mr. and Mrs. Brosz made their home with her parents until the fall of 1913, when
they moved to Torrington, Wyoming, where they stayed until the fall of 1914,
when they came back to No. Dak.  In 1915, they moved to the Haase homestead
where they lived until 1921, when they built their own farm a short distance
away.  They lived through many hardships and also enjoyed many blessings.  They
retired in 1952, when they moved to Kulm.

Mr. and Mrs. Brosz were faithful members of the Hoffnungsfeld Congregational
Church until the doors were closed, when they joined the United Church of Christ
Congregational Church of Kulm.

They were blessed with 5 children, one of whom died in infancy.  Edwin is
married to Lydia Miller, who is living on a farm 10 miles SE of Kulm.  Their
children are Karen, Dennis, Rodney, Jarvis and Gina.  Adolph, who is married to
Violet Schelke, now living in Alpena, Michigan, [has] two children; Diana and
Mark.  Arthur is married to Betty Krueger and is living in Jamestown, No. Dak.
Their children are Julie, Timmy and Roxann.  Annie, Mrs. Larry Christensen of
Jamestown, No. Dak., has two children; Cathy Ann and Sherri Lee.

Mr. and Mrs. Brosz celebrated their Golden Wedding Anniversary in the summer of
1962, at which time they were richly blessed by the calls and visits from their
many relatives and friends.


024.   BUCHHOLZ, DENNIS & RUTH (Young)

[picture]  Dennis Buchholz, son of Theodore Buchholz, was born April 15, 1934 at
Ashley, No. Dak.  Ruth Young, daughter of Philip Young, was born July 5, 1938 at
Merricourt, No. Dak.

Ruth and Dennis were married June 8, 1958.  They have a son, Scott, and a
daughter, Jill.

Dennis graduated from Ashley High School in 1952 and attended the North Dakota
State University of Pharmacy, graduating in 1956.

He has been working in the Kulm Drug Store since 1957, and has also served on
the school board for seven years, presently serving as president of the board.

Mr. and Mrs. Buchholz are members of the Zion Lutheran Church.


025.   BUECHLER, MAX & PAULINE (Gieser)

[picture]  Max C. Buechler, son of Frederich and Kathi Buechler, was born at
Kulm on January 3, 1898.  On June 26, 1924, Max married Pauline Gieser, daughter
of Gottlieb and Rosina Gieser of Fredonia.  They have two children: Charles Wm.,
a deputy bank examiner with the State Banking Dept. at Bismarck, in the Small
Loans Dept., and Darlene Shirley married to Captain Robert Weir, pilot and
instructor for Universal Air Transport, Ypsilanti, Mich.  They have two
children, Robert and Cheryl.

The Buechlers have lived in Kulm and Fredonia all their lives.  Max started
working for the Pruetz Bros. In the grain and banking business in 1916 and has
now completed his fiftieth year in the banking business with his brother, Sharpe
Pruetz.  Max is the vice-president of the Kulm State Bank.  He will be honored
as a fiftieth year banker by the North Dakota Bankers Association this year.


026.   BUERKLE, ALBERT & ELLA (Mogck)

[picture]  Miss Ella M. Mogck, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. C.B. Mogck, and Mr.
Albert H. Buerkle were united in marriage April 5, 1934, at Kulm, N.D.  They
have resided in the Kulm area all their life and bought their home in the City
of Kulm, 1938.  Mr. Albert Buerkle is manager of the Farmers Co-op Elevator.  He
has been employed at the elevator since 1939.

Two children were born to the couple.  Dorothy was born on March 31, 1938, and
Lyle was born October 18, 1944.  Dorothy was married to Glen G. Goehring, March
22, 1960.  Mr. Goehring is employed at the Farmers Elevator.  They are the
parents of a daughter, Candace Marie.

Lyle A. Buerkle married Doris I. Wolf, July 9, 1966.  He is presently teaching
at Appleton, Minn.


027.   BUERKLE, JOHN & ELEANORA (Burkle)

[picture]  John C. Buerkle, son of Johannes H. Buerkle and Christina, nee Hehr,
was born in logan County, 3 miles east of Fredonia on August 27, 1904.  He
married Eleanora Burkle, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Rudolph Burkle, on August 27,
1936.

Mr. and Mrs. Buerkle were blessed with three sons: Gerald in Kulm, Richard
employed in South Dak., and Roger at home.  Mr. Buerkle passed away December 10,
1962.  He was an active worker in Norden Township and was assessor for 18 years.
He was also on the church council of St. John's Lutheran Church in Fredonia,
later serving at St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Kulm.  Mrs. Buerkle moved to Kulm
on Oct. 1, 1963.


028.   BUERKLEY, FRED & CHRISTINA (Burkle)

[picture]  Fred Buerkley, son of Henry and Dorthea, nee Nettle, buerkley, was
born June 8, 1899 on a farm two and one half miles west of Kulm.  It was here
where he was raised and grew to manhood.

His parents migrated from Russia to America in 1898.

The house that was home to Fred was not as modern as the homes of today, but an
old sod house.  Fred did many odd jobs on the farm, helping his father, but
mainly herding cattle, picking wild garlic and wild onions for his lunch.

Fred was married to Christina Burkle, daughter of Martin and Eva, nee
Oberlander, Burkle.  Christina was born on March 30, 1902, on a farm three miles
east of Fredonia, on her father's farm which is now known as the Henry Fehling
farm.  It was here on this farm she was raised to womanhood.

Fred and Christina were married in the Congregational Church of Fredonia, by
Rev. Nuetzmann, on December 30, 1919.  They made their home on several farms in
the surrounding communities and purchased the farm, now owned by Conrad Kinzler,
in 1941.  This farm had formerly been owned by Fred's father.

Fred served on the school board of Norden Township for 16 years.  Twelve of
these he served as president.  He was also active in other community affairs.
They were both active members in the Congregational Church.

Fred and Christina had many hardships in their life on the farm, especially in
the drought years of the 30's.  Fred has [had] his threshing machine for many
years did threshing for several years.

Eleven children were born to this union: Robert, Herbert and Vivian (Mrs. Edwin
Heidinger) of Kulm; Palmer of Fredonia; Floyd of Fargo, No. Dak.; Lucille (Mrs.
Melvin Walker) of Fargo, No. Dak.; Marilyn (Mrs. Howard Weiler) of Detroit
Lakes, Minn.; and Fredrick still at home.  Three died in infancy.

Mr. and Mrs. Buerkley sold their farm to Conrad Kinzler in 1956 and moved to
Jamestown where they are residing at 415 5 Ave. N.W.

They are both presently employed in Jamestown and are active members in the
First Baptist Church.


029.   BUERKLEY, HERBERT & ALICE (Woehl)

[picture]  Herbert Buerkley, son of Mr. and Mrs. Fred Buerkley, was born October
19, 1923, on a farm near Fredonia, North Dakota.

On November 11, 1951, he was united in marriage to Alice Woehl, daughter of Mr.
and Mrs. Gust Woehl.  They are now farming 11 miles south of Kulm, North Dakota.

They were blessed with 5 children: Herbert Jr. 14, Shirley Ann 12, Warren 11,
Carolyn 9, and Susan 6.

They are members of the Berlin Baptist Church of rural Fredonia, where presently
he is serving as Deacon and Sunday School Supt.


030.   BURKLE, RUDOLPH & LYDIA (Betch)

[picture]  Rudolph, son of Mr. and Mrs. Martin Burkley, was born April 25, 1894
in LaMoure County, 4 miles west of Kulm.

March 22, 1917, he was married to Lydia Betch, who was born in South Russia,
December 16, 1896.  She had come to the U.S., when she was 9 years old, with her
parents and settled on a farm south of Gackle.

After they were married, they lived on the old homestead of Martin Burkle until
1937 when the family moved to Devils Lake, where he was employed in the creamery
until 1944.  They then moved to Jamestown where he was employed by the Carpenter
Lumber Co.

To this union 6 children were born.  Mrs. Eleanore Buerkle, Kulm; Clifford
Burkle, Jamestown; Milbert, who was killed in World War II; Alvina (Mrs. Francis
Docktor), Jamestown; Rolland Burkle, Seattle, Wash.; Jona (Mrs. Duane Rohde)
Watertown, So. Dak.

They were lifetime members of the First Congregational Church.

Mr. and Mrs. Burkle both went to be with their Lord in February, 1960.


031.   CARLSON, FRANK & EBBA (Johnson)

[picture]  Son of Mr. and Mrs. August Carlson was born in Sweden, November 1,
1884 and came to the U.S. in 1887 with his parents, who homesteaded in Swede
Township.  He married Ebba Johnson, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Anton Johnson, in
1916 and built a home on Section 30 in Swede Township and farmed until they
retired in 1964 and moved to Kulm.

Mr. and Mrs. Carlson were blessed with five children: Francis lives in Detroit,
Michigan; Eldon married Tillie Doering, who farms NW of Kulm.  Children are
Angela, Allen, Cynthia and Delight; Harley married Ann Mari Lundgren.  Children
are Carol, Kenneth and Wendell.  Harley and Morris Sjostrom operate the J.U.
Case Agency; Delores, at home; Jean married Paul Liechty and [they] are farming.
Children are Mary, Emil, Lorne, Amy and Steven.


032.   CARLSON, LESLIE & VIVIAN (Erickson)

[picture]  Leslie H. Carlson, son of Mrs. Gussie Carlson and the late Carl
Carlson, was born August 6, 1916.  On December 10, 1946, he married Vivian
Erickson, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Gust Erickson.  They have three children:
Joan Marie, presently attending Ellendale College; Jane Linette and Debra June.

The Carlsons have farmed all their lives and have lived on the same farm
northwest of Kulm.  Recently, they have set up a small bakery in their home and
supply local stores.

The Carlsons are members of the Kulm Assembly of God Church.


033.   DAVIDSON, LEROY 7 JULIA (Malm)

[picture]  LeRoy Davidson, son of Mr. and Mrs. G.H. Davidson, was born July 11,
1909 at Kulm.  He farms nine miles north of Kulm.  He married Julia Malm,
daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Gust Malm, on June 29, 1940.  Julia was born January
29, 1911 and passed away April 8, 1965.


034.   DAVIDSON, MILTON & NEVA (Houge)

[picture]  Milton Davidson, youngest son of Mr. and Mrs. G.H. Davidson, was born
May 29, 1911 at Kulm.  He married Neva Houge, born Feb. 4, 1913 at Edgeley.  She
is the daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Clarence Houge of Edgeley.  They were married
Oct. 15, 1935 and live on the old family farm seven miles north of Kulm, in
Swede Township.

They have one son, Merlin, born January 24, 1938.  He married Gladys Kalmbach,
Nov. 2, 1958.  They have one daughter, Candace Ann, born Oct. 11, 1961.  They
live in Jamestown, N.D.

Milton was a member of Swede Township School Board several years, Swede Township
Telephone Co. board of directors several years, and is presently township
assessor.


035.   DITTUS, GERHARD & LEONA (Rudolph)

[picture]  Gerhard Dittus, son of Mr. and Mrs. Gottfried Dittus, was born April
21, 1927 in Logan County.  On July 1, 1953, he was united in marriage with Leona
Rudolph of Wishek, N.D.  To this union were born two children, Deborah and Lyla.

The Dittus's are living on the homestead farm of his parents and have farmed
since the death of his father in 1948.


036.   ELHARD, ALBERT & IDA (Heller)

Albert Elhard, son of Andrew and Katherina nee Fode, was born in 1904 in LaMoure
County, near Jud, N.D.  He was married to Ida P. Heller, daughter of Samuel and
Katherina Heller, in 1928.  They were active in farming until 1964, when they
moved to their new home in Kulm.  At present, Albert is employed by the Gackle
Bros. Inc.


037.   ELHARD, EMANUEL & EMELIA (Kuhn)

[Refer to 1957 book]  Emanuel Elhard, son of Andreas and Katherina Elhard, was
born in Bessarabia, Russia on May 6, 1893.  He married Emelia Kuhn, daughter of
Gottfried and Olga Kuhn, who was born two and one-half miles SW of Kulm on March
11, 1897.  They were married in Kulm on March 29, 1917.  The couple farmed for
many years in the Kulm area until retiring to their present home in 1954.  They
are members of the First Congregational Church in Kulm.

Mr. and Mrs. Elhard have two sons: George of Drayton, North Dakota and Reuben of
Kulm, and eight grandchildren.  One son, Milton, passed away July 4, 1949, at
the age of 21.


038.   ERICKSON, GUST & ANNIE (Bergeson)

[picture]  Gust Erickson, son of Mr. and Mrs. Johan Erickson, was born in Sweden
on February 20, 1898.  In July of 1921, he came to America and made his home
with John Arvidson, except for the time he worked in Chicago.  On December 7,
1926, Gust married Annie Bergeson, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Hans Bergeson of
Streeter.  To this union nine children were born: Vivian (Mrs. Leslie Carlson);
Phyllis (Mrs. Randy Land); Ernest at Rosemount, Minn.; Judy (Mrs. Phil Nelson)
Minneapolis; John at home; and three children who have passed away.  A son died
at birth, Norman and Sally (Mrs. Stan Lindgren).


039.   FEY, JOHN & VIOLA (Roeszler)

John A., son of Phillip and Rosina Fey, was born in Kulm, March 29, 1924.  On
October 1, 1949, he married Viola Roeszler, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Gottfried
Roeszler of Ashley, now of Sacramento, Calif.  The Feys have two children: David
Phillip and Becki Louise.

The Feys live in Seattle, Wash., where Mr. Fey works for Smith-Williston.


040.   FEY, ROBERT & IDA (Siewert)

[picture]  Mrs. Robert Fey, nee Ida Siewert, daughter of Andreas and Louisa
(Dickoff) Siewert, was born July 25, 1899 about nine miles southwest of Kulm.

Robert Fey had immigrated to this country with his parents at the age of four
years.  He spent two years serving his country during World War I and on
November 27, 1919, he and Ida were married and they farmed in the Kulm area for
23 years.  They then moved to Kulm, where Mr. Fey was employed by the Soo Line
Railroad until his death on May 2, 1961.

Their children are: Ernest, married to Deloris Herman, Missoula, Mont.; Marie,
Mrs. Herbert Hilscher, who has four children; and Ruby (Mrs. Russell Gackle),
who has two sons all of Kulm.


041.   FEY, THEO. & JOHANNA (Schlauch)

[picture]  Theodore Fey, son of Jacob and Christina Fey, was born in McIntosh
County on May 29, 1910.  On July 24, 1932, Ted married Johanna Schlauch,
daughter of Jacob and Christina Schlauch who lived fifteen and one-half miles
southwest of Kulm.  They have three children: Donovan D., married to Mary Louise
Kleingartner on May 10, 1959.  They have one son, Randy Ray, who lives on the
home farm and farms with his parents; Ivadell Y., in medical research at the U.
of Calif. San Francisco Medical Center, presently has taken a year's leave and
is a stewardess with World Airways; and Eileen J., attending college at NDSU at
Fargo.

The Feys are members of First Congregational Church of Kulm and Ted is a member
of the Rod and Gun Club.


042.   FISCHER, AUGUST & KATHERINA (Beglau)

August Fischer was born in Tarutina [Tarutino], Bessarabia, near Odessa, Russia
on July 24, 1868.  In 1886, he came to the U.S. with his parents.  Their first
stop was at Scotland, S.D., where they lived about a year, then came to McIntosh
County, N.D.

He married Katherina Beglau on Feb. 27, 1891, a Lutheran pastor, the Rev. Tzapf,
performing the ceremony.  They lived with his parents a short time then filed on
land in Antelope Valley, about 20 miles southwest of Kulm.  After two years,
they moved to a farm about two miles east of the then new town of Kulm.

After several trips to other parts of the country and to the Elgin, N.D. area,
they made Kulm their permanent home after 1919.  He was city policeman for some
time around 1924.

Mr. Fischer was a carpenter, and he and his brother-in-law, Peter Kurtz, built
many houses and barns in and around Kulm.

Mrs. Fischer, the former Katherina Beglau, was born in Beresina, Bessarabia on
May 11, 1875.  The Beglau family came to this country at the same time as did
the Fischers and all settled in the same area after arriving here.

The Fischers were the parents of eight children.  The eldest, Reinhold, a
veteran of W.W. I died about 16 years ago.  Mrs. John (Magdalena) Beglau died
about 10 years ago.

The surviving children are: Mrs. Gottlieb (Mary) Fischer and Mrs. Albert
(Otellia) Jenner, both of Lodi, Calif.; Mrs. Fred (Julia) Guthmueller, China
Lake, Calif.; Mrs. Charles W. (Emma) KaDell, Yucaipa, Calif.; Edward Fischer,
Aberdeen, S.D.; and William Fischer, Linton, N.D.

Mrs. Fischer died Nov. 17, 1928.  August Fischer died Jan. 15, 1957.


043.   FISCHER, WILLIAM & VIOLET (Bibelheimer)

[picture]  William Fischer, son of August and Katharina Fischer, was born on a
farm southeast of Elgin, N.D., Aug. 21, 1914.  The family later moved to Kulm,
where he received his elementary and high school education, graduating from Kulm
High School in 1932.

He worked on a farm, taught rural schools southwest of Kulm, attended Jamestown
College, Wahpeton State School of Science and Washington University at St.
Louis, Mo.

During World War II, he served with the Army in France, Germany and Austria.

Following his discharge in 1945, he took up the printing trade, worked for a
while in Lodi, Calif., then transferred to the Napoleon Homestead at Napoleon,
N.D.

On December 1, 1947, he became editor of the Wishek Star and in 1949 became
editor of the Emmons County Record at Linton.  At present, he is editor and
publisher of the Emmons County Record.

His wife is the former Violet Bibelheimer.  They were married April 20, 1951.


044.   FLEGEL, CHRISTIAN SR. & KAROLINE (Klukas)

Mr. Flegel was born in Kulm, South Russia in 1836 and married Karoline Klukas in
1856.  She was also born in Kulm, South Russia.

They came to the U.S. in 1889, as their oldest son Frederick was already here,
and filed on a quarter section one-half mile west of his place.  Their other
son, Christian came three years later.  They farmed until 1906 when they moved
to Kulm.

Mrs. Flegel died in 1913 and Mr. Flegel in 1923.


045.   FLEGEL, DANIEL & MONA (Elliot)

[This information supplements the History of the 1965 Jubilee Book]  Daniel D.
Flegel, son of Christian and Mona Flegel, was born in Kulm South Russia on
December 20, 1887.  On September 23, 1912, he married Mona Elliot, daughter of
Mr. and Mrs. Walter Elliot of St. Cloud, Minnesota.  The Flegels moved to their
present location where they have been farming since 1910.  Although they are
retired, they still remain on the farm, with two of their sons, Lyonel and
Bryce, operating the farm.

The Flegels have 10 children: Zona of Bend, Oregon; Sicily Ann, Bend, Oregon;
Elliot, Prineville, Oregon; Eloys, West Fork, Arkansas; Seaton, Bend, Oregon;
Winston, Prineville, Oregon; Bryce, Kulm; Morland, Ashland, Alabama; Lyonel,
Kulm; and York, Willmar, Minnesota.  They also have 23 grandchildren and 8 great
grandchildren.


046.   FLEGEL, FREDRICH

[Refer to 1957 book]  Genevieve (Mrs. Irwin Zinter) is in Browns Valley, Minn.
Mayo is married to Barbara Huck of Belevedere, Ill.  They live in LeSueur,
Minn., where he is an industrial engineer with the Green Giant Co.  Claire Marie
is on the high school faculty in Campbell, Minn.

They have two grandchildren, Dawn Irene Feil and James Fredrick Flegel.


047.   FLEGEL, LYONEL & MARJORIE (Miller)

[picture]  Lyonel Flegel, son of Mr. and Mrs. Dan D. Flegel, was born at Kulm on
October 1, 1928.  He married Marjorie Miller, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Henry
Miller, on June 12, 1955.  Mrs. Flegel was born September 26, 1934.  Mr. Flegel
farms 3 miles northwest of Kulm, where he has lived since birth.

Mr. and Mrs. Flegel have four children: Kelly 11, Crystal 10, Kurt 8, Luann 4.

Lyonel is a member of the Kulm School Board and a veteran of the Korean War.
His wife, Marjorie, is president of the Jolly Homemakers and the children are
also active in scouts and 4-H.


048.   FRANZ, JOHN

[picture; refer to 1957 book]  Mr. and Mrs. Franz have three children: Arthur
living 17 miles south of Kulm on the family farm; Adeline, now Mrs. Fred
Brandenburger, also living south of town; and Anna, Mrs. Clinton Knopp, who
resides in Jamestown.

Mr. and Mrs. Franz retired in November, 1952, and have made their home in Kulm
since that time.


049.   FREDERICK, LOUIS & JULIA (Steltzer)

[picture]  Louis Frederick, son of Henry Frederick, was born April 9, 1887 in
Russian Poland.  He served in the Russian Army for 3 years and came to the U.S.,
April, 1913.  He worked as a farm laborer until he enlisted in the U.S. Army at
Camp Dodge, Iowa and went overseas June 24, 1918.  He returned to the States in
1919 and worked as a farm laborer and married Julia Steltzer, daughter of
Heinrich Steltzer from Leipzig, Russia, on Feb. 13, 1924.  They lived around
Ellendale until Oct., 1949 and moved to Kulm as a retired farmer.  They have 10
living children: Helen, married to Isadore Schock, Lehr; Robert at Dickinson;
Gust at Forbes; Art, Edwin and Henry at Ellendale; Elsie, married to Jackie
Hayenga, Guelph; Ida, married to Curtiss Becker, Rapid City; Hilda, married to
Duanne Mogck, Minot, N.D.


050.   FREGIEN, EDWIN & IDA (Woehl)

[picture]  Mr. Fregien was born May 10, 1927 at Ashley, son of Gottlieb and
Bertha Fregien.  On October 9, 1955, he was united in marriage to Ida Woehl,
daughter of Gust and Christina Woehl of Fredonia.  Mrs. Fregien graduated from
the Kulm Public School in 1954 and taught one year of school.  After their
marriage, they moved to a farm south of Kulm and have been living there since.

They have one child, Brenda Ann, age 9, who was born Dec. 30, 1957.

Mr. and Mrs. Fregien are members of the First Congregational Church at Kulm.


051.   FREGIEN, GOTTLIEB & BERTHA (Hoffman)

[picture]  Bertha Fregien was born in the Coldwater community, McIntosh County
on January 31, 1902, daughter of John and Christina (nee Walz) Hoffman.  She was
baptized and confirmed in the Lutheran faith.  She lived on the farm with her
parents until her marriage to Gottlieb F. Fregien on July 9, 1922.

On the morning of September 1, 1925, the home and its belongings were completely
destroyed by fire.  The children, Esther, then aged 2 and Anna Marie, 6 months,
and a few clothes were saved.  Soon thereafter, a house was purchased from Dan
Patzer near Kulm and they moved to the farm.  The drought also presented many
obstacles as it did to many other folks at that time.

On July 26, 1945, Mr. Fregien bought the Christian Schimke farm 16 miles south
of Kulm.  On October. 13, Mr. Fregien suddenly passed away at the Aberdeen
hospital, leaving Mrs. Fregien and her four children.  The children are Esther,
Mrs. Joe Clarke, Aberdeen; Ann, Mrs. Orval Knoles, Spokane, Wash.; Edwin J. and
Harold W. of Kulm.

After the funeral, the family moved to the newly purchased farm, where they
continued to farm another ten years.

Mrs. Fregien spent some time in Spokane, Wash., employed as a nurse's aid in the
hospital and also in private homes.  After returning, she continued this kind of
work at Aberdeen.  In 1962, she purchased the Lydia Grosz home in Kulm and has
since made her home here.  She is a member of the First Congregational Church.


052.   FREGIEN, HAROLD & AUDREY (Lemke)

[picture]  Harold W. Fregien was born Sept. 13, 1931 to Mr. and Mrs. Gottlieb F.
Fregien east of Ashley.  Later the family moved 16 miles south of Kulm.  He
attended local schools and in 1948 enrolled at the Reisch School of
Auctioneering, Mason City, Iowa.  In 1953, he was inducted into two years of
military service, during which time he was united in marriage to Audrey Ann
Lemke, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Rudolph Lemke and they lived in Sheboygan, Wis.
Upon their return, Harold was employed at Brost Bros. As a salesman.  He later
purchased the former Ohmer Grenz farm.

Prior to her marriage, Audrey graduated from the local high school and attended
business college at Fargo and UND-IC at Ellendale and was employed there and
also taught a rural school for one year.

They have two children: Claudia Ann and Stuart Allen.


053.   FREGIEN, JOHN

[picture; refer to 1957 book]  After retirement in 1950, John and Hermina
Fregien started farming again from town until 1963, when they rented their land
to two of their sons-in-law.

For many years, he served on the school board, township supervisor and as
township assessor.  He was active in politics and a member of the Non- Partisan
League.  They were interested in community activities.  Their home was the scene
of many social gatherings.  They were always willing to lend a helping hand to
their neighbors.

They enjoyed going on short as well as extended trips with their family, which
included trips to Canada, Seattle Worlds Fair, and California, and in 1962 took
their daughters and sons-in-law to Hawaii.

In March of 1965, their family helped them celebrate their Golden Wedding
Anniversary by having open house in the church parlor.

This union was blessed with three children: Delores, Mrs. Gottlieb Hartman,
Kulm; Alice, Mrs. Lee Hollan, Kulm; Viola, Mrs. Alvin Moltzen, New Salem, N.D.;
and 7 grandchildren.

Mr. and Mrs. Fregien have been life long members of the Congregational Church.


054.   FREGIEN, THEOBOLD

[picture; refer to 1957 book]  Mr. and Mrs. Fregien's two daughters now are Mrs.
Conrad (Loretta) Kinzler and Mrs. Lee (Ella) Wendland of Alexandria, Minnesota.
They are active members of the first Congregational Church in Kulm.  The
Fregiens observed their 50th Wedding Anniversary on June 14, 1964 with an Open
House celebration at their home.


055.   GACKLE, ALICE

Mrs. Otto Gackle has lived in the apartment above Gackle Bros., Inc. store since
1964.  Otto passed away in June of 1958, and Alice keeps herself busy working in
the store, with church activities, and her travels.  She receives numerous
visits from her children and travels to the west coast occasionally.


056.   GACKLE, BRYAN & ARLENE (Nesset)

Bryan Ernest Gackle, son of Edith and Ernest Gackle, and Arlene Nesset of Fargo,
N.Dak., were married July 31, 1934.  Mr. and Mrs. Bryan Gackle are graduates of
North Dakota State University.

Bryan served as First Lieutenant in the U.S. Army.

Before going to Dickenson State College, he was Technical Director of the Little
Country Theater of N.D.S.U. and also served as instructor from 1959 to 1963.  He
received his Masters Degree in 1960.  He was the Director of Old Four Eyes, the
Theodore Roosevelt story, at Medora, N.Dak., for 3 years before taking his
present position at Dickenson State College where he is Chairman of speech and
theater and arts dept.  During the summer months, he directs the new Sosondowa
amphitheater on the college campus where he produces three different plays
during July and August.

Mr. and Mrs. Gackle have 3 children: David Bryan, born October 6, 1959;
Elizabeth Kay, October 13, 1960; and Kris Ann, born December 17, 1965.


057.   GACKLE, DONALD & PHYLLIS (King)

Donald Christoph Gackle was born April 12, 1929.  He is the youngest son of
Alice and the late Otto Gackle.  He married Phyllis King, a lab technician at
the St. Michael Hospital in Grand Forks, in January of 1951.  They have 2
children: Michael William, born March 18, 1952 in Baltimore, Maryland and
Cynthia Alice, May 18, 1955 in Fargo.

After his discharge in the U.S. Army Counter Intelligence Corps in 1953, he
worked for 10 years for the Greater North Dakota Association, as Public
Relations, advertising and publicity programs and as editor of the magazine,
North Dakotan.  In 1963, he resigned the position and purchased the McLean
County Independent.  He is editor and publisher of the newspaper in Garrison,
N.D.


058.   GACKLE, ERVIN & RUBY (Mogck)

[picture]  Ervin Gackle, son of Mr. and Mrs. Gottlieb Gackle, Sr., was born
September 12, 1912.  On November 5, 1935, he married Ruby M. Mogck, daughter of
Mr. and Mrs. C.B. Mogck.  They have six children: Gerald, married to Arlene
Alexander of Lisbon and they have 2 girls, Kim and Kari.  He is business
administrator of the Gold Mine in Lead, So. Dak.; Earl, married to Sylvia
Hettich of Kulm, [has] one girl, Jodi and one boy, Christopher.  He is employed
by Melroe's of Gwinner in Accounts Payable Dept.; Elaine, married to George
Olson of Canton, So. Dak. [has] two boys, Timothy and Michael.  He works for
Melore's in Parts Dept. City Desk;  Keith works for Melore's in the Production
Control Office; Joyce is a 9th grader; and Steve is in the 3rd grade.

Ervin has worked at Brost Bros. Since 1959.  Mrs. Gackle is employed at The
Farmers Union Oil Co.  In September of 1966, they purchased the former C.B.
Mogck home and make it their residence.


059.   GACKLE, FRED & MINNIE (Wittmayer)

[picture]  Fred Gackle, second son of George and Lydia Grosz Gackle, was born in
Emmons County, Linton, North Dakota, September 23, 1889, but resided in Kulm
most of his life.  He enlisted in the Army and served in Company B, 130th
Machine Gun Btn., from April 2, 1918 to May 3, 1919.

Fred married Minnie Wittmayer, of Fredonia, May 8, 1920.  He was the father of
four children--Eugene, Roger, Lillian and Norma Jean.  The children all
graduated from Kulm High School.  While living in Kulm, Fred was associated with
his brothers in the implement, hardware, and grain elevator business.

In 1946, the family moved to Eugene, Oregon, where he lived until his death,
October 28, 1955, at the age of 66.

Minnie remarried November 15, 1958 to John J. (Jack) Campbell of Detroit Lakes,
Minnesota, and they now live in Eugene, Oregon since Jack's retirement.

Eugene (Gene) and wife Anne live in Santa Maria, California, working for
Lockheed Aircraft Corp., Missile Division, Vandenburg Air Force Base.  They have
three children.

Roger and wife Thresa live in Kent, Washington.  He is employed at United Air
Lines.  They have five children.  Roger is a veteran of World War II and served
with the Army Air Corps from 1941 until 1945.

Lillian, or Mrs. E.C. Couch, has been traveling with her husband who has been
working in Turkey, West Pakistan, and India since February, 1963.  Eugene,
Oregon is their home.  [They] have one son, Roger.

Norma Jean, or Mrs. Martin L. Lien, lives in Eugene, Oregon.  [She is a]
Housewife and instructor at the Lane Community College, Home Economics Division.
Mr. Martin works for Lane County, Oregon.


060.   GACKLE, GEORGE & CAROL (Lake)

George D. Gackle, son of the late George Gackle and Mrs. George (Hildebrand)
Gackle, was born at Kulm on January 7, 1925.  George attended grade school and
high school here and was graduated in 1942.  He attended DBC in Fargo for about
18 months.

George was married to Carol Joan Lake, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. H.L. Lake of
Grand Forks, North Dakota, on June 12, 1949.  They moved to Minneapolis in 1960
and previously had lived in Clinton, Iowa for four years.  George has been
employed by the Rahr Malting Co. of Minneapolis since 1965 as Chief Accountant.
Mr. Gackle is a Certified Public Accountant and his wife, Carol, teaches first
grade in the Bloomington, Minnesota Schools.

Two daughters were born to Mr. and Mrs. Gackle.  Debra Joan, 17 years old, lives
with her parents and will be a senior in the fall of 1967.  She spent her junior
year in Tokyo, Japan, where she lived in the home of Mr. and Mrs. George
Parrott, who are missionaries in Tokyo.  Laurel Kay, [lives with her] parents
and will be a freshman in the fall of 1967.  She is very active and enjoys
skiing with her father.


061.   GACKLE, HENRY & BARBARA (Koenig)

[picture]  Henry Gackle, son of Gottlieb Gackle Sr. and Christine Haag Gackle,
was born November 24, 1909 at Kulm.  He married Barbara Koenig, daughter of the
late John Koenig and Rose Koenig of Lehr, on October 21, 1933.

The Gackles have been operating the Gackle Grocery ever since February of 1948.

They have two sons and one daughter: James married Arlene Brennan of McVille,
N.D. and [they] have four children: Dale, Michael, Todd and Mark.  Their home is
at Fargo, N.D.

Lloyd married Elaine Bjur, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Caleb Bjur of Kulm.  They
have three children: Randy, Jody and Tim.  They also live in Fargo.

Janice married Dean Bennett of Jud and [they] have one daughter, Amy Barbara.
Mr. Bennett will be teaching in Ashley this school term.


062.   GACKLE, LEROY & MARION (Jantz)

LeRoy Gackle, son of Mr. and Mrs. Gottlieb Gackle, was born at Kulm on October
1, 1929.  He united in marriage with Marion Jantz, daughter of Mr. and Mrs.
Philip Jantz, on October 28, 1951.

Mr. Gackle has been farming since 1951, although he moved to town in the fall of
1956.  The couple reside 3 blocks east of main street in Kulm.  They have two
children, Warren, age 10 and Kathleen 8.  The Gackles are members of Zion
Lutheran Church in Kulm


063.   GACKLE, ROBERT & BERNICE (Laeger)

[picture]  Robert Otto Gackle, oldest son of Alice and the late Otto Gackle, was
born March 26, 1925.  He married Bernice Laeger, daughter of Helen and the late
August Laeger, October 25, 1947.  To this union 3 children were born: Wesley
Robert, November 15, 1950; Roberta Bernice, March 28, 1953; Ann Louise, July 13,
1965.  Robert is affiliated with Gackle Bros. Inc. and began working for the
firm in 1946, after serving 3 years in the U.S.N., spending 18 months in the
Pacific War Theater.  For the past 12 years, he has been chief of the Volunteer
Fire Dept.


064.   GACKLE, ROY DANIEL

Roy Gackle, second son of Bertha and the late George Gackle, graduated from Kulm
High School in 1944.  Shortly after graduation, he entered the United States
Navy and served in the Medical Corps for 2 years.  After his discharge from the
Armed Forces, he attended 1 year at the University of North Dakota in Grand
Forks.  He attended and graduated from the Los Angeles Marinello School of
Cosmetology, receiving his OHFC.  For the past 16 years, he has been an
instructor in the San Francisco and San Rafael Marinello School of Beauty.  He
is presently living in San Rafael, California.


065.   GACKLE, RUSSELL & RUBY (Fey)

Russell, son of Elsie and Gottlieb Gackle, was born in Kulm, March 5, 1932.  He
was married to Ruby Fey, daughter of Ida and the late Robert Fey, on November
28, 1954.  They have 2 sons, Darrell and Dale.

Russell served from March 1952 to March 1954 in the U.S. Marine Corps, seeing
action in the Korean War.  After service, he attended a barber college in Fargo
and is a licensed master barber.  He began working for Gackle Bros. Inc. in 1955
as a serviceman and in 1959 became their parts man.


066.   GACKLE, WILLIAM & MERILYN (Goehner)

[picture]  William Frederick Gackle, second son of Alice and the late Otto
Gackle, was born in Kulm, December 7, 1927.  Mrs. Gackle, the former Merilyn
Goehner, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Martin Goehner of Jamestown, was born April 2,

April 1, 1951; David William, January 7, 1953; Mary Alice, July 9, 1957; and

courses after his service.  He was in the national Guard 6 years.  He is active
in politics, having served in the House of Representatives in the 1963 and 1967
sessions.  The Gackles live on a farm in Pomona View Township.


067.   GALL, EMANUEL & ROSE (Janke)

[picture]  Emanuel Gall, son of John and Magdalena Gall, was born at Wishek,
N.D., April 18, 1910.  Rose Gall, daughter of John and Lydia Janke, was born at

Mr. and Mrs. Gall were married by Rev. Bernard Moos on November 28, 1935 in the
Congregational New Beresina Church.  (Now the Nickisch Funeral Home).

After living at Wishek for 3 years, they moved to Kulm and began farming on a
farm 4 miles east of Kulm, which was homesteaded and built by Mrs. Gall's
grandfather, Joseph Konrad.  They later bought their own farm and have farmed in
Pomona View Township for 30 years.

Mr. Gall served on the Pomona View school board for many years, and also as
supervisor until the past year.  He is also a school bus driver and a member of
the Rod and Gun Club.

Mr. and Mrs. Gall now live in Kulm.  Both have been life-long members of the
Congregational Church and both sing in the choir.  Mrs. Gall also enjoys being a
member of the Dorcas Society, one of the leaders in the Boys and Girls
Fellowship and the Harmony Homemakers Club.

Two children were born to Mr. and Mrs. Gall.  Donald John, deceased, and Arlyce
Joan, who with her husband Harris Widmer and son, Keith live at Fargo.


068.   GEORGE, OSWALD & CLARA (Keller)

On May 28, 1943, he was married to Clara Keller, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Henry
Keller of Kulm.

Ossie served in the U.S. Navy during W.W. II and was later again called into
active duty during the Korean conflict for six months.  After his discharge, he
worked for F.C. Smith, the electrician.  Later, he attended Science School at
Wahpteon and received his license as electrician and then started a business of
his own in 1957.

Ossie was a member of the Norden school board for two years and is a member of
the American Legion, Lions Club, Fireman, Rod and Gun Club and of the town
board.

The Georges' have three children: Patricia of Jamestown, Morris and Vicki of
Kulm.

070.   GOEHNER, FRED & CHRISTINA (Rath)

[picture]  Fred J. Goehner, son of Johannes, or John, Goehner and Eva nee

U.S. from Beresina, Bessarabia, South Russia and settled in Dickey County in
German Township where they homesteaded.  They were engaged in farming until 1920
when they retired and made their home in Kulm.  The son, Fred, started farming
on the home place.

Christina Rath, daughter of Katherina and Christoph Rath, and Fred were married,
Nov. 10, 1921.  Christoph Rath came to the U.S. in 1903 from Albota, South
Russia, and settled on a farm near Merricourt.

Two children were born to this union.  Norman, August 8, 1927 and Vernon,
September 24, 1932.  Norman lives in Minneapolis and works for a finance
company.  His children are Gregory, Cynthia and Carolyn.  Vernon lives in
Spearfish, S.D. and is working for the Homestake Mine in Lead, S.D.  His
children are Pamela, Steve, Bonnie and Connie-twins, and Roger.

The Goehners were engaged in farming for 45 years and enjoyed many hardships and
blessings together.  They sold their farm in 1965 and they moved to Kulm where
they purchased a home.  They are both active in the First Congregational Church
in Kulm.

077.   GROSZ, DANIEL JR.

[picture]  Daniel Grosz Jr. was born in South Russia in 1869.  At the age of 8,
he came to this country with his parents, the John Grosz's, who settled in
Scotland, S.D.

In 1892, he married Magdaline Schlechter and again with his parents moved to
Kulm.  Here he started a general merchandise store with his brother, Godfrey and
father.

They were the parents of Clara Grosz, the first child born in Kulm on December
20, 1893.

He was very active in local and civic affairs.  He helped build and was a
charter member of the First Congregational Church.  He also took part on the
city council and school and township boards.

Mrs. Grosz died in 1910, leaving her husband and 10 children.

Mr. Grosz married Wilhelmina Tobler in June, 1911.  She was born in New Elft,
South Russia in 1874 and was a Registered Nurse in a Russian hospital before
coming to this country in 1905 and homesteading on 160 acres of land about 20
miles south of Kulm, known then as Wirch.  She often performed the work of a
midwife in that community.

One son, Elmer, was born to them.

In 1920, they moved to a farm one and one-half miles north of Kulm, where they
lived until 1936, when poor health forced him to retire.  They then moved back
into town into their first home, occupied now by Mrs. Eddie Grosz.  Mr. Grosz
died in December of 1936.

Mrs. Grosz married Frederick Kost in 1940 and moved to Parkston, S.D.  After Mr.
Kost's death in 1948, she moved to Glendive, Calif. And made her home with her
son, Elmer and his family until her death on Feb. 2, 1966.

Their children were Clara, Mrs. Robert Hollan, deceased; Albert, St. Cloud,
Minn.; Lydia Dimmick, Kent, Wash.; Leona McSweeney, Glendive, Calif.; Eddie,
deceased; Bill, deceased; Alvin, deceased; Harry, Seattle, Wash.; Milton, Costa
Mesa, Calif. And Elmer, Glendale, Calif.


078.   GROSZ, DAVID & THERESA (Weispfenning)

[picture]  Mr. David Grosz was born on April 27, 1896 to August and Christina
9nee Breitgreitz) Grosz in Kulm, So. Russia.  He immigrated here with his
parents, settling near Parkston, South Dakota.  Later, they moved to McIntosh
County, No. Dak.  Mr. Grosz spent from 1917 to 1919 in the service during World

Five children were born to this union.  They are Earl, who arms in McIntosh
County and has seven children: Willard who also farms in McIntosh Co.,; Lucille,
Mrs. Herman Munsch of near Fredonia; Eliza, Mrs. Reinie Harr who has four
children and lived in Kulm; and James who has three children and lives in
Breckenridge, Minn.

081.   GRUNEICH, ERWIN & HULDA (Schlauch)

084.   HANNELD, ALVIN & BERTHA (Doering)
