085.   HARTMAN, AUGUST & KATIE (Wendland)

[picture]  August Hartman, born in Wittenburg, Germany, on August 4, 1876,
immigrated to the United States with his mother, brothers, and sister.  His
father had passed away in 1883.

The boys were close to the age of compulsory military obligation in Germany
which was the major factor in deciding to leave the home land.

The family immigrated to Kansas and settled near Wakeenie in 1889.

A few years later, Joe, August's oldest brother, homesteaded in Dakota territory
near his sister Polly (Mrs. Joe Hollan).  After hearing of the land that was
still available for homesteading in Dakota Territory, the rest of the family
came in an immigrant car to the vicinity south of what is now Kulm.

For several years, August worked as a farm laborer near Ellendale, where he also
went to a country school for a few months during the winter.  In his early
twenties, he purchased half interest in the Taylor-Hartman wagon shop.  He held
his partnership until he married.

He was united in marriage to Katie Wendland on December 29, 1904, at the Joe
Hollan home.  Katie was born on February 13, 1879, at Neu-Elft, South Russia.
At the age of 14, she came to America with her mother, step-father, brothers and
sister.  They settled near Wirch, N. Dak.  Katie worked for her cousin, John
Wirch, for 3 years at $65.00 a year, then for Tom Shimmens for several years.
It was during that time that she joined them on a scouting trip to the Black
Hills with 2 covered wagons.  In addition to helping with the care of the
family, she drove one of the wagon teams.

The following summer, she joined them on a trip to the Yellowstone National
Park.  They took along the necessary food supplies, which included a cow.  There
were various other jobs she had in the Kulm and Ellendale area.

Mr. and Mrs. Hartman farmed in the Kulm and Wirch vicinities for several years.
They moved to Ashley in 1909, where he farmed and did custom threshing with a
steam rig.  His wife ran the cook car which followed.  She prepared meals for
anywhere from 16 to 20 men.  In 1915, they moved back to the Kulm area and
settled on the Joe Hartman Homestead where he continued farming and threshing.
In 1925, they purchased the Fred Sherbenski 640 acre farm 2 miles south.  They
lived here until they retired and moved to Ellendale in 1935.

Both August and Katie took an active part in community affairs.  They were
members of the Hoffnungsfeld Church.  He was township supervisor and township
treasurer for many years; was director of the Farmers Co-operative elevator in
Kulm; was a director of the Farmers Store in Kulm (located where the present
Drug Store and Bowling Alley are); they were charter members of the German
Northwest Farmers Union Local and took an active part in the Non- Partisan
League.

Because of their generous hospitality, they boarded the school teachers for many
years.  Their home was the scene of many impromptu social gatherings.  After
they moved to Ellendale, they joined the EUB Church and she was an active member
of their ladies aid.

This union was blessed with 8 children:  Lee of Kulm, N. Dak.; Ernest,
Ellendale, N. Dak. (deceased-1962); Gottlieb, Kulm, N. Dak.; Annie, Mrs. Howard
Haven, Bremerton, Wash.; Joe, Vallejo, Calif. (deceased-1954); and Alma, Mrs.
Jack Wells, Salem, Ore.; two children died in infancy.  There are 14 grand
children.

Mr. Hartman passed away in 1946 and Mrs. Hartman in 1966.


086.   HARTMAN, GOTTLIEB & DELORES (Fregien)

[picture]  Gottlieb Hartman, son of August and Katie (nee Wendland) Hartman, was
born March 26, 1911 in Ashley, North Dakota.  At the age of 4, the family moved
to the Joe Hartman farm 5 miles south of Kulm.

In 1925, the family again moved to two miles south on the Fred Sherbenski farm
in Northwest Township in Dickey County, which his parents bought (where he still
lives); after his parents retired and moved to Ellendale in 1935.  He and his
brother Lee, rented and operated the farm.

In 1938, Gottlieb decided to change partnership and on September 25th married
Delores Irene, daughter of John and Hermina (nee Netz) Fregien of Fredonia,
North Dakota.  The ceremony took place in the Gnadenfeld church southwest of
Kulm.

Because the farm was not large enough for two families, Lee and his family moved
back to Uncle Joe Hartman's farm; close enough to continue a help-out
partnership.

This union was blessed with three children: Gerald and Karen, both graduates of
North Dakota State University with B.A. degrees, and Larry attending Dakota
Business College at Fargo, N. Dak.

The new partnership was not too prosperous; their total gross income the first
year was $973.00.  With a healthy and enthusiastic spirit, being able to charge
the groceries and gas, using the cream checks for miscellaneous expense, and the
crop much better, they doubled their income the following year.  With the
enthusiasm they had of better crops and prices, they bought the 640 acre home
farm in 1941 at $8.15 an acre, and have since then purchased additional land,
making this one of the larger diversified grain and livestock units in the area-
300 head of Hereford cattle and 60 sheep.

Knowing that farmers had to be interested in their production beyond their farm
gate, Gottlieb and delores became active in Farmers Union, farm co- operatives,
politics, etc., and have since been elected and served on local and regional
boards.

At the present time, Gottlieb is president of the Farmers Co-operative Elevator
Company in Kulm and has served as vice president and secretary for the past 23
years; is director of the Dickey County Farmers Union since 1944 and president
for the past 14 years; is State Director of the North Dakota Farmers Union since
1961; is director of the Farmers Union Marketing Association of South St. Paul,
Minn. since 1963; is a member of the Dickey County executive board of the
Democrat-Non-Partisan League Party since 1952; is a member of the Whitestone
Battlefield Park Board since 1952.  Previously, he was township treasurer for 18
years; was on the credit committee of the Kulm Credit Union for 10 years, and
was one of the organizers of the Dickey County Crop Improvement Association.
Having leadership qualities, he has been appointed and served on various
committees throughout the years.

Delores, sharing her husband's views and interests in meetings, also had her own
interests and became active in various other organizations.  She is a charter
member of the Northwest Thrifty Homemakers Club, having held various offices and
is presently vice-president; she was elected Dickey County Homemakers council
president in 1948 and 1949; she is secretary of the German Northwest Farmers
Union Local; is president of the 28th District Democrat- Non-Partisan League, a
Women's Organization; is Dickey County International Farm Youth Exchange (IFYE)
chairman; is Dickey County Homemakers Safety chairman; is a member of the Dorcas
Society of their church; and is a past president of the Kulm PTA.

In 1963, Delores was one of 10 farm people of the United States chosen to go to
India for 10 weeks on a "Peace Mission" under Farmers and World Affairs.

This was a very interesting and challenging experience, and most rewarding.  10
people from India showed their gratitude by visiting at the Hartman home for
several days in 1964, while touring in the United States.

On Delores' return trip, they spent several days at the Holy Land, visiting
Jesus' birth place, Calvary, Tomb, etc.  She was very impressed.  Seeing the
places where Biblical events occurred brought a new understanding and
appreciation of studying the Bible.  They also visited at Cairo, Egypt; Rome,
Italy; London, England; and the United Nations in New York City.

Gottlieb and Delores are interested in people and they have welcomed visitors
from 14 different countries.  Their hospitality is most generous, having
increased the number to 43 people.  Their "international family" includes one
man from Chile; one from Ceylon; 2 IFYE students from India, who all spent one
month each on their farm, and 2 men from England, who each spent 9 months
working with them.  Their son, Gerald had spent 9 months in England under the
same Farmers Union program in 1963.

They have had hired help throughout the years, many of whom became near members
of the family.

Floyd Mittelstedt, a lad of 14 years old, came to work for them for a few days
in 1952, and stayed with them for 7 years while they helped him through high
school and college.

With their Farmers Union, co-operative, political and other activities, they
have attended many regional and national conventions, and have made many lasting
friends throughout the States and world.

In the midst of many meetings, conventions and a full social calendar, their
children honored them by celebrating their Sliver Wedding Anniversary in 1963.

Gottlieb and Delores are life-long members of the Congregational Church.


087.   HARTMAN, LEE & KATHERINE (Pfaff)

[picture]  Lee Hartman was born, December 26, 1906, on the Joe Hartman Homestead
(5 miles south and 1 mile east of Kulm, N. Dak.).  In 1908, his parents moved to
their Homestead near that of the Wirch's 16 miles south and 2 miles west of
Kulm.  After living there for 1 year, the family again pulled up their stakes
and moved to Ashley, N. Dak. to become "city dwellers".  While they lived in
Ashley, the elder Mr. Hartman farmed and worked as a custom-thresher.  1915 was
another year to move.  This time the family moved back to the Joe Hartman
Homestead and remained there for 10 years before making the last move as a
family to the Fred Sherbenski farm 2 miles south.  His parents purchased this
farm shortly before the move.

Periodically, during the winters, Lee would work for $10.00 a month for Robert
Fey, Fred Staeck, and Dan Harter.  Money came easy in those days!  In 1926,
Christ Roth and Lee satisfied their pioneer spirit by venturing forth in 30
below weather I a 1924 Model-T-Ford with no heater (it had good side curtains0
to Bullhead, S. Dak.  A trip of about 140 miles that took around 10 hours.  It
got a bit chilly at times so Lee took turns running beside the car to keep his
feet warm.  In Bullhead, Lee worked for Clude Hatch at $50.00 a month.  The
sleeping quarters were furnished by the management, a tent with 2 mattresses and
a couple of blankets.  The second year, Lee was promoted to combine operator and
earned $7.00 a day.

At the beginning of the depression, many farms were mortgaged  and up for sale.
The incentive to farm was not squelched and in 1932, Lee took over another man's
mortgage on the livestock and machinery for $1040.00 at 8% interest.  His
chattels included 17 cows and calves, 4 work horses, 27 sheep, plow, disc,
drill, binder, 2 wagons, and anything else that he owned.  He rented a farm from
Charles Trumbell (11 miles south and 2 miles east of Kulm) and also took on 140
of his father's sheep on shares.  One-fourth of all increase was paid to the
landlord as rent, one-half of the sheep increase was paid to his father, and he
kept the balance.

The feed and seed that he needed was purchased from G.R. Montaney at the
following rates: Barley-52 lbs. per bu., and Oats-40 lbs. per bu., both at 10
cents per bu.  1933 was a dry year, but the farmers managed to get enough hay
and feed for their livestock.  In 1934 (the Dirty 30's), there was no hay or
feed available.  It was extremely dry and there was no market for livestock
except for the government program which paid $20.00 per head for those over 2
years, $15.00 for those under 2 years, and $8.00 for calves.  Edwin Schulz and
Lee were appointed to ear tag all of the cattle that were to be sold to the
government.  They received 10 cents a head and worked from 4 a.m. until med-
morning and again from 6 p.m. until 9:30 p.m.  The rest of the day was spent
working on the country highway, blading with 3 horses at $1.00 per hour.

Because of the severe drought, Lee and 2 of his brothers (Gottlieb and Ernest)
went in search of feed and hay in the fall of 1934.  They were disappointed in
the Wyndmere vicinity, but successful in their search near Valley City at the
John Pfaff farm.  The hay was mostly pidgeon grass and stubble.  They made 90
tons of hay that fall and rented a vacant farm.  In October, they moved about 60
head of cows and horses from Kulm to southwest of Valley City with a saddle
horse and Lee's 1929 Chevy 2-door.  It took about 3 days to make the drive.
They remained there over the winter and moved the cattle home in the spring (a 5
day trip).

1935 was a good year for hay and feed, but there was no wheat because of rust.
1936 was still very dry but there was some carry over hay from the previous
year.  He kept 12 milk cows and 5 stock cows.  On April 18, 1936, Lee married
Katherine Pfaff (daughter of Mr. and Mrs. John Pfaff).  In 1938, Lee moved his
family to the Joe Hartman Homestead and purchased it in 1946 from the Joe
Hartman Estate.  He has remained there ever since.

To this union 3 girls were born.  Shirley Ann, who received her RN from Mercy
School of Nursing in Valley City, her BS from UND in Grand Forks, and her MSN
from the University of Colorado in Boulder, is presently a Captain in the Nurse
Corps of the United States Air Force and is stationed at Homestead, Florida.
Darleen Joyce, who received her BS from Valley City State College and has begun
work on her MPE, is presently an instructor in the physical education department
at VCSC.  And Sharon Kathleen (Mrs. Ken Ford), received her B.S. from Valley
City State College and is teaching junior high physical education in Norfolk,
Virginia, where her husband is stationed with the US Navy.  The girls planned a
surprise celebration for their parents' Silver Wedding Anniversary in 1961, with
relatives sharing this occasion with them.

Active in community affairs, Lee has been Secretary-treasurer of the Kulm FU Oil
Company since 1940, on the Supervisory Board of the Kulm Credit Union since its
beginning, Secretary-treasurer of the LP Gas in Edgeley since 1951, treasurer of
the First Congregational Church since 1960, member of the Selective Service
Board of Dickey County since 1951, a charter member of the Rod and Gun Club.
Previously, he had been a Rural School Board member for 8 years and Township
Supervisor for 9 years.  Besides many other committees, he was a life long
member of the Congregational Church and a member of the choirs for 25 years.


088.   HAUFF, DAVE & ANETTA (Dittus)

[picture]  Dave Hauff, son of Mr. and Mrs. John Hauff, was born in McIntosh
County on December 30, 1924.  On July 16, 1944, Dave married Anetta Dittus,
daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Gottfried Dittus of Fredonia.  To this union four
children were born: Darvin (married to Judy Arlt and living in Aberdeen, So.
Dak., where he is a senior at Northern State College), Myron (with the U.S. Army
in Fort Polk, Louisiana), Diana and Donald at home.

The Hauffs farmed south of Kulm until June 4, 1964 when Mr. Hauff was killed in
a car accident.  Mrs. Hauff and children remained on the farm until 1965, when
they moved into Kulm.


089.   HAUFF, JACOB SR. & ELIZABETH (Grosz)

[picture]  Jacob Hauff was born February 2, 1866 in South Russia, son of Mr. and
Mrs. Lucas Hauff.  He married Elizabeth Grosz in 1888.  She was born September
17, 1867, also in South Russia.  This union was blessed with eleven children:
Rosa (deceased); John of Fredonia; Maggie (deceased); Christine (Mrs. Gedien
Koth) Aberdeen, S.Dak.; Reinhold (deceased); Olga (deceased); Bertha (Mrs. Fred
Miller), Mennika, N.Dak.; Jacob of Kulm; Adolph of Sidney, Nebr.; Albert
(deceased); and Benjamin of Kulm.

Mr. and Mrs. Hauff came to this country in 1891.  They homesteaded about four
miles southwest of Kulm, which was known later as the VanNeaton farm and is now
owned by Otto Mueller.  They sold their homestead to VanNeaton in 1902 and
bought a farm in McIntosh County, ten miles southwest of Kulm, known at that
time as the Mueller place.

Mr. and Mrs. Hauff endured many hardships as did all the pioneers.  Edgeley was
the closest town when they first settled here.  Mr. Hauff even made some trips
to Ellendale for supplies.

The Hauffs retired in 1920 and moved to Fredonia.  They were both charter
members of the Congregational Church of Kulm and later were members of the
Gnadenfeld Church, which was the First Congregational Church in the State of No.
Dak. (it was organized in 1886).  When they retired and moved to Fredonia, they
joined the Nazareth Congregational Church.


090.   HAUFF, JAKE & ESTHER (Leischner)

[picture]  Jake Hauff, son of Mr. and Mrs. Jacob Jauff of Fredonia, was born May
18, 1902 in McIntosh County on a farm southeast of Fredonia.  He was united in
marriage with Esther Leischner, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Daniel Leischner, on
June 29, 1927.  Mrs. Hauff was born in Logan County, north of Lehr, N.Dak., on
May 12, 1905.

Five children were born to this union.  They are Earl, Floyd, Della, Gary and
Linda.  Gary passed away at the age of 1 1/2.

The Hauffs farmed in McIntosh County until 1945, after which they moved to a
farm northeast of Kulm and have farmed there since.  They have maintained
continuous residence in the Kulm area for 39 years.



091.   HECK, GOTTFRIED & IRENE (Siewert)

[picture]  Gottfried Heck, son of Christian and Mary Heck, was born in Dickey
County on May 11, 1898.  He lived in this territory most of his life with the
exception of a few years when his parents moved on a farm near Monango.  At the
age of 4, he moved back with his parents and lived near Kulm for two years.

On January 14, 1927, he was united in marriage with Irene Siewert, daughter of
John and Mathilda Siewert of Kulm.  Mrs. Heck was born and raised in the Kulm
territory and lived here all her life.  They have lived on their present farm
since October, 1931.

Mr. and Mrs. Heck were blessed with six children: Mrs. Art (Lillian) Rost, Kulm;
Mrs. Don (Florence) Liekhus, Fargo; Donald of Kulm; Mrs. Norm L. (Doris Ann)
Steiner, Beaverton, Oregon; and Arnold G. Heck of Sturgis, South Dakota.


092.   HEHR, ALBERT & IRENE (Hehr)

Albert Hehr, son of Mr. and Mrs. John Hehr, was born October 12, 1911.  On July
21, 1946, he was married to Irene Hehr, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. August Hehr.
They have 3 children: Sharyn, James, and John.

Albert Hehr is a veteran of World War II, having served in the army from 1941 to
1945.  Two years of this time was spent in the Pacific Theater, New Guinea and
the Philippines.  They farmed 5 1/2 miles southeast of Kulm until October 1955,
when they moved to Kulm.  Since, Albert has been working for the Farmers Co-
operative Elevator.  Mrs. Hehr is treasurer of the Kulm Credit Union.  Mr. and
Mrs. Hehr are members of the St. Paul's Lutheran Church.


093.   HEHR, AUGUST

[picture]  August Hehr was born January 31, 1883 at Dennivitz [Dennewitz], South
Russia to Mathaus and Wilhelmina (Kalk) Hehr.  On May 15, 1906, he was married
to Maria Kalmbach of Katzbach, South Russia.

On June 9, 1906, they left their native country to make their home in America.
They arrived at Kulm oin August 7, 1906.  For a short time, they worked for his
half-brother, John Hehr.  Then they bought a farm 8 miles east of Kulm, where
they lived until the fall of 1936.  Then they moved to Kulm.

Eleven children were born to this union.  Three died in infancy.  Those living
are: Harven, Fayette, Iowa; Alvin, Edgeley; Alden, Kulm; Mrs. Edwin Hehr
(Esther), Edgeley; Hugo, Leads, S. Dak.; Reuben; Mrs. Albert Hehr (Irene); and
Mrs. Harold Schuldheisz (Ruth), Kulm.

On April 12, 1944, Mrs. Hehr passed away.  In 1953, he married Maria (patzer)
Reule.

Mr. Hehr was a charter member of the Ev. Zion Lutheran Church.  He died July 21,
1959.

095.   HEHR, EDWARD & ANNA (Schuldheisz)

[picture]  Edward J. Hehr was born to Mr. and Mrs. John Hehr on July 31, 1898 in
Dickey County, where he grew to manhood.  On February 11, 1925, he married Anna
Schuldheisz, who was born on May 2, 1903 to Mr. and Mrs. Jacob Schuldheisz near
Kulm.

Mr. and Mrs. Hehr both grew up in the Kulm vicinity and after their marriage he
did farming except for 5 years when they operated a grocery store at Frazee,
Minn.

The Hehrs have three children and 11 grandchildren.  Their children are: Allen
Dale and Myron, who live in spokane, Wash., and Durant, living in Seattle, wash.

Ed and Anna are members of the St. Paul Lutheran Church.


096.   HEHR, JOHN

[picture]  John Hehr was born October 29, 1867 at Dennevitz, South Russia to
Mathaus and Susanna (Bohnert) Hehr.  In 1893, he married Maria Rossman and came
to America the same year.  They homesteaded in Dickey County and later moved to
a farm 3 1/2 miles southeast of Kulm.  In 1905, sparks from the Soo Line train
started a prairie fire which destroyed all the buildings on the family farm.
True pioneers, Mr. and Mrs. Hehr did not give up but rebuild again.  In 1919,
they moved to Kulm where they lived until 1927 when they again moved out to
their farm.

Mrs. Hehr died in December, 1931.

They were blessed with sixteen children.  The first four children died during
the diphtheria epidemic.  One died in infancy, John in 1919 and Emma in 1943.
Living are: Edward, Kulm; Mrs. Ida Dallmann, Aberdeen; Mrs. Louise Janke,
Wapato, Wash.; Emil, Minneapolis, Minn.; Mrs. Peter Klein (Bertha), Bismarck,
N.Dak.; Mrs. Jacob Ulrich (Anna), Ashley, N. Dak.; Albert, Kulm; Edwin, Edgeley,
N. Dak.; and Mrs. Deblert Washburn (Edna), Salem, Oregon.

Mr. Hehr married Margaret Stoeppler of Wishek, N. Dak. in 1932 and moved to the
city of Kulm in 1936.  He passed away December 13, 1950.  He was an active
member of St. Paul's Lutheran Church.


097.   HEHR, REUBEN & EMILY (Hoffer)

098.   HEHR, ROBERT & IDA (Krueger)

[picture]  Robert Hehr, son of George and Dorothea Hehr, was born north of
Fredonia, N. Dak. on December 4, 1894.  On March 10, 1921, he was united in
marriage to Ida Krueger, daughter of Jacob and Dorothea Krueger, also of
Fredonia.

Mr. Hehr was inducted into the army in May, 1918, during the first World War.
He served in England and France for 14 months.  After his return, he married and
moved on a farm 10 miles north of Fredonia.  The Hehrs farmed and endured many
hardships like all farmers during this time, but were determined never to
falter.  They enjoyed many blessings from the fruits of their labors and retired
in 1962 at which time they moved to Kulm.

The Hehrs have six children: Milbert, married and living in Port Angeles,

103.   HERMAN, GOTTLIEB &  ALMA (Janke)

[picture]  Gottlieb G. Herman was born March 31, 1906, six miles east and two

attended grade school in Young Township School No. I.

He started farming for himself in 1930, six miles east of Kulm, in Pomona View
Township in LaMoure County.

He was united in marriage to Alma Janke on November 15, 1936.  Alma Janke was
born February 18, 1917, on her parents' farm seven and a half miles southeast of

Janke.  She attended grade school in Young Township School No. IV.  Gottlieb and
Alma are at present living on their farm six miles east of Kulm, in Pomona View
Township in LaMoure County.

Besides farming, Mr. Herman has been active in Pomona View Farmers Union Local,
and was township treasurer for twenty years.  At present, he is supervisor for
Pomona View Township.  He is also a member of the Kulm Rod and Gun Club.
Gottlieb and Alma Herman are members of the First Congregational Church of Kulm.
Mrs. Herman is a member of the Dorcas Society and is in the mixed choir.

They have one daughter, Delphine, born June 14, 1938.  She graduated from Kulm

111.   HIEB, EDWIN & PATRICIA (Bolger)

They have two children: Beatrice 18, a graduate of Kulm High School and Steven
15, a freshman.


115.   HILDEBRAND, ELMER & YVONNE (Kinslow)

Elmer Hildebrand, son of Mrs. Emily Hildebrand and the late Adolph Hildebrand,
was born November 17, 1924.  On March 14, 1948, Elmer was married to Yvonne
Kinslow, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Clarence Kinslow.  To this union four children
were born: Nancy, Crysta Jean, Gloriann, and Mark.

Elmer has farmed all his life.  He is a director of the Kulm Farmers Union Oil
Co., a member of the Rod and Gun Club and of the Lions Club.  He has been a
director of the Dickey Crop Improvement Association.

Mrs. Hildebrand is a member of the Dorcas Society of the First Congregational
Church where they are members.  She is a teacher of boys and girls fellowship of
that church.  She is also known as "The Avon Lady".


116.   HILDEBRAND, FRED & LYDIA (Maier)

[picture]  Fred W. Hildebrand, son of Mr. and Mrs. Fred G. Hildebrand, was born
in Dickey County, North Dakota on December 8, 1894.  He married Lydia Maier,
daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Christian Maier, on April 4, 1916.  Mrs. Maier was born
in McIntosh County, November 26, 1892.  Mr. and Mrs. Hildebrand lived on their
farm 6 miles south of Kulm, in Dickey County, until 1953.  They built a new home
in Kulm, on south main street, after retiring from the farm.

Mr. Hildebrand was very active in community affairs, having served on the school
board of the Hollan School District for 34 years and the Township board for 27
years.  He was also on the Farmers Co-op Elevator board for 14 years and an ASC
committee man for 13 years.

Six children were born to Mr. and Mrs Hildebrand, one son died in infancy.  The
others are: Walter Lee, Garland, Texas; Clifford, Kulm; Gordon F., Lead, S.
Dak.; Mrs. Clifford (Mabel) Ruff, Tacoma, Wash.; Mrs. Arthur (Esther) Rempfer,
Lodi, Calif.

Mr. and Mrs. Hildebrand celebrated their Golden Wedding Anniversary a year ago
on April 4, 1966.  Their children were all present for this happy occasion.
They are members of the Kulm Zion EUB Church.


117.   HILDEBRAND, RICHARD & MATHILDA (Brauer)

[picture]  Richard Hildebrand, son of Frederick and Louisa Hildebrand, was born
September 17, 1907, in Dickey County, 10 miles south of Kulm.  He married
Mathilda Brauer, daughter of Christian and Marie Brauer, on November 7, 1929.

Mr. and Mrs. Hildebrand purchased a farm on the northwest edge of Kulm in 1930.
Here a son, Lloyd, farms in partnership with his father.  They have three other
children: Ardell, Kindred, N. Dak.; Mrs. Larry (Joan) Swick, Minneapolis, Minn.;
James, in the US Navy at Great Lakes, Ill.

Mr. Hildebrand has held various township offices and at present is township
supervisor.

The Hildebrands are members of the Evangelical Zion Lutheran Church, where Mr.
Hildebrand has served as president of the church board, secretary, and, at
present, is a trustee on the church council.


118.   HOGGARTH, CLEON & PHYLLIS (Fredrickson)

Cleon Hoggarth, son of Vern Hoggarth, was born January 5, 1939.  He was married
to Phyllis Frederickson, January 9, 1960, the daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Otto
Fredrickson of Kensal, N. Dak.  They have two sons: Curtis age 6, and Cory age
3.

Cleon attended the State School of Science at Wahpeton, N. Dak. and served six
years in the National Guard.  He has been Standard Oil Bulk Agent since 1960.
They purchased their home, formerly owned by Ohmer Grenz, in 1964.


119.   HOLLAN, ED & EMMA (Hiom)

Ed Hollan was born on March 4, 1896 at the old homestead south of Kulm where he
grew to manhood.  He is the son of Joe Hollan, one of the earliest pioneer
settlers of this community, having come here in 1883.

When World War I was declared on Germany, Ed was 21 years of age and he was
taken in the first draft.  His military induction was on September 21, 1917.
This first group of Dickey County boys were sent to Camp Dodge, Iowa.  Ed served
throughout the war with the 352nd Infantry Band in the U.S. and France.  During
part of the time, he was regimental Bugler.  He was discharged from the army on
June 14, 1919, at Camp Dodge, Iowa.

After his discharge, Ed went to Chicago where he studied cornet under H.A.
Vander Cook, a noted cornet and trumpet teacher of that time.

Ed Hollan married Emma Hiom, a teacher of the Braddock-Kintyre community.  Emma
is the daughter of Tom Hiom, who was one of Kulm's earliest pioneers.  Tom Hiom
served as marshal of Kulm, stagecoach driver and hotel keeper.

In the fall of 1920, Mr. and Mrs. Ed Hollan moved to Elkhorn, Wisc.  For four
years, Ed worked in the Holtan Band Instrument Factory.  During this time, he
played with the Holtan-Elkhorn Band and the Elkhorn American Legion Band.  In
1924, the Hollans moved to Milwaukee where they resided for one year.

In 1925, Ed and Emma returned to Kulm and started farming south of Kulm.

At this time, Ed was asked to re-organize the Kulm Band.  This band has been
under his direction ever since.  The Kulm Band has played for many celebrations
throughout the State of No. Dak.  They were given the distinction of being named
"The Senators Band", by Senators Wm. Langer and Milton Young, in 1925.  Ed also
directed bands at Edgeley and Merricourt.  For one school year, he directed the
Kulm School Band when they were unable to secure a teacher.  He served with the
N. D. American Legion Band for 16 years and played at times with the Jamestown
Elks Band.  In years past, many of the young people of this community received
their musical start by taking lessons from Ed.

Through the years, both Mr. and Mrs. Hollan have been active in community
affairs and have always been ready to help a neighbor in need.  Ed served for 30
years as assessor of Northwest Township and 20 years as treasurer of Hollan
School District.  Since 1942, he has served on the Whitestone Battlefield Board.
Emma is a charter member of the Northwest Thrifty Homemakers Club.  They belong
to the Assembly of God Church.

The Hollans have three children: Lorraine (Mrs. Gottlieb Schock) of Kulm; Edwin
L. (Bud) of rural Kulm; and Sylvia (Mrs. Irving Melroe) of Lisbon, N. Dak.  They
have six grandchildren.


120.   HOLLAN, LEE & ALICE (Fregien)

[picture]  Lee Robert Hollan (better known as Bibbie) was born on a farm four
miles south and one mile west of Kulm, September 8, 1916.  He is the son of the
late Robert J. and Clara Hollan (nee Grosz).  It was the farm which his parents
built up.  At the age of five, Lee's mother, Clara, passed away at the age of
29.  She was the first baby born in Kulm.

In the spring of 1923, Lee's father, Rob, married Sophia Klundt of Jamestown, N.
Dal. And Lee acquired a new mother.  Lee attended grade school in Hollan School
District.  Due to helping his father on the farm, Lee was unable to continue his
education at the time.  However, with the musical background of his father,
grandfather and uncle, Lee, too, became a lover of music.  With untiring
efforts, his father and his Uncle Ed Hollan instructed him in trumpet lessons.
Much of his leisure time was devoted to studying music and reading.  Having a
strong determination for education, he started high school in 1936 at the
Ellendale Normal and Industrial School and graduated in 1939 with some
additional hours in instrumental music and Industrial Arts.  On July 19, 1939,
Mr. Hollan was united in marriage with Alice Fregien, daughter of John and
Hermina Fregien (nee Netz).  They were married in the Gnadenfeld Congregational
Church, same church her parents were married in.

Lee and Alice have two children: Dennis Dwight and Curtis Lee.  Both are
graduates of the Kulm High School.  They attended Northern State College,
Aberdeen, where they received their Bachelor of Science Degree in Secondary
Education with a composite major in music, both vocal and instrumental.  They
are members of the Phi Mu Alpha Sinfonia, a national music fraternity.  Curtis
also attended NDSU, Fargo for a year and a half.

In their junior and senior year in high school, they attended the International
Music Camp at the International Peace Garden.  They have been active members of
the Farmers Union Junior program receiving their 5-year Torchbearer award, which
is the highest award made by the National Farmers Union.  They also were awarded
scholarships to the All-State Camp near Denver, Colorado, where they were
elected to serve on the National Junior Advisory Council representing the young
people of North Dakota at the National Convention which was held in Washington,
D.C. and New York City at that time.  In June, 1962, Curtis was on tour with the
NDSU Gold Star Band to the Seattle World's Fair to play for N. Dak. Day.  Dennis
is married to Patricia Hieb, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Arnold Hieb, Hosmer, S.
Dak.  Both Dennis and his wife are presently teaching in the Hosmer School;
Dennis, band and chorus instructor and Patricia, teaching 6th grade.

Curtis is the music instructor in the Boyd, Minnesota school system.

Mrs. Lee Hollan is a graduate of the Kulm High School and UND-Ellendale Center.
She has also taken some work at Northern State College, Aberdeen, So. Dak. and
extension work through NDSU, Fargo.  Having been an elementary teacher by
profession, Mrs. Hollan has taught many years in rural schools in McIntosh and
Dickey County; also in the Fredonia school as well as five years in the Kulm
School.  She has always had a deep concern about what happens in our school,
which she feels is a place to secure the talent and the interest to meet the
challenge that confronts us.  She helped organize the first PTA in Kulm; was
chairman of the Educational Committee of the Kulm Credit Union when it was first
organized in 1950; is a charter member of the Kulm Alumni Association; both she
and Lee are members of the UND-Ellendale Branch Alumni Association; has served
for many years as Education Director of Junior and Youth Activities for German
Northwest Local; past secretary of the Northwest Thrifty Homemakers Club and
past president of the Kulm Band Mothers.  Volunteer activities have been an
important part of her life.  At present she is McIntosh County Chairman of the
N. Dak. Heart Association and has been for the past three years.  She is also a
member of the Congregational Church Dorcas Society.

Mr. Hollan has also spent a full and active life in the community, having served
in several capacities in various organizations; member of the Congregational
Church Orchestra, member of the Congregational Church Men's Choir, served as
school clerk in Springfield School District for many years, president and
secretary of German Northwest Farmers Union Local, member of the Jamestown Elks
Band, secretary of the McIntosh County Democratic Organization, charter member
and past director of the Kulm Credit Union when it was first organized and at
present is a supervisor for the Roloff Township Board and has been for many
years.  At present, he is also a member of the Rod and Gun Club, member of the
Lions Club and a long-time member of the Kulm Concert Band.  Mr. and Mrs. Hollan
have always been interested in politics and government, but never harbored
political ambition on a county level.  They have devoted much time over the
years in helping to maintain a Democratic Organization in the county.  He has
been elected Democratic Precinct Committeeman for many years.  Lee and Alice
farmed with Lee's father for one year and then, in the fall of 1940, they
purchased the 160 acre farm on which they now live, located four miles south and
three miles west of Kulm., which formerly was owned by Rynold Flegel.

Through the years, the Hollans have increased their acreage by purchasing
additional land and acquiring some from her parents.  They also range a herd of
Hereford cattle.  For the past ten years, Lee has been affiliated with insurance
work.  As of last summer, Alice became a distributor of Holiday Magic Cosmetics
of San Rafael, Calif. And ,at the present, time has moved to Master Distributor,
building her own retail organization.

Lee and Alice, with the help of Dennis and Curtis, have put much time and effort
into improving their home and the grounds, an effort in which they have taken
profitable pride.

In July, 1964, the family of Bobbie and Alice (as everybody knows them best)
entertained approximately 250 guests at an out-door barbecue, celebrating their
25th wedding anniversary.

Mr. and Mrs. Hollan were members of the Gnadenfeld Congregational Church until
1953 and now are members of the Kulm Congregational Church.


121.   HOLMGREN, GUNNAR & HILDUR (Bjur)

[picture]  Hildur Bjur, daughter of Leander Bjur, was born on a farm north of
Kulm on September 24, 1892.  Her parents moved from Norsjo, Sweden to north of
Kulm in 1885.  On January 4, 1918, she married Gunnar Holmgren.  His parents
lived in Norsjo, Sweden.  The Holmgrens had five children: Erick (died when 1
day old), Ida (Mrs. Raymond Kinzler of Seattle, Wash.), Josephine (Mrs. Leo
Haughey of Paulsboro, New Jersey, died in 1955), Erick (married to Lorna Larson
and farming the home farm) and Wilbur (married to Shirley Lind, and living in
Seattle, Wash.).

The Holmgrens farmed north of Kulm until 1943, when Gunnar died.  Mrs. Holmgren
and the children remained on the farm until the children were grown.  She then
moved into Kulm and Erick and Wilbur managed the farm.


122.   IRION, OTTO & ANNA (Haag)

[picture]  Otto Irion, son of Mr. and Mrs. Jacob Irion, was born in LaMoure
County on September 27, 1898.  On March 30, 1908, Otto married Anna C. Haag,
daughter of Johann and Maria Haag of Fredonia.  To this union five children were
born: Florence (Mrs. Raymond Schlecht of Fredonia), Adeline (Mrs. Chester Dobler
of Kalispell, Mont.), EuGene (married and a career Navy man at Portsmouth, N.
Hamp.), Willard (married and assistant manager of Buttrey's at Kalispell,
Mont.), Gordon (married and works for Farmers Union Oil Co. of Kulm).

The Irions have farmed since 1927 and are presently living on what is known as
the Jacob H. Heller place.  Otto has been assessor of Janke Township for 22
years and also works as hail adjuster for the State Hail Dept.  The family are
members of Martin Luther Lutheran Church.


123.   ISAAK, ERNEST & MADELINE (Kayner)

[picture]  Ernest Isaak, son of Mr. and Mrs. Gottfried Isaak, was born February
4, 1903 on the homestead which his grandfather, Christian Isaak had filed on
when he came to America from South Russia.

On November 25, 1927, Mr. Isaak was united in marriage to Madeline Kayner of
Regina, Saskatchewan, Canada.  Mrs. Isaak is a graduate of the Ellendale Normal
and Industrial School and has taught school for 24 years, 3 of them in the Kulm
Public School.

Mr. Isaak was an interior painter, and also painted many elevators and churches
in the surrounding towns.  He also traveled and sold Stay-Polish and Golden
Poultry Tonic, both products compounded by himself.

In 1942, he bought his father's farm where he is engaged in grain farming and
raising of livestock.  He has had a large sign painted which reads, "Ernest
Isaaks Ranch, here we separate the rich from the poor."  Their many friends
enjoy their kind hospitality and are always welcome.

Mr. and Mrs. Isaak have one daughter, Sylvia.  She is the wife of Dr. F.M.
Raines, an optometrist in San Luis Obispo, California.  They have 2 sons, Money
and Craig.


124.   ISAAK, HARRY & OLGA (Strobel)

[picture]  Harry C. Isaak, son of Mr. and Mrs. N.G. Isaak, was born May 5, 1911,
ten miles southwest of Kulm.  He attended Kulm School and graduated from high
school in 1930, after which he attended the North Dakota State University for
four years.

On December 23, 1934, he married Olga Strobel of Kulm, daughter of Mr. and Mrs.
Fredrick Strobel.  They are the parents of three daughters: Marcene, Sonja and
Sheila.  Marcene is married to Mr. A.W. Puhlmann and they reside in Moorhead,
Minn.  They have one son, Todd, four years old and a daughter, Tonja, born April
8, 1967.  Sonja is a junior in Kulm High School and Sheila is in the seventh
grade.

In 1934, Mr. Isaak leased a service station, in Moorhead, Minn., from Mobil Oil
Co. which he operated until 1943.  In 1943, he moved to Kulm and engaged in
farming.  In 1956, he purchased the N.G. Isaak Elevator and established a seed
and custom seed cleaning plant and used the farming operations for seed
purposes.

Mr. and Mrs. Isaak have been life long members of the First Congregational
Church and have been active in church choirs and other various offices and
organizations for the past twenty-five years.  Mr. Isaak was church treasurer
and secretary for many years.  He is now an officer of the Eastern Association
of The United Church of Christ and also a director on the board of the Wishek
Congregational Retirement Home for the Aged.  He served on the Kulm School Board
and City Council.  He is a past president of Lions Club and was the building
chairman of the Kulm Health Center and served as a director on the board for
many years.


125.   ISAAK, NATHANIEL & CHRISTINA (Fregien)

[picture]  Mr. and Mrs. N.G. Isaak now have three granddaughters: Marcene, who
is married to A.W. Puhlman of Moorhead, Minn.; Sonja and Sheila Isaak, who are
at home.  They have two great grandchildren: Todd and Tonja Puhlmann.


126.   ISAAK, WILLIAM & ANNIE (Gackle)

[picture; refer to 1957 book]  Mr. and Mrs. Isaak are now retired.  The Isaaks
are members of Zion Lutheran Church.  Their two daughters, Dianna, Mrs. Jo
Cofell, is now living in Bismarck, has two sons; and Karen, Mrs. Wayne Lucht,
lives in New Rockford and has one daughter.


127.   JANS, ROLAND & MARION (Gackle)

Roland L. Jans, son of William and Olga Jans, was born April 16, 1919.  On July
25, 1943, he married Marion Gackle, daughter of Martin and Florence Gackle.  To
this union three children have been born: William Wesley, Rita Marie and Paul
Roland.

Roland was a clerk in a grocery and general merchandise store in Fredonia from
1933-1938, teller in Kulm State Bank from 1940-1942, at which time he entered
the Armed Service for four years, 2 1/2 of which were spent overseas in the
European theater.  He returned to the Kulm State Bank in October, 1945, at which
time he was made cashier and in 1958 was named a director.  Roland is a member
of the Vol. Fire Dept., American Legion, Elks and Eagles and the Kulm Lions
Club.

Mrs. Jans is a registered nurse and worked at the N.D.S.U. Health Center and the
Dakota Clinic in Fargo, prior to her marriage.


128.   JENNER, EDMUND & JULIA (Fedje)

[picture]  Edmund Jenner, youngest son of Mr. and Mrs. P.J. Jenner, was born
February 5, 1901 on the farm his parents homesteaded, where he has been farming
since 1921.  He was married to Julia Fedje, daughter of Jens Fedje, of Milnor,
N. Dak., September 3, 1928.  They have 2 daughters: Donna Mae Jenner, who is
teaching in Browning, Montana, and Geraldine Faye, who was married to Jarold
Slocumb on December 21, 1955, now living in Mountlake Terrace, Washington.
Their 2 children are Laura Jo and Nancy Lee.  Donna Mae is a graduate of Valley
City State Teachers College, and Geraldine attended college in Waverly, Iowa for
a year, and one year at Aberdeen Business College.

On May 8, 1922, the Jenner home was partly destroyed by a tornado, the barn and
other farm buildings were completely demolished.


129.   JENNER, P.

[picture]  Mr. and Mrs. P.J. Jenner homesteaded 4 miles south of Kulm in 1895.
They had 10 living children.  They are: Katie (Mrs. Christ Mogck), John, Christ,
Adolph, Jacob, Henry, Paul, Albert, Emma (Mrs. Bailey Davenport) and Edmund.
The ones living are John, Edmund and Paul.

They farmed for many years with the help of his 8 sons.  He owned 2 sections of
good farm land.  Their first home was a sod house, until they built their new
home, which is presently owned by their son, Edmund.  In 1918, they moved to
Kulm and lived in the house owned by John Stroehl.

His obituary stated he was a power of strength and always willing to give advice
and help where needed.


130.   JOHANSON, GOTTFRED

[picture]  In 1905, a family by the name of Gottfred Johanson, with their two
daughters, arrived in Kulm from Sweden.  After the fire that destroyed the
Gackle block, the west side of main street, Mr. Johanson built a 2-story
building where the Brost Bros. Store is at present.  Being a baker by trade, he
started a Bakery and Coffee Shop that served the town and community for 6 years.
In 1911, they sold their business to J.A. Peterson, who, after a year's time,
sold and moved to Spokane, Washington.  The Johanson family moved to Grass
Range, Montana, where they farmed until 1940, when they retired and moved to
Lewsitown, Montana.  Mr. Johanson passed away in 1962.  Mrs. Johanson is still
living and is 94 years of age.  Her daughter, Ebba Mackinzie, widowed with one
son, lives with her mother.  The other daughter lives in Washington.  She is
Mrs. Elsa Setzer.


131.   JOHNSON, STANLEY & HELEN (Anderson)

[picture]  Stanley Johnson, son of Mr. and Mrs. Ludvig Johnson, was born
December 6, 1918.  On November 29, 1941, Stanley married Helen Anderson,
daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Carl A. Anderson.  The Johnsons have farmed all their
married life and are presently living 9 miles northeast of Kulm.  They have four
children: Janet (Mrs. Don Erickson of Kulm), Dwight (married to Elaine Kramlich)
of Kulm, Bonnie and Donovan at home.

Mr. and Mrs. Johnson are members of the Assembly of God Church.


132.   JOHNSTONE, JAMES & JOYCE (Gackle)

Joyce Edith Gackle, daughter of Edith and Ernest Gackle, was married to James W.
Johnstone, August 15, 1948.  Mr. and Mrs. Johnstone are graduates of the North
Dakota State University.  Mr. Johnstone served 4 years in the army during World
War II.  He served 3 terms as Representative of Burleigh County.

They have 3 children: James Scott, born May 28, 1950; Paul William, born May 22,
1952; and Joyce Ann, who was born September 2, 1955.  Mr. Johnstone is working
for the Melroe Manufacturing Company as Director of Marketing.

They are living in Lisbon, N. Dak.


133.   KELLER, HENRY & AMELIA (Steinwand)

[picture]  Henry Keller was born on October 18, 1880 to Mr. and Mrs. John Keller
at Kloestitz, South Russia.

On December 23, 1907, Mr. Keller married Amelia Steinwand, daughter of Mr. and
Mrs. George Steinwand of Kloestitz, South Russia.  The Steinwand and Keller
family came to America in 1905 and settled in the Merricourt area, then later
settled on a farm south of Kulm.  In 1920, the Kellers moved to Kulm where he
worked as a laboring man, and then he operated a trucking service between
Bismarck and Kulm for many years.  Later in life, he trucked grain for farmers
until his death on April 30, 1949.  Mrs. Keller still resides in Kulm.

To this union five children were born: Erna, Elsie and Olga all deceased; Oscar,
Phillip, S.Dak.;  and Mrs. Ossie George, Kulm, N. Dak.


134.   KELLER, ROBERT

[picture]  Robert Keller, age 5, together with his father and mother, the late
David and Emelia Keller, came to this country in 1905 and farmed with his father
until he grew to manhood.  In 1922, Robert married Lydia Bader, daughter of the
late Daniel and Marie Bader of Kulm.  They bought the late Grandfather Keller's
farm and lived there until his retirement.

Three children were born to this union: Violet (Mrs. Joe Cruz of San Diego,
Calif.); Clifford R. of Bottineau, No. Dak.; and Arthur M. of Racine, Wisconsin.
Mrs. Keller passed away in August of 1937.

Besides farming, Mr. Keller served as director of the Pomona View school board
for 12 years and supervisor for Pomona View Township for 15 years.

In 1950, Robert married Katie Lautt Hieb of Fredonia.  They continued farming
until the fall of 1965, when, due to ill health, they sold the farm and built a
new home in Kulm.


135.   KINZLER, ALBERT & KATHRYN (Krueger)

[picture]  Mr. Albert Kinzler was born to Andreas and Regina Kinzler on January
1, 1901 in Norden Township, LaMoure County.  On February 8, 1923, he was married
to Kathryn Krueger, the daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Jacob Krueger in Logan County,
N.D.

The Kinzlers, who farmed northwest of Kulm, have three sons.  They are: Eugene
of Jamestown, Earl of Farmington, Michigan, and Paul, who resides at home with
his parents.  They are members of the Zion Lutheran Church at Kulm.


136.   KINZLER, CONRAD & LORETTA (Fregien)

[picture]  Conrad Kinzler, son of Christoph and Maria Kinzler, was born at
Fredonia, N. Dak. on June 4, 1941.  He married Loretta Violet Fregien, daughter
of Theobald and Christina Fregien of Kulm.

The Kinzlers began farming ten miles south of Kulm in 1941.  In 1942, a son,
Ronald was born to the couple and another son, Roger arrived in 1944.  The
Kinzlers sold their farm to Herbert Buerkley in 1956 and bought a farm 2 miles
west of Kulm.

The oldest son, Ronald, married Arva Dell Hehr, daughter of Mr. and Mrs.
Clarence Hehr, in April, 1966.  They took over the farm of their parents, who,
with Roger, moved to a new farmstead 2 1/2 miles west of Kulm.  Arva Dell is
employed at the Kulm Messenger.

The Kinzlers built the ranch on which they now reside in 1964 and 1965.  It is
known to many as the Wagon Wheel Ranch, or the little Peace Garden of N. Dak.
They spent many long hours planting trees and flowers, which adds to the
appearance of the ranch.

Mr. Kinzler and son, Roger have a nice herd of Black Angus cattle on the ranch.
The Kinzlers' hobby is keeping the ranch neat and attractive.  Roger is also
employed at Brost Bros. Auto and Implement.

The family are members of the Kulm Assembly of God Church.


137.   KINZLER, FRED & EMMA (Ruff)

[picture; This recent picture and data supplements the 1957 Jubilee Book]  The
Kinzlers have three sons, as pictured  From left to right is: Lawrence, a
carpenter, is married to Leona Moench and lives in Minneapolis; Wilbert, married
to Thelma Dallmann, lives on the family farm and has three children; Milton,
married to Marion Burdick, is teaching in Grand Forks and the couple have 2
girls.


138.   KINZLER, MARTIN & LYDIA (Rath)

[picture]  Martin Kinzler, son of the late Mr. and Mrs. Andreas Kinzler, was
born on a farm northwest of Kulm on September 3, 1899.  He was united in
marriage with Lydia Rath, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Andrew Rath, on July 18,
1923, at Kulm, N. Dak.

This union was blessed with four children: Luella, now Mrs. Clarence Ost of Jud,
N. Dak.; Lamar and Duane at home on the family farm.  A daughter, Darlene Jane,
passed away May 5, 1944.  The family farm was purchased in 1947 and is located 6
miles northwest of Kulm.  Mr. and Mrs. Kinzler have retired from farming, but
remain on the family farm and their sons operate the farm.

The Kinzlers have three grandchildren and are members of Zion Lutheran Church of
Kulm.


139.   KJOS, HAROLD & ADELLA (Kleingartner)

[picture]  Mr. Harold Kjos was born to Elling and Anna Kjos on March 24, 1922.

to Adella Kleingartner, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Christ Kleingartner of
Fredonia, N. Dak.

Mr. Kjos was president of the Kulm School Board from 1961 to 1964 and an active
member for seven years.

The Kjos' have three sons, all at home.  They are Timothy, Gary and Richard.


140.   KLEINGARTNER, WILLIAM & ELSIE (Riebhagen)

[picture]  William Kleingartner was born April 2, 1914 near Gackle, N. Dak.
After several years, his parents, Mr. and Mrs. Edward Kleingartner, moved near
Fredonia, where he grew to manhood.

He was united in marriage to Elsie Riebhagen of Fredonia on September 3, 1937.

He started farming six miles northwest of Fredonia.  In 1941, the Kleingartners
purchased a farm 7 1/2 miles northeast of Kulm and moved the following year.

William served on [the Pomona View] school board for a number of years, and is
supervisor of the township now.

They are members of the First Congregational Church of Kulm.  William served as
usher for a number of years.  Elsie is a member of the Dorcas Society, ladies
chorus and mixed choir.

They have four children: Mary Louise (Mrs. Donovan Fey) of Kulm; Kathleen (Mrs.
Kenneth Kinzler) of Detroit, Mich.; Larry, a senior of Jamestown College; and
Randa Jean, a 7th grader at the Kulm School.

145.   KOSANKE, ANDREW & ANNA (Mertz)

[picture of Gustav Grosz family]  Andrew Kosanke was born January 1, 1876 at

Friedenstal, South Russia.  On October 28, 1893, they were married in South

passed away are their son, Otto and daughter Lydia (Mrs. Alexander Schuldheisz)
who were residents of Kulm.  Surviving at present are their daughters: Mrs.
Pauline Grosz of Kulm; Emma, Mrs. Otto Winter of Goodrich, N. Dak.; Mrs. Annie

155.   LAND, ANTON & ANNA (Sodstad)

[picture]  Almost everyone living in the present age of space and rockets can
also tell about ancestors that were born on another continent in the 1800's.  It
would be no different with Anton and Anna Land, who passed away in recent years.

Anton Land was born at Drammen, Norway in 1879.  When he was only eight years
old, he went to live with an uncle who raised him to young manhood.  The dream
and adventure of traveling to America must have come forcefully to his heart and
mind as it did to many others during that time.  In 1908, he and a part of
others came to America, which at that time was fast becoming populated.  Many
had homesteaded some years before this.  Sometime in the early 1900's, Anton
purchased some land ten miles northwest of Kulm, which to this day remains the
"home" place.  A son, Helge, resides there now.

In 1888, in Christiana or Oslo, Norway, a fair baby girl named Anna was born to
a young couple whose last name was Sodstad.  This girl grew to young womanhood
and providence led her to America in 1913.  She worked around the Kulm vicinity
for some time.

Even in those days of limited mobility, young people did meet upon occasion;
perhaps with more excitement than in this day and age.  These meetings between
Anton and Anna developed overtones of romance and, in 1915, they were united in
marriage at Aberdeen, South Dakota.  Upon returning home, they came to Elling
Kjos' (who lived north of Kulm), to a nicely planned wedding supper.

Anna had a sister, who was married to Walter Garside, and they lived south of
Kulm on the Ted Wirch farm.  On rare occasions, Anna took the horse and buggy,
with the baby, and drove out there to visit.  She stayed overnight and returned
home the following day.

Three sons were born to Anton and Anna.  Their names are: Helge, Alvin and
Rangvald.  They all live north of Kulm.  The grandchildren include Arlyn, Gayle,
Charlotte, Sharon and Iris, children of Mr. and Mrs. Alvin Land; also Thomas,
Dean, Janine and Patti, children of Mr. and Mrs. Randy Land.


156.   LANGE, DARWIN & DOROTHY (Bader)

[picture]  Darwin Oscar Lange, son of Mr. and Mrs. Oscar Lange, was born May 24,
1923 in Kulm.  He married Dorothy Bader, daughter of Mrs. Mary Hiller, on June
19, 1948.

Darwin graduated from Kulm High School in 1941 and from Wahpeton Science School
in 1946.  He attended UND at Grand Forks and graduated from there in 1948.
Darwin served in the U.S. Army and served in the Panama Canal Zone.

Mr. and Mrs. Lange have three children: Sandra Jean, Linda Denise and Richard
Darwin.  The family resides in Stockton, Calif., where Mr. Lange is employed as
Chief Accountant for Naiagi Farms since 1960.


157.   LANGE, HAROLD & EDNA (Young)

[picture]  Harold Frederick Lange was born at Kulm on April 18, 1921 to Mr. and
Mrs. Oscar Lange.  He graduated from the Kulm High School in 1939 and attended

Bowling Green Ohio University.

159.   LARSON, ELMER & OLGA (Grundberg)

[picture]  Mrs. Elmer Larson (Olga Grundberg) was born in the Swede Township
community in 1898.  She moved with her parents to Canada in 1900, but came back
to the Kulm area in 1916, when she married Elmer Larson.  They farmed in Raney
Township until their retirement to Kulm in 1958.  Their children are Edith (Mrs.

160.   LEMKE, RUDOLPH & ANNA (Hehr)

[picture]  Rudolph Lemke was born on April 14, 1898, 16 miles south of Kulm,
N.D.  He is the son of Adam and Christine (nee Litke) Lemke.

This is where he grew to manhood.  As soon as he was of age, and farming was to
be his line, he thought there should be a better way than to depend on horse
power.  That is when he decided to buy himself a Fordson tractor, which seemed
some improvement over plowing with the old plugs.  But after a few years even
that was too slow going.  He was the first tractor farmer in the community.  In
1927, he bought a complete Case rig, consisting of tractor, plow and a threshing
machine.  With that threshing rig, he would do custom threshing for two months
and more.  In severe weather, cold and at times even snow.  Sometimes the crew
had to sleep in the hay stack or next to the chicken roosts with the roosters
crowing every few hours.  There was no alarm clock needed.  But the crew
survived.

On June 9, 1929, he married Miss Anna Hehr of Fredonia, N.D.  She was born
October 14, 1903, daughter of George and Dorothea (nee Bietz) Hehr.  To this
union was born one daughter, Audrey Ann, now Mrs. Harold Fregien of Kulm.

They built a new farm a half mile south of his parent's place.  Things were
pretty rough.  There was a terrific drought in progress which lasted for about
seven years.  So the new farm progressed very slowly.  Finding a good supply of
water was the big problem.  Many times, water was carried a half mile uphill
with a three gallon pail.  There was a natural spring there.  The cattle had to
be taken there for watering in the winter time.

August 9, 1945, the house was struck by lightning, during a flash thunderstorm,
and burned to the ground.  Some of the contents were saved.  A little granary
was their headquarters until winter threatened.  They then moved into the vacant
house on Lemke folks place.  But all the stock was left on the home place and
that meant walking back and forth all winter to do the chores and milking the
cows.

Misfortune stuck again on September 9, when three of the best milk cows died
from eating sweet corn, and the remaining cow had to be sold later.  So only
stock cows were kept.

Excavating for the basement started in October, 1945.  Material for a dwelling
was hard to get, being there was a war going on.  A permit had to be obtained
and, thru the Red Cross, building material for emergency use was made available
for the right price of course.  So, with the help of good friends and neighbors,
the house came into being.  After the basement was done and the frame was
enclosed, they moved in.  After 13 months, it was finally finished.

Diversified farming was their line until 1957, when they sold the live stock and
now do only grain farming.

They also have a scenic garden which is there to enjoy for anyone wishing to do
so.


161.   LINDBLOM, CARL & IDA (Offerdahl)

[picture]  Carl Lindblom, son of Anders and Margareta Holm Lindblom, was born in
Norden Township, LaMoure County, March 5, 1891.  He was married to Ida
Offerdahl, June 30, 1915, daughter of James Iverson Offerdahl and Anna Hereld
Offerdahl of Readstown, Wisconsin.  Both parents deceased.

Their children are: Carol Irene, Mrs. Walter Busche, Oakes, N. Dak.; Marjorie
Angela, Mrs. Robert Clapp, Adams, N. Dak.; and Patricia Ann, Mrs. Robert Butler
of Fargo, N. Dak.

Carl Lindblom spent his childhood years and grew to manhood, living with and
working with his parents on their farm in Norden Township.  Their first home was
a warm and comfortable sod house which was later replaced by a larger frame
building.  While still a young man, he became a member of the Swedish Baptist
Church.  After his parents' death, he continued to operate the farm until he was
elected Sheriff of LaMoure County in 1933.  He served as township clerk and
school clerk of Norden Township for 18 years; was county chairman for March of
Dimes for 4 years, headed the county set-up to raise funds for the Crippled
Children's Home and School at Jamestown, N.D.; was a member of the North Dakota
Peace Officers Ass'n, and served as deputy sheriff between terms as sheriff.  He
has completed 20 years of serviced when death claimed him on December 26, 1954
in their home in LaMoure, N. Dak.


162.   LINDBLOM, CONRAD & ELSA (Bjorquist)

[picture]  Conrad Lindblom, the son of Anders and Margareta Holm Lindblom, was
born in Norden Township, LaMoure County, November 16, 1888.  He married Elsa
Bjorquist, daughter of P.A. and Tilda Bjorquist, the parents living in Sweden at
that time, on June 23, 1915.  Their children are: Wesley Edmund, who is
Executive Secretary for the Columbia Baptist Conference in Seattle Wash.; Cecil
Mauritz, who is a pastor of the Baptist Church in Worcester, Mass.; Constance
Bertina, Mrs. Irwin Bjelland, who are missionaries in the Philippine Islands;
and Dorothy Miriam, Mrs. John Stefanoff, Great Falls, Montana.  Mr. Stefanoff is
a teacher in the Great Falls Schools.

The Conrad Lindblom family operated their farm in Swede Township, LaMoure County
until 1940, when they moved to a farm near Elk River, Minn., where they lived
for 2 years.  They moved to St. Paul, Minn., where Mr. Lindblom was employed as
a guard at the First National Bank of St. Paul, until they moved to Seattle,
Wash., where they invested in a new home and are now living.  Mr. Lindblom is in
ill health and, at present, is confined to a Nursing Home in Seattle, Wash.


163.   LINDBLOM, GUST & TILDA

[picture]  Gust Lindblom, son of Anders and Margareta Holm Lindblom, was born in
Norsjo, Sweden, June 18, 1879, and came to America with his parents in 1887.
His wife's name was Tilda and her parents lived in Sweden.  A son died in
infancy and a daughter died at the age of 9.  A daughter, Naomi, Mrs. Roy D.
Fromm lives in Yakima Wash.  A daughter, Myrtle, Mrs. Harold Binks, lives in
Green Valley, Arizona.

Mr. Lindblom spent his early years with his parents, on his father's homestead,
in Norden Township.  In the pioneer days, they lived in a small sod house.  When
he was older, he procured a farm for himself in Pomona View Township, where he
lived until his death.

He was an active member of the Swedish Baptist Church, where he served as deacon
for many years.  After his demise, his widow and daughters moved to Yakima,
Wash., where Mrs. Lindblom passed away in 1962.

166.   LINDGREN, LARS & KAREN (Bjur)

[picture]  Lars Konrad Lindgren was born in Sweden and came to Kulm and made his

was a daughter of Anton Bjur.  To this union 5 children were born: Emmy, Lillie,
Mabel, Clarence and Evan.  The 2 surviving children are Mabel (Mrs. LeRoy
Dellinger), and Evan Lindgren, both living in Yakima, Washington.  L.K., as he

and the first gasoline tractor in this community.  Mr. and Mrs. Lindgren lived
on a farm north of Kulm until 1911, when they moved to Yakima.


167.   LINDGREN, MILTON & ERMA (Lundgren)

[picture]  Milton Lindgren was born to Mr. and Mrs. Carl Lindgren of Yakima,

Erma Lundgren, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Signar Lundgren of Kulm, No. Dak., after
which they bought the Ostrom farm and lived there for a number of years.  Later,
they sold the farm and moved to Kulm, where Milton was employed at Brost

Center and locker plant for about three years before moving to Yakima, Wash.,

Department and Erma works for a warehouse packing plant as a part time job.

The Lindgrens, who are active members of the Midwest Baptist Church, have three
children: Harvey, a freshman in high school; Marlin, in the eighth grade and
Colleen, in the sixth grade.


168.   LINDGREN, ROSE

[picture]  Rose Marie Lindgren, daughter of Margaret and Nels C. Lindgren was
born in Swede Township, north of Kulm, January 4, 1916.  Rose Marie attended the
country school through the 8th grade and graduated from high school at the Good
Samaritan School in Fargo.  While attending the school, which is now the
Crippled Children's School at Jamestown, North Dakota, she was a member of an
extension group of handicapped girls who traveled in the interest of the school,
presenting programs of singing and speaking.  She also attended the Fargo
Business College, and also received credits from the University of North Dakota
at Grand Forks, through the rehabilitation program for news writing.  From her
early childhood, she had Muscular Dystrophy, which in later years caused her to
become more inactive.  In spite of her handicap, she led an active life, serving
as clerk on the Swede School District for 14 years.  She embroidered, crocheted,
and painted beautiful work which she sold.  She taught Sunday School for over 25
years, rarely missing a Sunday.  She had a lovely soprano voice, singing for
many public services.  Her patience, her smiling face, and her willingness to
comfort and help others, made her a blessing to many.  Rose Marie passed away
December 23, 1964.


169.   LINDGREN, VERNON & ESTHER (Lundgren)

[picture]  Vernon Lindgren is the son of Mr. and Mrs. Peter Lindgren of Kulm.
During World War II, he spent three years in the service, most of this time was
spent in North Africa and Italy.  After his return, Mr. Lindgren went into
farming and, on June 23, 1950, he was married to Esther Lundgren, daughter of
Mr. and Mrs. Signar Lundgren of Kulm.  In 1950, the Lindgrens moved to the

Mrs. Lindgren is in the teaching profession and at present is teaching the third

They have one son, Mark Signar, a freshman in the local school.


170.   LUCK, HAROLD & LORRAINE (Mueller)

[picture]  Mr. Harold Luck, son of Mrs. Albert Luck, Jamestown, N. Dak., was
born at Dickey, N. Dak. on October 7, 1918.  He was graduated from high school
at Adrian, N. Dak.

He was engaged in farming with his father until 1948, when he became employed
with the Salzer Lumber Co. at LaMoure, N. Dak.

In 1951, he came to Kulm as manager of the Salzer Lumber Co.

He was married to Lorraine Mueller, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Edwin Mueller, on
December 12, 1953 at the Zion Lutheran Church in Kulm.  They have four children:
Mary, Nancy, Lori and Randy.

They reside in their present remodeled home one block east of the old post
office, which was originally the first school house and later the Reformed
Church of Kulm.

In 1965, they purchased the Salzer Lumber Co. Yard and changed the name to the
present Kulm Lumber Co.

Harold is active in many community activities: Lion's Club, Rod and Gun Club,
member of the Kulm Health Center, Fireman, city alderman for 11 years, and also
has devoted much time to baseball and basketball.  Harold has served as
president of the Lions, Rod and Gun, Health Center Board, and the City Council.


171.   LUNDGREN, EDWIN & AUDREY (Norden)

[picture]  Mr. Edwin Lundgren was born in Swede Township on March 23, 1913 to
Mr. and Mrs. Signar Lundgren.  June 22, 1951, he was married to Audrey June
Norden, daughter of Oscar and Hannah Norden, Minnedosa, Manitoba, Canada.

Mr. Lundgren has been farming in the present location since 1928.  Edwin and
June are members of the Kulm Baptist Church, where he is chairman of the Trustee
Board, usher and other capacities, a member of the Kulm School Board, on the
board of directors of the Farmers Union Oil Company, Kulm Credit Union and
Lions.  June has served as organist and pianist at the Baptist Church for 16
years and also served other committees of the church, was PTA president for two
years, member of the Kulm Homemakers and is also a private music instructor,
having served in this capacity for 18 years.

The Lundgrens have four children: Brent, Gloria, Blaine and Glenda, all at home.

Mrs. Lundgren notes with interest that her father, Oscar Norden (now deceased)
was a former Kulmite, having immigrated here with his parents, Mr. and Mrs. Carl
Norden and other members of his family from Sweden around 1884.  They lived on
the farm where the Alvin Lands now live.  Around 1889, the Carl Norden family
moved to Newdale, Manitoba, Canada.


172.   LUNDGREN, ROBERT & MARION (Ostrom)

[picture]  Robert Lundgren, son of Mr. and Mrs. Signar Lundgren, was born at
Parshall, No. Dak. on October 5, 1915.  On October 19, 1939, Robert married
Marion Ostrom, daughter of John Ostrom of Sprague, Manitoba.  To this union five
children were born: James (works at Kulm Lumber Co. and drives the school bus),
Wayne (married and works on Edwin Lundgren's farm), John, Mary Lou and Sherwood
at home.

The Lundgrens have farmed all their married life and, since 1944, have lived 4
miles north of Kulm.  They are members of the Kulm Baptist Church.


173.   LUNDGREN, SIGNAR & JENNIE (Lundstrom)

[picture; refer to 1957 book]  In the fall of 1959, Mr. and Mrs. Signar Lundgren
had a new home built in the city of Kulm and, New Year's Day of 1960, they, with
their daughter Evelyn, moved into it.  Although Signar is semi-retired, he
considers it his hobby to go out on the home farm, which his son Edwin now
farms, and drive tractor during the Spring and Summer months.

The Lundgrens have traveled extensively these past years.  In October of 1966,
Mr. and Mrs. Lundgren and Evelyn, together with Signar's brother Henning, his
wife and daughter Britt, made a "flying trip" to Sweden, Signar's homeland.  It
was a first trip, however, for his wife and daughter.

As was mentioned in the 1957 Jubilee Book, Mrs. Lundgren (Jennie) was the first
baby girl born in Swede Township.  She was born May 12, 1887.

The Lundgrens have been blessed with nine children, twenty-three grandchildren
and give great grandchildren.  They will celebrate their 55th Wedding
Anniversary, July 1, 1967.

They are active members of the Kulm Baptist Church.


174.   MALM, DENVY & LENORA (Larson)

177.   MALM, GUST & ALMA (Holmquist)

[picture]  Mrs. Gust Malm (Alma Holmquist) was born in 1888 in northern Sweden.
She came to America in 1907 and was united in marriage to Gust Malm at LaMoure,
North Dakota in 1910.  They may have been the first couple to make the trip to
the LaMoure County Courthouse by automobile.  They farmed in Swede Township
until 1948, when they built a home in Kulm.  The Malms had five children: Julia
(Mrs. LeRoy Davidson), Edwin, Elmo, Denvy, and Lewis.  Edwin, Elmo, and Denvy
farm in this area.  Lewis is employed in Minot.  Julia was a resident of this
community also until her death in 1965.  Mr. Malm passed away in 1960.


178.   MALM, VERNON & ELLEN (Davidson)

179.   MAYER, JUSTINE

[picture]  Justine Flegel, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Christian Flegel, was born
July 22, 1882, in Kulm, South Russia.  Came to this country with her parents in
1892.  Before Kulm was started, railroad was extended to Kulm that fall, nearest
town was Edgeley.  She remembers many hardships as most pioneers.

The first winters, the family of 7 lived in a one room clay house.  The next
year they built a bigger house of clay and rock.  Hard work, but a big
improvement.  She also helped break sod with oxen.

School wasn't what it is now, the teach would stay at a farm home and teach the
children for about two or three weeks and then go to the next place and so on.

When the Flegel family came to Ellendale, they were met by John Sperling, who
took them to his home, twelve miles southwest of where Kulm is now.

She later married this John Sperling's son, Dave, November 14, 1903.  Dave was
born October 24, 1877 in Mannsburg, South Russia, came to the U.S. with his
parents in 1886, to Scotland, South Dakota, stayed there until 1888, when they
moved to North Dakota and settled 12 miles southwest of what is now Kulm.

The first years of their married life was spent on a homestead 12 miles south of
Gackle.  Then they moved back to Kulm where Dave was dray man for many years,
later he was City Police.

This union was blessed with four children.  Three died in infancy.  Otto H. is
living in Jamestown; Dave Sperling passed away August 18, 1931.

Mrs. Sperling later married Gustav Lechner of Parkston, South Dakota.  After his
death, she married John Mayer in 1943 and now resides in American Falls, Idaho.


180.   MILLER, HENRY & ESTHER (Nill)

[picture]  Henry Miller was born to John H. and Christina (Eslinger) Miller on
January 12, 1908 at Carson, No. Dak.  At the age of 4 years, he and his parents
moved to a farm 14 miles south of Kulm.  On June 12, 1932, he was married to
Esther Nill, daughter of George and Karolina (Lemke) Nill of Merricourt, No.
Dak.

Mr. Miller has farmed all his life and moved to the present location, 13 miles
south of Kulm, in 1932.

The Millers have three children.  They are: Marjorie, Mrs. Lionel Flegel, who
lives 3 miles northwest of Kulm and has three children; Ervin, who is married to
Arlene Weist.  They have one son and live on the farm with his parents; and
Duane Miller, who is attending NDSU and is majoring in chemistry.

Mr. Miller is serving his third term as Dickey County Commissioner from the
fourth district, is a member of the Whitestone Park Board and a director of the
Farmers Co-op Elevator, was a school clerk for 26 years in the German School
District, 4-H leader for five years and church treasurer of the Hoffnungsfeld
church for a number of years.


181.   MILLER, JOHN & CHRISTINA (Eslinger)

Mr. and Mrs. Miller lived to celebrate their Golden Wedding Anniversary.  Many
friends attended the open house and program prepared by their children to honor
them on this memorable occasion.  Another rare event occurred on this day when a
great-granddaughter, Crystal Flegel, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Lyonel (Marjorie
Miller) Flegel was born, bringing the total members in the family circle to 50
on their 50th Wedding Anniversary.

Mrs. Miller passed away on January 25, 1960 at the age of 73 and Mr. Miller
passed away on March 29, 1963 at the age of 81.

190.   NELSON, ALBERT & ADDIE (Hughes)

[picture]  Albert was born at Canistota, S. Dak., to Fred Nelson and Ida
(Johnson) Nelson.  Both natives of Sweden.  He moved with his parents to a
homestead 6 miles north of Lehr in 1905, where he grew to manhood.  He attended
the local schools and the Iowa State Automobile and Tractor School at Sioux
City.  Mr. Nelson was always interested in radio and t.v. work and studied all
available material.  He, with Oscar Krause and Harold Nagel, constructed one of
the first crystal sets in existence in these parts.

He was married to Addie Hughes at Brazil, Indiana, December 24, 1922.  Addie was
the daughter of Charles Hughes and Cora (Hancock) Hughes.  She was born at
Reelsville, Indiana, attended elementary and high school at Brazil, Indiana and
got her higher education at Indiana State College at Terre Haute, Indiana.  She
came to North Dakota in 1918 to teach school.  She taught at Ashley, Lehr, and
Fredonia for various lengths of time.  The Nelsons farmed on the home place,
north of Lehr, for twenty years.  Owing to drought and crop failures, they were
forced to give up farming in 1941.  They moved to Burnstad and had a general
store for four years.  Mr. Nelson repaired radios on the side.  He was invited
by the Lion's Club to come to Kulm and start a permanent repair shop, which he
did in 1945.  Addie has worked in both restaurants, for both Krein, Dell Palm
and as receptionist for Dr. J.E. Grenz.  Albert has 4 brothers and 2 sisters,
and Addie has 3 sister.  The Nelsons have no children.
