193.   NETZ, JOHN & ELSIE (Schott)

[picture at end of family histories]  Among the native born of pioneer parents
of Kulm are Mr. and Mrs. J.D. Netz.  Mr. Netz is a son of the late Mr. and Mrs.
Daniel Netz and was born, in a sod home on the farm his parents homesteaded 12
miles southwest of Kulm, on November 11, 1900.  Mrs. Netz is a daughter of the
late J.P. Schott and was born in their farm home 4 miles west of Kulm.

Mr. Netz (known as Johnnie) received his elementary training in Roloff Township,
attended high school in Kulm, and while attending high school, he worked for his
room and board in the Kulm Hotel and rode the twelve miles to his home on
weekends by bicycle.  After teaching school for several years in the Springfield
School District, he attended the Bismarck Business College in Bismarck.

He began working for various business places in Cleveland, Fredonia, and Kulm,
and operated a filling station in Lehr until 1930, when he entered into the
farming business.

On October 25, 1931, he was united in holy wedlock to Miss Elsie L. Schott, and
they farmed two miles west of Kulm until the fall of 1938, when they auctioned
off their livestock and farm machinery, and moved into Kulm, where Mr. Netz was
instructor of the Adult Education Classes in Citizenship, literacy, and first
aid.  These classes were held in the evening in the 8th grade room in the Kulm
school house, with an average attendance of 26.

In 1942, the Netz' bought some farming land 4 miles west of Kulm, and grain
farmed thru 1962, when they quit the farming business, and since 1963 Mr. Netz
has been employed as Bookkeeper by Carlson and Sjostrom Implement.  For ten
years, Johnnie has served as City Assessor of Kulm, and, since 1952, has been on
the Supervisor Committee of the Kulm Credit Union.  Mrs. Netz is employed in
Palms Dept. Store since 1951.

The Netz' have been life long members of the Congregational Church, in which
both were baptized, confirmed, married and converted.

Mrs. Netz enjoys being a member of the Dorcas Society, on the Sunday School
teachers staff since 1952, and working with the Boys and Girls Fellowship and
Children's Chapel.  She has served in various offices in these organizations,
and works industriously in the promotion of Home and Foreign Mission Work.

Mr. Netz served as Church Secretary for ten years and was a member of the
Finance Committee for a number of years, and at present is a member of the
Church's Auditing Committee.


194.   NETZ, THEODORE & KATIE (Sandau)

[picture]  Theodore Netz, son of Mr. and Mrs. Daniel Netz, was born on a farm
southwest of Kulm on August 23, 1891.

On December 12, 1917, Ted married Katie Sandau, daughter of Mr. and Mrs.
Gottlieb Sandau and born October 26, 1896.  To this union eleven children were
born: Violet (Cheverly, Md.), Irene (Hawley, Minn.), Kermit (Twin Falls, Idaho),
Vangeline (Tacoma, Wash.), Willard (Needles, Calif.), Sylvia (Renton, Wash.),
Lloyd (Buhl, Idaho), Daniel (Lead, S. Dak.), Theodore (Buhl, Idaho), Elroy (died
in 1958) and Iver (Minneapolis, Minn.).

The Netz' farmed southwest of Kulm from the time they were married until they
retired in 1952 and moved into Kulm.


195.   NICHOLS, LOUIS

Louis Nichols came to the Kulm community the fall of 1905 as a thresher hand and
tended separator for August Hartman.  He was known to most everyone as "Louie".
He was also a good engineer.  He was born of Irish parents, a native of Sparta.
He had worked in a gold mine.  He was a little mite of a man, a scant five foot
four but woe to anyone who might cross his path or step on his toes.  They
wouldn't do it again.  He enjoyed parties and dances and his ever ready Irish
wit won him a host of friends.

That winter he worked for Joe Hollan, bringing with him a team of horses.  Later
that winter, he bought another horse from Simon Hille, and one from Joe Hollan.
By early year standards, that was power enough to start farming.

That spring he moved in with the August Hartmans, he and August sharing
machinery, work and expenses.  He rented some land from Joe Hollan and some from
August Hartman.  After seeding was done, the boys bought an old Case steam
engine and a Minneapolis separator from the Lehr community.  Between then and
threshing time, the boys overhauled and carefully checked over the two threshing
rigs so, when threshing was ready, August took one outfit and Louie the other.
Each thresher had a long run, up to snow fall.  After the threshing was over,
Louie took up a homestead a mile or two north of the Whitestone Battlefield, and
after living on it the required length of time, proved up as the saying went,
but he threshed each fall, and after proving his right to the homestead, he
bought the Christ Sept farm where widow Emma Klettke and her sons live.
However, after a year or two, he rented out this farm and moved to his homestead
again.  In the meantime, he married and established a permanent home there.
When his children became of school age, having no school nearby, he moved to
Kulm where he opened up a small garage and machine shop, as he was a master
mechanic.  After several years, he moved to Edgeley, into larger quarters, and
continued in that line of work until his passing during the forties sometime.

His son George continued in the business for a number of years before leaving
Edgeley and moving to Carrington.

Louis loved life.  He didn't amass a fortune, but he left behind a host of
friends and fond memories which, in the end, means more than all the King's
gold.


196.   NILL, ROY & DOROTHY (Gackle)

197.   NILSEN, OLAF & BERTHA (Davidson)

[picture]  Olaf Nilsen, son of Andrew and Kristi Nilsen, was born in Hona Fas,

The Nilsens farmed for a period of time north of Kulm, then they moved to

station and restaurant.

When the Nilsens retired in 1962, the Zinkens took over the operation of the
filling station and restaurant.

passed away in 1966.


200.   O'BRIEN, JACK & DAN COLLINS

[picture]  Jack O'Brien, with his partner Dan Collins, came to the Kulm
community about the year of 1905.  They were Irish bachelors who came in from
Iowa, and true to tradition were likeable fellows who played an important role
in community life here in those pioneer times.  They farmed north of Kulm the
first year and then purchased a half section of raw prairie land 6 1/2 miles
south of Kulm, the south half of Section 27 in Northwest Township.  Here they
built up a temporary camp about one mile south of [the] Joe Hartman farm, where
Lee Hartman now lives.  In those years, farming was all done with horses, so the
boys started breaking up prairie with two breaking fallows.  They bought the
seed flax from Joe Hollan.  They raised a good crop of flax that year and moved
back again, north of Kulm, for winter quarters.

They returned in the spring and continued to break up the prairie.  That fall
they built up about 3 1/4 miles west of their camp.

They built a comfortable two room house and a barn for their horses, put down a
well and established permanent residence on the east side of the road, which is
now Highway 56.

The boys were musicians and played for practically all of the country dances
around the Kulm community, Jack playing the violin and Dan the mandolin.  Jack
was a very good violinist and throughout those years played with various
orchestras in Kulm.  He also played on the Kulm baseball team, and took part in
that never to be forgotten game between Kulm and Wishek, when Kulm won the
$1000.00 bet by a score of 4 to 2.

In the fall of 1909, the boys sold their farm to Fred Hildebrand Sr.  After the
threshing that fall, Jack and Dan decided to pay a visit to Iowa for the most
part of the winter.  Jack's brother Pat, taking care of the horses and keeping
house.  That spring 1910, the boys held an auction sale and sold their horses,
machinery and also household.

A new tract of land was being opened up in Alberta, Canada, so they decided to
look it over, the result being they homesteaded near Casper and started over
again in true pioneer fashion.  Jack continued playing for dances and played on
the fast Casper baseball team.

After a few years, Dan Collins sold his interest and returned to the States,
locating in Bothell, Wash., a suburb of Seattle.

Jack continued farming on his own until age forced his retirement.  The last
heard of him, he was in a Nursing Home in Casper.


201.   OBERLANDER, LEROY & WINONA (Widmer)

[picture]  LeRoy L. Oberlander, son of Mr. and Mrs. Louis Oberlander, was born
July 9, 1928 at Kulm, N. Dak.  He was married to Winona Widmer, daughter of
George Widmer, Fredonia, N. Dak.  They have 2 daughters: Mavis Jean age 7, and
MarCene Jane age 4.

LeRoy Oberlander served 2 years in the United States Army, in the Infantry.  He
received his basic training in Fort Ord, California and spent 14 months in
Japan.  After his service in the army, he returned to Kulm and has been farming
the past years, since 1955, on the Oberlander Homestead, which is 6 1/2 miles
southwest of Kulm.


202.   OGREN, GIDEON

[picture]  Mr. Gideon Ogren was born to Mr. and Mrs. Gottfred Ogren (nee Arizona
Malm) on May 17, 1906.

Gideon now farms the family homestead, 10 miles north of Kulm.  He has one
sister Alvina, Mrs. Clark Harris, who lives in Seattle, Washington.

Katherine Ericka Davidson, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Peter August Davidson of
Kulm.  To this union four children were born: Merritt of Edgeley; doris (Mrs.
Robert West), Torrance, Calif.; LaVerne (Mrs. Floyd Fredenburg), Edgeley; and
Orville of Kulm.

They also have nine grandchildren and five great grandchildren.

[picture with individual identification; refer to 1957 book]  Nels, Kulm;

205.   OGREN, VICTOR

[picture]  Victor Ogren was born to John and Tilda (Malm) Ogren, December 11,
1901, on his father's homestead, where he now lives, with the exception of a
year and half, when the family lived in British Columbia, Canada.

Among the many duties he has performed, he has served as president of the Swede
Farmers Union Local, when it was organized in 1928, and has served on the Board
of Directors of the Farmers Union in Edgeley, and on the Board of Directors of
the Farmers Co-op Elevator at Kulm for 22 years, serving as president from 1947
to 1951.  He is an active member of the Swede Telephone Co. since reconstruction
in 1953 and is a board member at the present time.

With his brothers Malkus, Nels, and Luther, they were the last ones in this
community to operate a Steam Threshing Rig and Gas Rig, until the Combines were
used, which made less work and fun threshing.


206.   OST, BEN & EMMA (Ruff)

[picture]  Ben Ost was born at Bessarabia, South Russia, November 22, 1894, the
son of Mr. and Mrs. Henry Ost, Sr.  He came with his parents to Logan County,
near Fredonia, on November 27, 1897.

Mr. Ost served in World War I and served overseas.

On November 20, 1919, Ben Ost was married to Emma Ruff and settled on a farm
near Fredonia.  Then, in 1926, they bought a farm near Guyson, which was a very
small town between Fredonia and Lehr.

Emma Ruff was born at Bessarabia, South Russia, April 2, 1901, the daughter of
Mr. and Mrs. Fred Ruff.  She came to the United States, May 30, 1902, and they
settled on a farm northwest of Fredonia, in Logan County.

In the fall of 1950, Mr. and Mrs. Ost retired from farming and moved to Kulm, N.
Dak.

Following are the names of the children:  Edmund F., who is married to Viola C.
Dallman.  They live at Fredonia, where Edmund is postmaster and Viola is a
postal clerk.  Edna L. is married to Ben. Koenig, who is a barber and lives at
Kulm, N. Dak.  Oscar H. is married to Viola M. Roloff and they farm west of
Fredonia, which is the home place of his parents.  Their address is Fredonia, N.
Dak.  Elva D. is married to Wilbert Gutschmidt and they farm north of Fredonia
on his home place and their address is Gackle, N. Dak.  There are eight
grandchildren.


207.   OTTMAR, CLINTON & GRACE (Gackle)

Clinton Ottmar was born November 17, 1928 to Mr. and Mrs. John Ottmar of Wishek.
On December 25, 1951, he was married to Grace Gackle, daughter of Mr. and Mrs.
George Gackle of Kulm.

Both Mr. and Mrs. Ottmar are graduates of the University of North Dakota.

Grace taught school for 2 years and Clint served in the service during the
Korean conflict.  They have lived in Jamestown since 1955, where he is an
attorney and also State's Attorney of Stutsman County.  They have four children.
They are: Timothy, Mark, Thomas and Gail.


208.   PAHL, WILLIAM & Ottilia (Bader)

[picture]  Born in Russia, December 18, 1895, son of August Pahl, the Pahls
arrived in the USA in May of 1899.  Mr. August Pahl homesteaded in McIntosh
County, where they made their home until 1907, when they sold the property and
moved to Dickey County and bought a farm in Grand Valley Township.  In 1920,
they moved to Forbes, N. Dak. and William took over the farm.

In 1919, Mr. Pahl was married to Ottilia Bader, at Ellendale, N. Dak.  Ten
children were born to this union: Harold Pahl, Griffith, Ind.; Elsie, Mrs. A.H.
Gebhardt, Clarkston, Wash.; Otto Pahl, Forbes, N. Dak.; Lenora, Mrs. R.V. Dane,
Wayzata, Minn.; Lloyd Pahl, Mound, Minn.; Walter Pahl, Maple Plain, Minn.; Loren
Pahl, Excelsior, Minn.; Beulah, Mrs. B. Fabor, Canyonville, Ore.; Pearl, Mrs.
Milden Krieger, Forbes, N. Dak.; Dorothy, Mrs. Allen Solhiem, Reynolds, N. Dak.

Mr. Pahl farmed on his father's farm until 1932, when he bought the Gottfried
Bader farm in Spring Valley, continued farming through good and bad till 1958.

He was a member of the school board for 15 years and a member of the township
board for 12 years.

In 1958, Mr. Pahl sold the farm to his son, Otto, and purchased a home in Kulm,
were they live.  The Pahls became members of the Evangelical Church in Kulm in
1948.

Four of the children graduated from Kulm High School.


209.   PALM, DELL & ANNETTA (Smith)

[picture]  Dell L. Palm, the son of Henry and Selma Palm of Lehr, N. Dak., was
born April 9, 1912.  Dell's father was the first barber in Kulm.  He married
Selma Stehr and later they moved to Lehr, where they homesteaded.

Dell is the second youngest of five children.

On November 29, 1935, he and Annetta Smith, daughter of Daniel and Rosina Smith,
were married.  For a while, they lived in Minnesota and Wisconsin before coming
back to take over the People's Store with Mr. and Mrs. A.J. Smith.  After the
fire of the store in 1948, they became owners of Palm's Department Store.

The father of Nettie was one of the first mailmen to deliver mail by horse and
buggy to a town southeast of Kulm called Helwig, where the farmers went to pick
up their mail.

The Palms have one daughter, Sharon Lee Holden, who presently lives in Fargo and
teaches in Moorhead, Minn.


210.   PATZER, DANIEL & JOHANNA (Waszner)

[picture]  Daniel Patzer, son of Christina and Daniel Patzer, was born in
McIntosh County, southwest of Kulm, on May 22, 1894.  Johanna Waszner Patzer was
born October 19, 1898 near Ashley.  Daughter of Jacob and Anna Maria Waszner.
Johanna and Daniel were married in the Lutheran Church in Ashley on October 26,
1916, by Rev. Lachmer.  They farmed on his father's farm.  After selling the
farm, they moved to Kulm.  They had three children: a son Herbert and daughter
Edna, who died in infancy, and a daughter, Alma (Mrs. Herbert Beich), who
resides in Kulm.  They have two grandchildren: Larry Beich of Murfreesboro,
Tennessee and Melody Beich, at present attending college.  Johanna and Daniel
are active members of the Congregational Church.  On October 26, 1966, they
celebrated their 50th Wedding Anniversary.


211.   PETTERSON, AUGUST

[picture]  August Petterson came from Sweden with his parents and settled near
Forman, North Dakota, where he grew to manhood, married and owned a butcher
shop.  In 1904, he came to Kulm and operated a butcher shop which was located on
the corner where the Kulm State Bank now stands.  He encouraged his friend
Elling Kjos, from Forman, to come to Kulm, which he did, working on the dray
line and assisted in the butcher shop.  Later, he sold his shop and operated a
restaurant which was located on the west side of main street, where the Drug
Store is at present.  Later, he owned a butcher shop on the east side of main
street, where the Wolff Barber Shop is now located.  They and their son Estel
moved to Spokane, Washington, where Estel is presently living.


212.   PITT, FERDINAND & ROSE

[picture]  Mr. and Mrs. Ferdinand Pitt were early settlers in the Kulm area.
They resided in Norden Township, a short distance from Kulm.

The couple had four children: Mrs. Albert Frohwerk, living in San Francisco;
Mrs. Raymond Rall, Kulm, N. Dak.; Mrs. Dale (Martha) Grossnickel, Indiana; and
Theodore, deceased.


213.   PRUETZ, SHARPE

[picture; refer to 1957 book]  Sharpe Pruetz is still functioning as president
of Kulm State Bank, and is currently president of the Lions Club, his second
term.  Mr. Pruetz was a presidential elector in 1960, when North Dakota's four
electors cast their vote for Richard F. Nixon.  John F. Kennedy received the
majority of electoral votes in the electoral college and became president.

Mrs. Pruetz is still active in Congregational Church activities.

218.   RENZ, JOHN & MARY (Wittmayer)

219.   ROST, CLIFFORD & AGNES (Weiler)

220.   ROST, JOHN & CHRISTINA (Bollinger)

[picture]  John Rost, son of Fred Rost, was born in Westpreussen, Germany on
November 20, 1898.  Mr. Rost grew to manhood in Germany and then served 3 years
in World War I, under Wilhelm Kaiser II.  He was wounded while in service and
held a prisoner in France for 28 months and 9 days.

In 1922, he decided to come to America because of fear of another war, and
arrived in Kulm in May, 1922.  Mr. Rost started arming northwest of Edgeley and
remained there for about three years.

On February 15, 1925, he married Christina Bollinger of Lehr, after which the
couple moved to Washington, returning in 1932.  They moved 10 miles southwest of
Kulm and lived there 19 years.  In October of 1951, they moved to Ellendale,
where, at present, Mr. Rost is employed with Dennis Whelan.

The couple was blessed with five children: Arthur, married to Lillian Heck of
Kulm; Ervin, Glendale, California; Alice, Ellendale, North Dakota; Annetta
married Edwin Fredrick of Ellendale; Hertha, who died on July 2, 1961.  They
also have six grandchildren.

222.   ROTT, CLARENCE & PEARL (Graff)

223.   RUFF, JOHN & MAGDALENA (Sprenger)

[picture]  John Russ was born in Artzees [Arzis], Bessarabia, South Russia on
the 24th day of November 1905, and came to the U.S. with his parents, Mr. and
Mrs. Jacob Ruff, who arrived in Kulm, North Dakota on the 26th day of November,
1909.  They settled on a farm, about three and one-half miles west of Kulm, that
his father had rented from John Stohl, and started farming in the spring of
1910.  In the fall of 1917, Mr. Ruff rented a farm, five and one-half miles west
of Kulm, from Dr. F.G. Benn of Minneapolis, Minnesota, and farmed there until
1923.

In the month of November, Mr. Ruff underwent surgery and died, leaving the
family fatherless.  This left the farm work for son, John Ruff.  Mrs. Jacob Ruff
was remaired to Adam Weidenbach of Wishek in the summer of 1925.  In the fall of
that same year, Mrs. Wiedenbach held an auction sale and sold all the property,
after which John Ruff rented the farm and started farming for himself.  He
married Magdalena Sprenger, of Wishek, at the Congregational Church on the 29th
of November.  He continued farming until 1934, when he sold out because of the
drought.

In 1939, Mr. Ruff rented a farm from D.F. Brost, which Mr. Ruff later purchased.
When Mr. Ruff's health started to fail, he purchased a home in town in 1962, at
which time he retired.  Mr. and Mrs. Ruff experienced many hardships, such as
the drought and the dust storms, and were determined never to give up, but
enjoyed the fruits of their labors.

The Ruffs were blessed with seven children: Clarence and LeRoy Ruff, Ashley,
North Dakota; LeVurne Ruff, Jamestown; Viola (Mrs. Eddie Raisers), New Rockford;
Delores (Mrs. Alvin Entzi), Fredonia; Mabel (Mrs. Alvin Pahl), Ashley; and Pearl
Ruff, Ellendale.

On August 17, 1936, Reinholt married Miss Emily Lux.

Emily is the daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Jacob Lux.

This union was blessed with three children.

The oldest son, Allen Ruff, is now employed in the Ashley, N. Dak. area as a

Their third child, Delmar, who is employed at the Farmers Union Oil Co., Ashley,

Mr. Ruff is residing in Ashley and is serving as the Chief of Police of Ashley,
N. Dak.

The Ruffs have one grandchild.  They also attend the EUB Church in Ashley, N.
Dak.

228.   SCHLECHT, GOTTHIELF & SUSI (Buerkle)

[picture]  Gotthielf Schlecht, son of Mr. and Mrs. Michael Schlecht, was born at
Fredonia, N. Dak. on March 22, 1911.  He married Susi Buerkle, daughter of Mr.
and Mrs. John G. Buerkle of Fredonia, on February 14, 1934.

The Schlechts lived north of Fredonia for 15 years, later moving to Kulm, where
they lived from 1949 to 1958.  In 1958, they moved back out to the farm, located
between Kulm and Edgeley, where they built a new farm home, finishing on
December 15, 1958.

Mr. and Mrs. Schlecht also saw years of drought, but, in spite of the many
disappointments through the dry years, continued their farming, which they are
still doing at present.

The Schlechts are active members of the Kulm Assembly of God Church.  They have
six children: Oliver L. Schlecht, who farms at Gackle, N. Dak.; Chester, a
farmer at Gackle; Muriel M. Carlson, who teaches in Ellendale; Wesley Schlecht

229.   SCHLECHT, JACOB & EMILIE (Sommerfeld)

230.   SCHNEIDER, ERNEST & CHRISTINE (Mogck)

Ernest Schneider was born in Berlin, Germany and came to the U.S. as a young
lad.  He operated the first meat market in Kulm, in the building now occupied by
Clarence Grosz, which was moved in 1917 from its original site where the Brost
Bros. Garage is now.  He had a very good business, especially in 1898, when the
railroad was extended west of Kulm.

In the year of 1902, Ernest was united in marriage to Christine Mogck, daughter
of Mr. and Mrs. Christian Mogck.  Then he attended a veterinary school in
Chicago and returned to Kulm as its first veterinarian.  He served this
community until about 1922, when the family moved to Bismarck, where he was
employed by the State as a veterinarian.  About 1935, they moved to Monte Bello,
Calif. And spent the remaining years of their lives there.

Dr. and Mrs. Schneider were blessed with three children: Lila, San Jose, Calif.;
Arnold, St. Louis, Missouri; and Lawrence, Washington, D.C.


231.   SCHNEIDER, J. & MARY (Steinwand)

[picture]  J.F. Schneider, son of Friedrich and Christina Schlenz Schneider, was
born March 15, 1902.  In 1925, Mr. Schneider started to farm for himself and on
April 6, 1926 he was married to Mary Steinwand, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Jacob
Steinwand, nee Katharina Engel.  In 1932, a son was born, but died shortly after
birth.

Mr. Schneider's farming consists of grain, dairy and purebred Hereford cattle.
In 1952, Hugo Kruger, a relative of Mrs. Schnider, came from Germany and joined
the Schneiders in their farming enterprise.  In 1956, Mr. Schneider became a
real estate salesman and, in 1960,  a real estate broker.  Now, Mr. Kruger does
the farming and Mr. Schneider devotes his time to the real estate business.


232.   SCHOCK, CHRIST & ROSINA (Rott)

Christ Schock was born in South Russia on June 2, 1892 and immigrated to this
country when he was 12 years of age.  His family settled on a farm between Kulm
and Ashley, N.D.

Christ married Rosina Rott in 1914.  They farmed near Ashley until 1920, at
which time they settled on a farm 15 miles southwest of Kulm.

The Schocks have 7 living sons and 2 daughters, 22 grandchildren and 6 great
grandchildren.  Henry, Fred and Arvin live in California; Arthur, Bennie, Elmer
and Bertha (Hasz) live in Ellendale, N.D.; Maggie (Bertsch) is near Forbes,
N.D.; and Gottlieb lives here in Kulm.

Mr. and Mrs. Schock retired in Ellendale 15 years ago.  On July 26, 1966, Mr.
Schock passed away.


233.   SCHOCK, EDWIN & OLGA (Konrad)

[picture]  Mr. Edwin Schock was born in March, Montana on December 30, 1915,the
son of Mr. and Mrs. Emanuel Schock.  On September 4, 1938, he was married to
Olga Konrad, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Jacob Konrad.  The Schocks, who have been
farming in the Kulm area, moved to the present location in 1962.  They have
three daughters.  They are Deloris, Mrs. Virgil Oswald, Mora, Minn.; Lucille,
Mrs. Howard Kusler, Bismarck, No. Dak.; and Vonnie Lou, Mrs. Lamont Moldenauer,
Missoula, Mont.


234.   SCHOCK, GOTTLIEB & LORRAINE (Hollan)

Gottlieb Schock, son of Mr. and Mrs. Christ Schock, was born on a farm south of
Kulm.  He married Lorraine Hollan, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Ed Hollan.  They
have three children: Thomas Craig, Lois Joy and Polly Jo.

Gottlieb was Standard Oil Agent in Kulm until 1960, when he was appointed Rural
Mail Carrier, on R. Rt. 2 of Kulm, by the U.S. Civil Service Commission.  He is
a member of the local American Legion Post, first serving as Post Adjutant and,
in the past 8 years, as Post Service Officer.  He is also a member of the Kulm
Lions Club, having served as Club Secretary- Treasurer the past 7 years, plus
zone chairman in 1965-66, and Deputy District Governor in 1966-67.

The Schock family are members of the American Lutheran Church in Kulm.


235.   SCHOTT, EMIL & FRIEDA (Klaudt)

[picture]  Mr. Emil A. Schott was born to Mr. and Mrs. John Peter Schott, five
miles west of Kulm, on April 28, 1914, where he grew to manhood.  He spent from
1942 to 1946 in the army and was stationed at different places in California.

On June 11, 1947, he was united in marriage to Mrs. Frieda Mittlestedt, daughter
of Mr. and Mrs. Gottlieb Klaudt of Beulah, No. Dak.  Soon after their marriage,
Mr. Schott purchased his parents' farm, where they still reside.

The Schotts have two children: Clayton and LuGail at home, and four children by
Mrs. Schott's previous marriage.  They are: Diana, Mrs. Darwin Nill of Monango,
No. Dak.; Floyd Mittelstedt, San Diego, Calif.; Nickolona is Mrs. Norbert
Steckler of Ipswich, So. Dak.; and Wesley Mittelstedt at Lowery Air Force Base,
Denver, Colo.


236.   SCHOTT, OTTO & ESTHER (Miller)

[picture]  Otto E. Schott was the oldest son of Mr. and Mrs. John Peter Schott.
He was born four miles west of Kulm and attended grade school in a rural school
1 miles west of the farm.  On July 11, 1937, he married Esther Miller, daughter
of the late Mr. and Mrs. John H. Miller.  She grew up about 12 miles south of
Kulm and also attended a rural grade school and then graduated from high school
at Ellendale and attended college there.  She taught school three years.  The
first four years of their wedded life were spent on a farm three miles west and
one mile south of Kulm.  They recall the grasshopper plague and the dust storms
of the late thirties.  However, they didn't give up the struggle to succeed and,
in 1941, purchased their own farm a few miles west of Kulm.  After five years,
they moved into the city of Kulm.  Otto operated the Schott Farm Equipment
store, an implement and appliance business, for seventeen years.  In 1963, he
liquidated the business and sold the building to the C and H Service.  He is
presently engaged in farming his twelve hundred and fifty acres of land
southwest of Kulm.

Six children were born to this union and they are: DeLyle John, graduated from
the local high school and received his Bachelor of Science degree in Mechanical
Engineering from the UND at Grand Forks, N. Dak.  He enlisted in the Air Force
as an officer and has received his Captain rating.  He has requested his
separation and will move to Cape Kennedy, Florida where he will be with the IBN
Co., after July 1, 1967.  He is married to Lory Nelson, daughter of Lt. Col. And
Mrs. James Nelson.  They have one daughter, Elise June.

Gilbert Bruce graduated from the Kulm High School.  He will be remembered as
being on the championship basketball team in 1958, when Kulm won the State Class
B high school basketball title.  They also went to the State in 1957.  He got
his B.S. degree in science and education from the UND, Grand Forks.  He taught
in the Crosby High School three years.  He received a science grant from the
University, where he is now going to graduate school.  Myrna Joyce graduated
from the local high school.  She also was active in 4-H work, receiving many
ribbons in that field.  She attended business college in Fargo for 2 years.  She
was employed in Denver for one year and is presently working in an insurance
firm as an underwriter assistant at Fargo.  Wayne Dwight, graduated from the
local high school.  He was a track star and broke a number of records.  He also
was a member of the basketball team that took the conference championship in
1962.  He graduated from the UND, Grand Forks with a BS Degree in business
administration.  He enlisted in the Air Force.  After June 5, he will be at
Lackland, Texas for basic training.  Barton Otto is a sophomore and Jeanette Joy
is a freshman in the local high school.  They all were active members during
their high school days in band, chorus and drama.  Mr. and Mrs. Schott and
family have been life long members of the Congregation Church and Sunday School.
The children have had twelve years of perfect attendance awards and also held
various offices in the CEYF, as well as the district Youth Fellowship.

The family picture was taken at the time of the silver anniversary of Mr. and
Mrs. Schott.


237.   SCHULDHEISZ, JACOB & CHRISTINA (Hehr)

[picture; refer to 1957 book]  Christina Schuldheisz, daughter of Mr. and Mrs.

7, 1902.  To this union were born seven daughters: Mrs. E.J. (Anna) Hehr; Mrs.
William (Emma) Staeck, Kulm; Mrs. Joe (Ida) Johs, St. Paul, Minn.; Mrs. Harold
(Bertha) Woodward; Mrs. Robert (Ruby) Duke; Mrs. William (Margaret) McCabe; and
Miss Elsa Schuldheisz, all of San Francisco, Calif.; three sons: Edwin, Edgeley;
Lawrence, Huntsville, Alabama, in the Army Ballistic Missile Corps for the past
20 years; and Harold on the home place, Kulm.  Mrs. Schuldheisz also has 23
grandchildren and 13 great grandchildren.  Josephine passed away at the age of
3.

Mr. Schuldheisz passed away November 21, 1961 at the age of 84.  Mrs.
Schuldheisz is still fairly active and lives alone.  She attends church
regularly and is a member of Ev. Zion Church in Kulm.


238.   SCHULDHEISZ, LUDWIG & CHRISTINA (Mauch)

[picture]  Ludwig Schuldheisz was born in Klostiz [Kloestitz], Russia on August
27, 1851.  On December 17, 1870, he married Christina Mauch, who was born on
September 9, 1852, also in Kloestitz, Russia.  In 1894, they immigrated to
America and settled on a farm six miles east of Kulm.  When, in 1903, they moved
to Witchita Falls, Texas, where they owned a cotton plantation.  They also lived
in Kingman, Kansas a number of years, when, in 1916, they moved back to Kulm,
where they lived until their death.  Mrs. Schuldheisz died February 27, 1937, at
the age of 85, and Mr. Schuldheisz passed away on December 11, 1938, at the age
of 86.  Mr. and Mrs. Schuldheisz were charter members of the St. Paul Lutheran
church and they had been married 67 years at the time of her death.

Their children were Jacob, John, Fred, Alex, Ludwig, Mrs. Maria Heck, Mrs.
Margaret Maier, Mrs Elizabeth LaBrenz, all deceased, and Dan living in Kulm.


239.   SHERMAN, R. & MATTIE (Potts)

R.H. Sherman was born September 16, 1888 at Mt. Vernon, South Dakota.  On
October 30, 1913, he married Mattie H. Potts at Vermillion, South Dakota.

Mr. Sherman practiced law at Kulm from August, 1914 to December, 1928.  He
served ad County Judge of LaMoure County from January, 1929 to January, 1939,
and was then appointed to State Board of Administration until August, 1957.

Mr. Sherman graduated from the School of Law at the University of South Dakota
in 1911 and practiced law in South Dakota before coming to Kulm in August, 1914.

Mr. and Mrs. Siegler farmed until 1946, when they moved into Kulm.  Mr. Siegler
passed away on April 22, 1955.  Mrs. Siegler still resides in Kulm.

244.   STEEN, BERNHARD & ALMA (Rolighed)

Bernhard T. Steen, son of Christian Hanson Steen and Petra Steen, was born at
Battle Lake, Minn. on June 10, 1884.  On November 10, 1926, Bernhard was married
to Alma Rolighed, daughter of Andrew and Anna Rolighed of Milan, Minn.

Bernhard's parents moved from Battle Lake, Minn. in 1893 and homesteaded
northeast of Fredonia.  In 1905, they moved to Kulm.  His mother passed away in
1925 and his father in 1926.  Bernhard was employed at the Kulm Farmers Elevator
for many years and, from 1926 to 1930, as its manager.  In 1930, he became a
traveling representative for the D.D. Tinney Commission Co. of Minneapolis and
served in that capacity for several years.  He also managed the Clitherall
Elevator for 15 years.  In 1958, Bernhard retired and, since that time, he and
Mrs. Steen have lived in Clitherall, Minn.

250.   TEMPLIEN, FRED & HEDWIG (Gall)

[picture]  Fred A. Templien, son of Mr. and Mrs. Michael Templien, was born
August 25, 1889 at Buffalo, Minn.  In 1906, Fred's parents homesteaded 7 miles
southeast of Kulm.  Fred served in World War I from September 20, 1917 to
August, 1919.  He then returned home to help his father on the farm as he was
the only son.  On October 26, 1931, Fred married Hedwig Gall, daughter of Mr.
and Mrs. Philipp Gall of Dickey County.  Within the next two years, Fred's
parents died, so he took over the home farm.

The Templiens have two sons: Carl A., farming the home farm since Fred retired
in 1960; and Emanuel H., married to Janice Hartman.  They have two children and
are living at Wahpeton, No. Dak., where Emanuel is taking schooling in blueprint
work.  Emanuel also served in the army from 1956 to 1959, spending much of that
time in Germany.

The Templiens knew many hardships during their years of farming, but at the same
time enjoyed it and still enjoy living their retirement years on the farm with
their son, Carl.


251.   TJERNLUND, CARL

252.   TJERNLUND, ELMER

[picture]  Elmer was the son of J.A. Thernlund, born October 22, 1883 in Oliden,
Norsjo, Sweden and came to this country in 1889 with his parents.  They settled
in Swede Township.  He went to Washington in 1903 and lived there until 1907,
when he returned to Kulm.

He married Elsa Nelson, February 22, 1909, of Mount Vernon, Wash., at the old
home place.  Mr. and Mrs. Tjernlund began their married life on a homestead
about 8 miles north of Fredonia, where their daughter, Dorothy (now Mrs. Emil
Wolff of Spokane, Wash.) was born.  Four days later, Mrs. Tjernlund passed away.

Mr. Tjernlund married Teckla Ostrom, September 7, 1911.  She was the daughter of
Mr. and Mrs. Carl Ostrom of Kulm.  They farmed until 1918, when they moved to
Kulm, where Mr. Tjernlund was a machine dealer until 1930.  They moved to
Minneapolis in 1943, where they spent the rest of their lives.  Both Mr. and
Mrs. Elmer Tjernlund were life long members of the Baptist Church.  They were
blessed with four children: (Ethel) Mrs. John Cowles, Minneapolis, Minn.;
Lorraine, Minneapolis, Minn.; (Phyllis) Mrs. Morris Carlson, Easton, Penn.; and
Rodney of Minneapolis, Minn.  They had 8 grandchildren.

Mr. Tjernlund passed away March 7, 1963 and Mrs. Tjernlund, March 5, 1966.


253.   TJERNLUND, OLAF

Mr. and Mrs. Olaf Olson Tjernlund were born in Sweden in 1829 and were married
in 1857.

They came to the U.S. with their two sons Anton and Peter, and arrived in
Edgeley in 1889.  They filed on a homestead in Swede Township.

Olaf died in 1891.  Mrs. Tjernlund in 1909.

They have one grandson at Kulm, Carl, who lives on the old homestead.


254.   TROMBUL, CHARLES

Charles Trombul came to the Kulm community in about 1904 and settled on a farm
eleven miles south and three miles east of Kulm.  He was a widower from Iowa and
left his children with some of his relatives there.  He was known to most
everyone as Charlie.  He was a man of education and refinement.  Many wondered
what brought him to these parts.

He was assisted by his faithful cook and hired man, Cinch Briggs.  After a
couple of years, a sister of Charlie's came to cook and keep house for him,
while Cinch, in pioneer terms, hatched out a homestead nearby.  The two
continued to work together for a few years.  Charlie, in the meantime, married
again.  A son was born to this union.  In 1917, Charlie built a large barn and
set up to dairy, along with farming.  After several years, he sold out to Cinch
Briggs and moved to California, where he retired in later years.

Charlie was a well read man and of good principal.  His departure from the
community left a void that was felt for a long time, but he left many friends
and fond memories behind.


255.   VOGEL, FRED & EDNA (Radke)

[picture]  Fred Vogel was born in So. Russia, February 2, 1906.  He came to this
country in 1910 and worked as a farm hand until 1927, when he was married to
Edna Radke.  He started farming on his own and is still farming.  In 1956, they
moved to Kulm and their son, Lawrence took over the farm.

They have six children: Ervin, Ellendale, who is RTA Co-op manager; Lawrence,
farmer; Lloyd, in Seattle, working with real estate; Lillian (Mrs. Rueben
Junkert), Jamestown; Rueben, a barber; Morris is in Denver, teaching school; and
Marcella, in Moorhead, Minn., employed by Red River Grain Co. of Moorhead.

Fred served on school and township boards, and on the Farmers Union Co-op
Elevator board for 18 years.  He is president of Kulm Credit Union since
organized in 1949.  He is also on the NDCU League board.

They are active members of Kulm Assembly of God Church and have lived in Kulm
since 1939.

258.   WEILER, GOTTFRIED

[picture]  Gottfried Weiler was born May 8, 1902, in South Russia, the son of
John and Christean Weiler.  They came to the U.S. in 1904 and homesteaded near
Ashley, No. Dak.  In 1907, the family moved to Clinton, Oklahome; in 1910 they
moved back to Ashley; they also farmed near Kulm and in 1916 they moved to
Wishek.  In 1918, Gottfried worked on the railroad at Wishek and, in 1921, the
family again moved to a farm south of Fredonia.

In February, 1925, Gottfried and Anna Seigler were married.  Four children were
born to this union.  They are: Agnes (Mrs. Clifford Rost, Kulm); Howard, Detroit
Lakes, Minn.; and two children died in infancy.  Gottfried purchased a farm in
1942, eleven miles south of Kulm, and has farmed there since that time.  Mrs.
Weiler became ill and spent six years in a wheelchair.  She passed away in
September, 1963.

On August 22, 1964, Mr. Weiler was married to Alma Hildebrand and they are
living on the Weiler farm.

261.   WIDMER, RUDOLPH & FRIEDA (Giesler)

[picture]  Rudolph Widmer, son of Mr. and Mrs. Andrew Widmer, was born in Russia
on October 2, 1898, and came to this country when he was 2 1/2 years old.  His
parents settled 11 miles southeast of Kulm when they arrived in this country.
At the age of 16, he went to Parkston, S.D., where he worked on the farm for
several years.  He returned home at the age of 22 and helped on the farm.

He married Frieda Giesler, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. John Giesler of Kulm, in
1925.  Mrs. Giesler was also born in Russia and arrived here, with her parents,
in 1910.  She attended Sunday school and was confirmed in the Hoffmanfield
Church by the Rev. H. Nuetzmann.  The couple lived 4 miles northeast of Jud in
1928, later moving to the farm where they are presently living.

The Widmers were blessed with eleven children.  Seven sons and 4 daughters:

263.   WOEHL, ALBERT & BERTHA (Bader)

[picture]  Albert Woehl was born to Phillip and Christina (nee Rott) Woehl on
May 20, 1908, at the farm home 11 miles south of Fredonia.  On March 20, 1932,
he was united in marriage to Bertha Etta Bader, who was born July 11, 1911, the
daughter of Daniel and Marie (nee Schultz) Bader, who lived 10 miles south of
Kulm.

The Woehls started to farm on the old homestead and, as Mr. Woehl recalls, their
first crop was in 1932, when they sold wheat @ 24 cents a bu.; rye @ 11 cents a
bu.; barley @ 8 cents a bu.; oats @ 6 cents a bu.; eggs @ 5 cents a doz., and a
10 gallon can of cream brought $3.00.  In 1935, there was a fair crop.  However,
for a few years following, there was no crop at all and they picked cow chips in
summer and fall for use as fuel during the winter.

The Woehls still reside on the old homestead and Albert is the oldest farmer
living in the Anderest voting precinct.  They have three daughters living in
Milpitas, Calif.  They are: Della Mae, Mrs. Clarence Heller.  Clarence is the
son of David and Soffie Heller.  Vivian, Mrs. Eugene A. Wahl.  Eugene is the son
of Fred Wahl.  And Sharon Grace, Mrs. Norman Denning.  Norman is the son of John
and Emma Denning.  They also have 7 grandchildren.



264.   WOEHL, ARTHUR & MARION (Kramlich)

1931.  On June 19, 1960, he was married to Marion Kramlich, daughter of Mrs. And

The Woehls have one child, Kevin Keith, born August 12, 1961.  They own and live
on the farm originally operated by his father, Gust Woehl.


265.   WOEHL, GUST & CHRISTINA (Schilling)

[picture]  Gust Woehl, son of Mr. and Mrs. Philip Woehl, was born April 6, 1899
on a farm south of Fredonia, North Dakota.

He was united in marriage to Christina Schilling.  Mrs. Woehl was born in
Borodino, South Russia  on May 15, 1905.  She was six years old when she came
with her parents to America on March 30, 1911.  Mr. and Mrs. Woehl moved to a
farm 15 miles southwest of Kulm, a farm they bought from Mr. and Mrs. John
Schweigert in 1926.

For many years, Gust was in the threshing and farming business.  For a couple of
years, we had good crops, but prices were real poor.  For a bushel of wheat, we
got 25 cents, barley 14 cents, eggs 10 cents, and cream was real cheap.  They
experienced some very bad years of severe drought, when dust clouds rolled
across the prairie, like a bad snow storm, leaving huge dust drifts along fences
and roads.

For fuel and cooking, we picked dried manure chips.  It was so dry, nothing grew
but thistles, which we cut for hay to keep our livestock alive.  Our cattle had
to be sold to the government.  Cows brought $20 and calves 4 to 6 dollars.

In the forties, crops became better, but due to the war, most everything was
rationed.  We had to have stamps to buy sugar, coffee, meat and gasoline.  In
1943, we got a permit to build a barn, obtaining the lumber from local and
neighboring towns.

Mr. Woehl served as director of Antelope School District for 21 years.

They attend the Berlin Baptist Church of rural Fredonia, North Dakota.

Five children were born to this union of which four are living.  One son,
Wilbert, died in infancy in 1929.  Alice, (Mrs. Herbert Buerkley, living on a
farm south of Kulm.  Arthur, married to Marion Kramlich, living on his father's
farm, [at] Fredonia.  Ida, (Mrs. Edwin Fregien), living on a farm south of Kulm.
Pearlene, (Mrs. Lloyd Koenig), living in West Fargo, North Dakota.  They also
have eight grandchildren.

Mrs. And Mrs. Woehl are now retired and living in Kulm.


266.   WOEHL, HENRY & EMMA (Koenig)

[picture]  Henry Woehl, who was born in McIntosh County, 17 miles southwest of
Kulm, is the son of Mr. and Mrs. Phillip Woehl.  On May 13, 1929, he was united
in marriage to Emma Koenig, the daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Nathaniel Koenig, who
lived 13 miles northwest of Fredonia.

Mr. and Mrs. Woehl started farming in 1929, when they built a two room house and
moved a small barn from the Phillip Woehl farm onto this quarter, which Henry
had purchased from his dad to make a home on their own.  Mr. Woehl started
farming with three pair of horses and a very small line of machinery.  They
endured many hardships during the depression years, when there were no crops to
be harvested and not enough moisture for even the grass to grow to be cut for
hay, so they put up thistles to feed the livestock.  For approximately eight
years, they purchased seed and sowed it and reaped very little when harvest time
came, which meant that it took a lot of courage and faith to keep going.  One
year, Mr. Woehl recalls when they had a fair crop standing, but, before they got
in with the header, the grasshoppers got in and made a clean sweep of the crop.

As the years went on, and things began to look more prosperous, the couple
purchased more land and began to operate with larger and more modern machinery.
The children grew up and left home.  Mr. and Mrs. Woehl farmed alone for three
years, but this was too much for them.  They sold their farm to their youngest
son, Raymond, in 1962 and moved into their new home in Kulm in March of 1963.
Mr. Woehl is now semi-retired.  As his ambition and interest in agriculture is
still to great to give up entirely, so he still does a little farming.

The Woehls have four children: LuElla, Mrs. George Gieger of Glendive, Mont.,
who has three children: the twins, Darlene, Mrs. Virgil Bjur, who farm northwest
of Kulm and Darwin, who is married to LaVonne Johnson and have three children;
and Raymond, who is married to DeNome Johnson, who, with their two daughters,
live on the Woehl homestead.


267.   WOEHL, RAYMOND & DENOME (Johnson)

[picture]  Raymond Woehl was born August 5, 1938 in McIntosh County, the son of
Mr. and Mrs. Henry Woehl.

On October 4, 1959, he married DeNome May Johnson, who was born May 12, 1935,
the daughter of Mrs. Fannie Johnson and the late Herman Johnson of LaMoure
County.

The Woehls spent over three years of their married life in Lead, So. Dak. where
Raymond was employed with the Homestake Gold Mine and did farming on the side,
when, in 1963, he purchased the farm from his parents.

They have two children: Charlene Rae, born January 16, 1961, and Cary Jean, born
October 13, 1966.


268.   ZACHRISON, CLAYTON & DARNELLA (Keller)

Clayton Zachrison, son of Mr. and Mrs. Verner Zachrison, was born at Kulm on May
20, 1930.  He married Darnella Keller, June 30, 1960.  She was born May 28,
1938, the daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Henry Keller of Kulm.

The couple moved to their present location, 7 miles northeast of Kulm, in Swede
Township, April 30, 1961, where they are engaged in farming.

Mr. and Mrs. Zachrison have two children: Jolene Kaye, age 3 and Laurel Ann, 1
1/2.


269.   ZACHRISON, CLIFFORD & AGDA (Bradford)

[picture]  Clifford W. Zachrison was born February 1, 1898 on a farm 14 miles
northwest of Kulm.  His parents were Mr. and Mrs. Zachri Johnson.  Mr. Zachrison
attended Swede School.  His father died at an early age, leaving Clifford to
help operate the farm with his mother, brothers and sisters.

On October 30, 1935, Clifford was united in marriage to Agda Bradford, daughter
of Mr. and Mrs. Carl Bradford, who farmed 5 miles north of Kulm.  They rented
his home farm and later bought it.

The Zachrisons have seven children: Burnette (Mrs. Art Schlenker, Kulm); Audrey
(Mrs. Raymond Roloff, Kulm); Eloyce (Mrs. Art Holmstrom, Alfred); Dale,
Litchville; Corinne (Mrs. James Kasowski, Buffalo, N.D.); LeMar (on the home
farm); and Renaye (Mrs. Curtis Moldenhauer, Gackle).


270.   ZACHRISON, VERNER & HILDUR (Bradford)

Verner Zachrison, son of Mr. and Mrs. Zachri Johnson, was born on a farm
northwest of Kulm on August 24, 1900.  On April 10, 1926, he married Hildur
Bradford, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Carol Bradford.  To this union four children
were born: Jeanette (Mrs. Lawrence Swingen of Lynwood, Calif.); Clayton (married
and farming north of Kulm); Delwayne (teaching in Calif.); and Carole Jean
(chemist, working at Metabolism Research Laboratory in Fargo, N. Dak.).

Since March of 1927, the Zachrisons have lived on and continued to farm the same
farm 5 miles northeast of Kulm.



ORGANIZATIONS -

AMERICAN LEGION AUXILIARY

[picture:  Mrs. Justina Bohnet, Mrs. Eric Burk, Anna Kjos, Mrs. Lydia Orth, Mrs.
Maggie Siewert, Ida Fey, Mrs. Fred Himmerick, Mrs. Richard Herman, Mrs. Mollie
Kosanke, Carol Hilscher, Kathy Koenig, Maggie Rossman, Mrs. Rineholt Harr, Mrs.
Earl Grosz, Mrs. Owen Wallace, Kathy Stadler, Mrs. Richard Stadler, Sandra Harr,
Mrs. John Borth, Vicki George, Mrs. Russel Gackle, Mrs. Jake Diede, Karen
Himmerich, Darlene Denise, Mrs. Herbert Hilscher, Mrs. Erma Muheim, Mrs. James
Herman, Kathy Bethke.  Not on picture: Mrs. Hulda Briske, Mrs. Art Okerlund,
Mrs. Rebecca Schmidt, Mrs. William Schwab, Lois Schock, Polly Schock and Julie
Wolff.]

The American Legion Auxiliary Unit to Robert Kurtz Legion Post No. 57, organized
on June 18, 1932, has functioned and participated in the aims and
accomplishments of the American Legion the past thirty-five years.

Mrs. Erick Burk and Mrs. Justina Bohnet are the only two charter members still
belonging to the unit.  Mrs. Rynold Kurtz and Mrs. C.W. Ware passed away in
1957.  Mrs. Rebecca Schmidt, Lodi, Calif., and Mrs. William (Violet) Schwab,
Lydon Station, Wisc., are out of state members.

Seven senior and eleven junior members have joined the unit in the past ten
years.  Carol Hilscher, a junior of more than ten years, was the unit's first
tiny tot and joined when just an infant.  The membership, at present, is thirty-
six.  The unit is rated superior through department and national qualifications.

We feel honored that Mrs. Alvin Moltzen, nee Viola Fregien, a former Kulmite,
has served in various State department offices including department president.
At present, she is National vice president of the Northwest Division, which
comprises seven States.

The unit's present officers are: president, Mrs. Richard Stadler; vice
president, Mrs. James Herman; secretary, Miss Maggie Rossman; treasurer, Mrs.
Herbert Hilscher; sergeant-at-arms, Mrs. Richard Herman; historian, Mrs. Mollie
Kosanke; and chaplain, Mrs. John Borth.

Our Gold Star mothers are Mrs. Marie Kurtz (deceased), World War I, and Mrs.
Anna Kjos and Mrs. Lydia Orth, World War II.


KULM CITY BAND 1925-1967

The Kulm City Band was organized late in 1925 and, down thru the years, has
never disbanded or hung up.  There are still four of the charter members serving
in the band.

The directors are: Hugo Ogren, Oscar Lange and Bill Wolf.  There are two other
members of long standing: C.W. Ward of Edgeley, who joined in 1929 and Lee
Holland in 1930.  During the band's hey day, it has played from Mandan to Fargo,
and practically all of the smaller towns within driving distance of Kulm.  On
July 4th of 1949, North Dakota U.S. Senators William Langer and Milton R. Young
honored the band by designating it the North Dakota U.S. Senators' Band.  The
late A.R. Kempf was the band's first Drum Major, followed by the late L.J.
Brown, father of the actress, Angie Dickinson.  After that, majorettes took
over.  Sylvia Hollan, the director's daughter was the band's first majorette,
followed by Marlyn Mueller, Ramona Young, Beatrice Kramlich, Jo Englebretzen,
Ardella Schlauch, Janice Gackle and the present majorette and Dairy Princess,
Nancy Forsman.  Ed Hollan is Director, Aft Hollan Assistant Director, and
William Gackle President.

WHITESTONE BATTLEFIELD STATE PART

Nestled in the grassy hills of western Dickey County lies the Whitestone
Battlefield Historic Park.  Here, on September 3, 1863, was fought the battle of
Whitestone Hill, the last stand of the Sioux, east of the Missouri River.

The U.S. Cavalry troops engaged were the 6th Iowa Calvary and the 2nd Nebraska
Cavalry under Colonels Furnas and Wilson, the entire command under Major General
Alfred Sully.

Beginning in August of 1862, Santees attacked the white settlements in southern
Minnesota, the attack known as the New Ulm Massacre.  Some forty or fifty of the
warriors were captured and executed.  The rest fled northwest to Dakota
Territory to the Sioux, who reluctantly gave them protection.

General Sibley, in the spring of 1863, was sent towards Devils Lake with a
brigade of cavalry and General Sully, from Sioux City, Iowa, with another
brigade of cavalry was to march toward where Bismarck is now located, the
intention of catching the warriors in a huge pinchers movement, crushing them in
one blow.  Their plans miscarried as Sully's supply boats were delayed by low
water in the Missouri River.  General Sibley fought the Indians in three running
fights between Devils Lake and Bismarck and a fourth battle south of Bismarck at
Apple Creek, where the Indians crossed the Missouri River.  Here Sibley rested
his troops, waiting for Sully one week.  As his supplies were running low, he
then marched his troops back to Minnesota, after which the Indians promptly re-
crossed the Missouri and wended their way about a hundred or more miles
southeast to Whitestone Hill for their annual buffalo hunt.  Though the main
village and assembly point was Whitestone Hill, the Indians had several places,
in what is now Dickey County, where the main "kill" was held.  The meat and
hides were brought to Whitestone where the meat was cured and the hides tanned.
Less than a third of the Indians in the hunting party were guilty of the
Minnesota Massacre in 1862.

Late in August, Gen. Sully, with his Iowa and Nebraska Cavalry, reached the site
of the last battle Sibley fought and learned from a deserted old Indian what had
happened.  Some historians tell that this old Indian was left behind to die, but
the medics took him, dressed his wounds and fed him, and so, to show his
gratitude, the Indian turned scout for Sully.

Sully then determined to take a quick swing to the southeast in search of the
Indians and then make a rapid march to his base camp north of Pierre.  After a
couple days of marching and scouting, Sully reached Long Lake, where the village
of Moffit now stands.  Here he spent several days sending out scouting parties,
but finding nothing.  He marched the following day to Red Lake, just outside of
the village of Burnstad.  On the following day, Sully marched his 6th Iowa and
2nd Nebraska cavalry units to the southeast, about three miles apart, with a
battalion of the 6th cavalry under Major House, as a scouting party, to the east
of the group.  Sully's guide, LaFramboise found the Indians in camp in ravines
near Whitestone Hill.  A scout was send back to Sully at all possible haste.  It
was mid-afternoon and Sully had called a halt at a good camping site where the
soldiers were resting after their long march and relaxing after an afternoon
meal.  The horses had been turned out to graze.  On the news of the scouting
party finding the Indians, at once the bugle sounded "Boots and Saddles", the
horses were taken up and in a short time the troops were on the gallop toward
the Indian village, some nine or more miles to the northeast.  Major House had
held the Indians at bay until the entire command came.

Historians differ on the details of the battle, but, according to Sully's
report, the Indians were hemmed in a ravine with Major House on the north and
Colonel Furnas on the south, with General Sulley and Light Artillery ready on
the west.  The battle started about sunset and was fierce for an hour or more.

Chief Two Bears (1797-1871) headed the Yanktonia Tribe.  In leading a charge,
Chief Big Head was killed and is buried near the monument.

It is believed that some of the soldiers were killed with their own bullets in
the cross-fire.

The next two days were spent in burying the dead, caring for the wounded and
gathering up the 100 or more Indian babies and other prisoners.  One report was
that an Indian baby was found trying to nurse on its dead mother.

Space forbids more records and details of this battle.  However, after more than
a century of retrospection, we can have little reason to blame the Indians for
fighting to save their homes and hunting grounds.  In the opinion of most
historians today, the Battle of Whitestone Hill was all a mistake and injustice
to the Indians.

Today, on the high hill stands a monument of a cavalry soldier sounding Taps for
his fallen comrades.  This monument was erected in 1909.  During the mid-
thirties, the rustic museum was built, the flag displayed, a well dug.  In the
following years, many improvements have been made.

In the early forties, with the passing of older board members, new board members
were selected, who lived more locally to Whitestone.

Interest in the place grew.  Landscaping was done, a concession stand built, a
dock for the boats and swimmers was put in.  The one room schools, passing out,
gave much of their playground equipment to the place.  Soft ball, volley ball,
horseshoe, etc. make up sports enjoyed by old and young.  About 1960, the ladies
of the Northwest Thrifty Homemakers Club took over the concession stand and
operated it Sunday afternoons and all holidays.

It was then that Whitestone began to roll, and attendance grew.  Each season saw
an increase in registration.  There has been an average of 3,500 registrations
each season from May 15th to October 15th, representing over 30 States annually
and several foreign registrations.

In July of 1963, the Whitestone Centennial celebration was held.  A two day
celebration in which an estimated 10,000 people were present on Saturday and
over 20,000 on Sunday.

Indian dancers from Fort Totten were the chief attraction and were given the
honor spot in the parade as well as first prize.

A rodeo was staged Saturday evening and on Sunday afternoon by the Maud Evans
Riding Club of Forbes.

Three different bands were present each day in the parade, the Kulm City Band
played both days.

A company of National Guard Engineers from Edgeley have started a project within
the grounds.  A sand beach has been put in for bathers near the new bath house.
The old burrow pit has been filled in and leveled out, and the roads within the
park are to be improved this spring and summer.

The following men comprise the Whitestone Battlefield Board: Gottlieb Hartman,
Henry Miller, Gottlieb Steinwand, Frank Rhoe, Don Glynn, Len Popp, and Ed Hollan
is chairman.  Otto Wendland is caretaker.


SWEDE TOWNSHIP BAND

[picture:  Gust Smith, Carl Carlson, John O. Davidson, Sigfrid Johnson, Victor
Samuelson, Algot Johnson, Hugo Bloomquist, Elmer Larson, Willie Samuelson,
Philip Ogren, Arvid Larson, Ted Johnson, Albert Samuelson, Frank Carlson, Hugo
Ogren, John Malm, Gust Malm.  Refer to 1957 book.]

More information on the Swede Township Band has been compiled from memories of
participants, mostly Hugo Ogren, who is still active in the Kulm Band.  The
Swede band was organized in 1906.

Their first performance was the 4th of July, 1906 celebration at the Gust
Dahlberg farm 8 miles north of Kulm.  They performed in Fredonia at the 4th of
July, 1907.  On July 4, 1908, they played at the celebration in Kulm and later
played for the old settlers picnic at the Joe Hollan farm.  In 1908, October 22,
they played for the Democrat Rally in Kulm, stayed overnight and the next day,
October 23, played for the Republican Rally.

In June, 1908, they played for the LaMoure County school picnic at the Steels
Grove five miles west of Edgeley.  Jones, of Rock, N.D., was the speaker.  On
February 22, 1909, they serenaded Mr. and Mrs. Elmer Tjernlund on their wedding
day.  They played for two Woodman's celebrations at Jud.  One in 1908 and the
other, July 4, 1910.

In 1911, they serenaded Mr. and Mrs. Elling Kjos on the evening of their
wedding.  We had to use torches for light and Mrs. Kjos said it was the most
beautiful sight she had ever seen.  They also played for a picnic at Leander
Bjur's Grove.  Their last performance was at a picnic at Ole Olsons in 1915.


KULM 4-H CLUB

[picture:  Kathy Peters, Crystal Flegel, Lori Luck, Denise Martinson, Nancy
Luck, Lee Ann Wallace, Joy Schock, Mrs. Alwin, Mary Luck, Joanne Johannes, Polly
Schock, Kathy Stadler, Janice Elhard.]

The Kulm 4-H Club was first organized in 1928 under the sponsorship of Kulm
Homemakers Club.  First leaders were Mrs. B.T. Steen and Mrs. Theodore Geidt,
who continued till 1937.  From 1937 till 1942, Mrs. Rynold Kurtz took over.  In
1942, Mrs. P.J. Eberhard and Mrs. Fred Alwin were elected leaders.  In 1959,
Mrs. Eberhard moved away and since then Mrs. Alwin has continued alone.

Kulm 4-H Club continues to be an outstanding organization, and members derive
much benefit from belonging to it.

Present members are: Joy Schock, Polly Jo Schock, Denise Martinson, Kathy
Stadler, Kathy Peters, Crystal Flegel, Janice Elhard, Mary Luck, Nancy Luck,
Joann Johannes, Lee Ann Wallace and Lori Luck.


KULM CIVIC LEAGUE

Kulm Civic League disbanded in 1963, holding their last meeting on June 11.  The
League left their treasury to the Kulm Swimming Pool Fund.  [Refer to 1957
book].

In addition to all the programs listed in the 1957 Jubilee book, the club in
more recent years sponsored a Pap test program in 1962, and supported a new Boy
Scout program before disbandment.  They also helped conduct the swim pool
campaign in 1961.  After ending their 50 years existence, the club turned over
the city auditorium kitchen to another group.

HARMONY HOMEMAKERS

[picture:  Ruth Janke, Rose Gall, Lorraine Janke, Edna Herrmann, Audrey Fregien,
Marion Gackle, Marilyn Gackle, Elsie Kleingartner, Ruth Herman and Helma Herman.
Absent was Eliza Harr.]

The Harmony Homemakers of the Kulm area was organized February 1954 with Marilyn
Gackle, Rose Gall, Alma Herman, Edna Herman, Esther Herman, Ruth Janke and Katie
Keller as Charter members.  These ladies were organized into a group whose
common interests center in homemaking and handicrafts.  Each year, the group
entertains their husbands at a banquet and, in July, the annual family picnic is
held.  For fund raising projects, food at auction sales has been served.  Each
year, members of the group have participated in activities at achievement day
held annually at Grand Rapids and also sponsor a candidate for Dairy Princess.
A girl has also been sponsored annually to either Music Camp or Girls State.

At the Jubilee of 1957, the club won first prize on their float, which consisted
of old relics.


JOLLY HOMEMAKERS

[picture:  Vivian Heidinger, Sharon Boe, Phyllis Hoggarth, Audrey Kinslow,
Phyllis Berntson, Joyce Johannes, Adella Kjos, Marjorie Flegel, Darnella
Zachrison and Evelyn Stadler.]

The Jolly Kulm Homemakers Club was organized in 1955.  There are ten members at
the present time.  They are as follows: Mrs. Harold Kjos, Mrs. Wm. Kinslow, Mrs.
Clayton Zachrison, Mrs. Richard Stadler, Mrs. Lionel Flegel, Mrs. Lester
Berntson, Mrs. Cleon Hoggarth, Mrs. Edwin Heidinger, Mrs. Maynard Johannes and
Mrs. Dennis Boe.  There are three Charter members: Mrs. Harold Kjos, Mrs.
Richard Stadler and Mrs. Lionel Flegel.

In 1960, the club sponsored Janet Tjernlund for the Dairy Princess Contest.  She
was chosen LaMoure County Dairy Princess.

The club donates to several charities.  We painted trash cans and donated them
to Whitestone Battlefield State Park.  The members entertained their husbands at
an annual valentine banquet.  A family picnic is held in July.  The club is
sponsoring a candidate for the 75th Jubilee and a unit for the parade.

The present officers are: President Mrs. Lionel Flegel; Vice President Mrs.
Harold Kjos; Treasurer Mrs. Richard Stadler; Secretary Mrs. Clayton Zachrison;
Program Chairman Mrs. Edwin Heidinger.


KULM HOMEMAKERS CLUB

[picture:  Mrs. W.E. Brost, Mrs. Harold Wolff, Anna Bjornson, Mrs. Reinhold
Beich, Mrs. Fred Alwin, Mrs. Wilbert Konrad, Mrs. Lyle Bethke, Mrs. Harry
Nelson, Mrs. Mildred Grosz, Mrs. Christina Bowman, Mrs. Elna Anderson, Mrs.
Margaret Eslinger and Mrs. Albert Nelson.  Not pictured: Mrs. Edwin Lundgren,
Mrs. Dorothy Maier.]

[picture:  CHARTER MEMBERS, KULM HOMEMAKERS CLUB.  Mrs. Eric Bowman, Mrs.
Margaret (Brost) Eslinger and Mrs. Emil Anderson were among the founders of Kulm
Homemakers Club in 1928.]

The Kulm Homemakers Club, which was organized March 10, 1928, has been active
ever since.  They have never disbanded.  They still have three charter members.
They make their money, cooking for the Lions Club once a month.  They still
sponsor the 4-H Club.  They have donated to the swimming pool fund and bought a
wheel chair which is to be loaned out free.  Each year, they donate to the
Community Chest, both schools, and the Florence Crittendon Home.  They send a
girl to Girls; State, and a student to the Summer Music Camp at the Peace
Gardens and give Christmas gifts to the patients at the State Hospital.  Present
officers are: Addie Nelson, president; Christine Bowman, vice-president; Viola
Konrad, secretary, and Annie Bjornson, treasurer.


NORTHWEST THRIFTY HOMEMAKERS

[picture:  Mrs. Edmund Jenner, Mrs. Ed Hollan, Mrs. Gottlieb Hartman, Mrs. Edgar
Schlenker, Mrs. Henry Miller, Mrs. Donald Heck, Mrs. Edwin L. Hollan.]

The Northwest Thrifty Homemakers Club was organized in 1942, with a membership
of 8 members.  The officers were President Mrs. Edmund Jenner, Vice President
Mrs. Fred Hildebrand, Secretary Mrs. Dave Hildebrand, Treas. Mrs. Lee Hartman.

This year our club will celebrate its 25th Anniversary.  The 2 Charter members
are Mrs. Edmund Jenner and Mrs. Gottlieb Hartman.

The club meets 2nd Tuesday of every month, and is very active and interested in
the progress of the community.  We helped organize two new Homemakers Clubs in
Dickey County, also opened a concession stand at the Whitestone Battlefield
Park, which has helped to bring many visitors to the park.

We also make many donations to charitable organizations.  The major and minor
lessons given by the project leaders are very beneficial and educational to
every member.  The present officers are: President Mrs. Henry Miller, Vice
President Mrs. Gottlieb Hartman, Treasurer Mrs. Edmund Jenner, Secretary Mrs.
Donald Heck.  Other members are Mrs. Ed Hollan and Mrs. Edgar Schlenker.

Our motto is "Today's Home Builds Tomorrow's World"

Our Creed is: I believe my home is sacred; a place where love, faith, hope and
devotions have their beginning; where each has his rights respected by others,
where joys and blessings, sorrows and disappointments are shared in common;
where God is reverend and honored; fellowmen respected and love is law.  I
believe it is my duty to live up to the best that is in me to attain this, to
fear things unworthy, to conquer difficulties by daring to attempt them, to be a
companion as well as counselor to my family, and to teach and live, love of
home, country, fellowmen and God.

ASSEMBLY OF GOD CHRIST AMBASSADORS

[picture:  Vicky Anderson, Bonnie Johnson, Carol Carlson, Betty Davidson, Daniel
Anderson, Stuart Anderson, Curtis Anderson, Carolyn Anderson, LeAnn Sjostrom,
Diane Anderson, Marvel Malm, Mary Liechty, Counselor Mrs. Elroy Schlecht,
Counselor Clifton Bjur, David Sjostrom, Carolyn Bjur, Gary Anderson, Kaye

[picture:  NEW ASSEMBLY OF GOD CHURCH.  Refer to 1957 book.]

[picture:  Rev. Leroy Johnson.  Present Assembly of God Minister.]

Kulm Assembly congregation has continued to grow and, in October of 1958, ground
was broken for a new church on lots purchased from Mrs. Maggie Siewert.  Much of
the labor on the new church was donated by the people of the local congregation
and the building was completed and ready for occupancy by the 15th  of October,
1959.  A crowd of close to 600 people were on hand for the dedication services
which were held on the 22nd of November, 1959.  Rev. K.E. Olson, District
Superintendent of the North Dakota District of the Assemblies of God officiated
at the dedicatory services.

The facilities of the new church building, a 44 x 90 structure, are being used
to their fullest capacity.

Rev. Lloyd C. Jorgenson was pastor of the congregation while they were in their
building program.  He resigned as pastor in October of 1960, after 14 years of
faithful service.  Following his resignation, Rev. Marlin Kallevig served the
church until November of 1965.  The present pastor, Rev. LeRoy Johnson, came in
May of 1966.

Evangelism is kept as the heart of the local congregation.  A strong emphasis is
given to missions each month.  Several missionaries on the foreign field are
being helped in their support.  Support is also being received for home mission
projects, as well as the radio ministry of "Revivaltime."

The following departments are active in the work of the church: The Choir,
Sunday School, Women['s Missionary Council, Men's Fellowship, Christ
Ambassadors, Missionettes, and the Royal Rangers.

The Choir.  The choir, composed of 22 members, has added much to the spiritual
worship of the church.  They minister in both the morning and evening services
each Sunday.  They have presented several sacred concerts in the church as well
as in other churches in the area.  The choir is under the direction of Mrs.
Clarence Anderson.  Mrs. LeRoy Johnson serves as pianist.

Women's Missionary Council.  The Women's Missionary Council , in the local
Assembly, was organized by Miss Evelyn Westland and Miss Edith Storlie.  Through
the years, this group has been actively engaged in helping both the foreign and
home missionary.  The prayers of this group, as well as the contributions of
money, clothing, household items, etc., have meant much to the missionaries.

The W.M.C.s are divided into three groups.  Each group meets separately once
each month for prayer and to work on various missionary projects.  The last
Thursday of each month, they meet together for a devotional service and prayer.

Men's Fellowship.  The Men's Fellowship meets once a month for their regular
services.  This group, as well as contributing and having a concern for
missions, has completed several projects in the church which has added to its
beauty and usefulness.  Work days are held periodically.

Christ Ambassadors.  The Christ Ambassadors is the youth organization of the
church.  They meet each Thursday night for their scheduled services.  Missions
are stressed through their "Speed-the-Light" program.  This program provides
equipment for missionaries.  An offering is received each month for this
purpose.


ZION LUTHERAN CHURCH ESTHER CIRCLE

[picture:  Mrs. Lester Berntson, Mrs. Wm. Remien, Mrs. Inselmann, Rev.
Inselmann, Mrs. Dennis Buchholz, Mrs. Harold Fregien, Mrs. Wilbert Kinzler, Mrs.
Bernard Fandrich, Mrs. Bernard Rasch, Mrs. Robert Fey, Mrs. Maynard Johannes,
Mrs. Russell Gackle and Mrs. Clarence Rott.  Not pictured: Mrs. Owen Wallace.]


ZION EVANGELICAL LUTHERAN CHURCH

[refer to 1957 book.]  Due to the merger of several Lutheran Synods in 1960, the
church's headquarters has moved to Minneapolis, Minnesota, and is now known by
the name "The American Lutheran Church."

Pomona Valley Lutheran Church disbanded in 1957 and most of its members joined
the Evangelical Lutheran Zion Church.  Pomona Lutheran was the first organized
church in the Kulm vicinity.  That was in the year 1888.

Ministers who served the church since 1957 are: Rev. Wm. Fink, 1957-1960; Rev.
Walter Hummel, 1961-1964; Rev. Lawrence C. Pratt, Jr., 1964-1966; Rev. Rudolf
Inselmann is the present pastor who came to Kulm on August 23, 1966.

Two young people from this congregation, Mr. and Mrs. Llewellyn Hille, serve as
missionaries in Cameroon, West Africa.

At present, the congregation has a baptized membership of 200 souls.  It has a
Ladies organization known as Zion Lutheran Church Women, which is divided into
two circles: Esther and Mary-Martha.  It also has a Men of the Church
organization known as the Brotherhood and a Luther League, consisting of its
young people.  It has a fine Sunday School and a Junior Choir.

Members of the Church Council are: Pastor, Rev. R. Inselmann; President, Lloyd
Hildebrand; Deacons, Myron Holman, Russell Gackle, LeMar Kinzler; Trustees,
Richard Hildebrand, William Remein, Albert Heidinger; Secretary, Gene Young;
Financial Secretary, Duane Kinzler; Treasurer, Lester Berntson; Sunday School
Superintendent, Mrs. Dennis Buchholz.


ZION LUTHERAN MARY-MARTHA CIRCLE

[picture:  Mrs. Martin Kinzler, Mrs. Inselmann, Rev. Inselmann, Mrs. Edmund
Jenner, Mrs. Max Holman, Mrs. Gottlieb Schock, Mrs. Walter Puhlman, Mrs. Jake

KULM BAPTIST CHURCH

[picture:  Pastor Gerald Holmes and his wife, Jean, and their daughters, Kathy
Lynn and Tammy Sue had moved from Minneapolis, Minn. to Kulm on February 23,
1965.  Pastor Holmes had concluded his ministry at Scandia Baptist Church of
Waconia, Minnesota where he had served for 6 1/2 years.  Since their arrival in
Kulm, they have had one addition to their family, a son, Paul Wayne, who was
born on February 7th of this year.

Rev. Holmes is a graduate of Virginia Junior College, Virginia, Minnesota, the
University of Minnesota at Duluth, Minnesota and Bethel Theological Seminary in
St. Paul, Minn.  He was ordained to the Gospel Ministry in August of 1961 at
Waconia, Minnesota.]

"Just thinking back from 1957 to 1967."  Credo Erickson continued here as pastor
until August, 1959.  The Lord blessed their ministry during their stay here.  In
November, 1959, Jim Hubbard became our pastor.  Their love and concern in all
branches of the work was greatly appreciated.  During their ministry here, our
church was moved into Kulm.  In February, 1965, Rev. G. Holmes came here as
pastor and continues faithfully in the work.  The Holmes are living in the
parsonage that was purchased last April.  The parsonage out in the country was
sold to the Gust Ericksons.

Edwin Lindgren is the Vice Chairman of the church.

Our Sunday School Superintendent is Robert Buerkley and Junior Department
Superintendent is Alice Thernlund.  The Sunday School continues to help support
the Irvin Bjellands, missionaries to the Philippines.

Our older folks still active in the work are: Carl Anderson, Eddie Ostrom, Mr.
and Mrs. Signar Lundgren, Mrs. Christine Bowman, Mrs. Elna Anderson, and Carl
Tjernlund.  Some of our older members, living away from here, are: Mrs. Alma
Lindgren, Yakima, Washington; John Anderson, Seattle, Washington; and Mrs.
Haydle Smith, Minneapolis, Minnesota.  To all of these, we want to give special
honor and thanks for their faithfulness in prayer and support of the church;
indeed, we can truly say we appreciate them very much.

On July 1-2, 1967, we will observe the Seventy-fifth Anniversary of the Kulm
Baptist Church with our theme as "Looking Unto Jesus."

KULM BAPTIST MISSION CIRCLE

[picture:  Mrs. Robert Lundgren, Mrs. Erie Franz, President Mrs. Carlton
Lindgren, Mrs. J.E. Grenz, Mrs. Signar Lundgren, Mrs. Vernon Lindgren, Treasurer
Mrs. Christine Bowman, Mrs. Elna Anderson, Mrs. Henry Schuett, Mrs. Carl

[Refer to 1957 book]  The Kulm Baptist Mission Circle was organized in December
of 1907 by Missionary Mathilda Moberg, who later became Mrs. J.A. Tjernlund.
Our first executive committee was Mrs. J.A. Tjernlund, Elna Ostrom, Teckla
Ostrom, and Amanda Bjur.

Our ladies are a very active group and at present have several projects.  Our
first aim is Missions-sponsoring a Girls' Missionary Guild, sewing garments for
Missionary Supply Depot in Chicago, White Cross work for overseas, and sending
used clothing to the needy.  Just recently we had a library built in the church.

Three of our long time members that are still active in the work are Mrs. Elna
Anderson, Mrs. Christine Bowman, and Mrs. Signar Lundgren.  Mrs. Alma Lindgren,
also a longtime member, is living in Yakima, Washington.

Our executive committee, at the present, includes: President, Mrs. Carlton
Lindgren; Vice President, Mrs. Edwin Lindgren; Secretary, Mrs. Virgil Bjur;
Treasurer, Mrs. Christine Bowman.

Our prayer is that each one of us may be faithful in our service for the Lord.


KULM BAPTIST CHRISTIAN YOUTH FELLOWSHIP

[picture:  Mark Lindgren, Cheryl Lindgren, Secretary LuAnn Malm, Mary Lou
Lundgren, Gloria Lundgren, Brent Miedema, Billy Hamlin, Murray Franz, President
Emery Lindgren, Johnny Lundgren, Brent Lundgren.  Counselors are Caroline
Lindgren and Alice Tjernlund.  Absent: Lawrence Holmgren, Veryle Bjur, Linda and

1 Timothy 4:12

Our Teenager group officially became members of our National C.Y.F. of America
in December of 1954, during the pastorate of Reverend and Mrs. Paul White.  Our
first officers were Howard and Russel Tjernlund, and Sharon and Elisabeth
Lindgren.

The C.Y.F. members attend rallies and have recently hosted the North Dakota
rally.

In December of 1962, several of our youth attended the Teen Convention in
Washington, D.C.

Throughout the year, we have several Funspirations, inviting young people from
our local and out of town churches.  During the summer months, we especially
enjoy recreation at "Roy's Park."

The young people have been in charge of several services and are doing fine
work.

Our present officers are Emery, Mark, and Cheryl Lindgren, and LuAnne Malm.
Counselors are Caroline Lindgren, Alice Tjernlund, and Pastor Holmes.

Not pictured are Lorence Holmgren, Veryle Bjur, Linda and Lowell Lindgren.
Lowell is in the service, stationed at Fort Hood, Texas, and has just recently
completed a course as cook in Virginia.


[ZION EVANGELICAL UNITED BRETHREN CHURCH]

[picture:  Rev. Dennis E. Boe]

Current pastor is Rev. Dennis E. Boe.  Born in Kenmare, September 16, 1939.  He
is married to Sharon A. (Vixo) Boe and has two daughters, Dove 4, and Tammie 2.

He graduated from Minot High School in 1957.  From 1957 until 1962 was employed
and, while working, studied part time on his Bachelors Degree of Science at
Minot State College.

In June 1962, he was licensed to preach in the Methodist Church and appointed to
Rock Lake, N.D., serving one year.

In June 1963, the Methodist Church appointed him to Velva, N.D., where he served
for 2 1/2 years.  During this time, he continued part time studies toward a
Bachelor of Science Degree at Minot State College.

In June 1966, he was appointed to serve the Zion E.U.B. Church in Kulm.  During
this past year at Kulm, he has been commuting to college at Ellendale, N.D.,
where he is continuing studies toward a Bachelor of Science Degree with a major
in Social Science, and a minor in Art.  He will graduate in June of 1968.

CONGREGATIONAL LADIES AID

[picture:  Mrs. Reinhold Klettke, Mrs. Justina Bohnet, Mrs. Dan Patzer, Mrs.

Charter members, namely, Mrs. Justina Bohnet, Mrs. N.G. Isaak and Mrs. George
Gackle.

Officers are: President Mrs. Justina Bohnet; Vice President Mrs. Ossie George;
Secretary Mrs. George Gackle and Treasurer Mrs. Alice Gackle.

[picture:  CONGREGATIONAL ORGANIST.  Anna Billigmeier has served the
Congregational Church congregation faithfully since 1951.]

[picture:  REV. FRED HEYDEL]

Pastor and Mrs. Fred Heydel and family came to Kulm in April, 1963 from
Portland, Oregon, where he served the Evangelical Congregational Church over a
period of five years.

Pastor Heydel was born in Germany and came to America in his childhood years.
He received his schooling in Portland, Oregon, Los Angeles, Calif.  He returned
to Germany, before World War II, and completed a 4-year theological course in
Frankfurt, Germany.  While there, he met his wife, a pastor's daughter.

The Heydels have two daughters, Monika (Mrs. Keller) and Evelyn, and two sons,
John and Thomas.  Their son John received his four-year service discharge from
the U.S. Air Force in the spring of 1967, following one year of service in
Vietnam.

Rev. Heydel served parishes in New Jersey, Nebraska, Colorado, Portland, Oregon
and was engaged in religious radio broadcasting in Los Angeles, California for
several years.
