Rita Jans, Chrysta Hildebrand, Clyde Grosz, Youth Counselor Mrs. William Gackle,
Larry Goehring, Clayton Schott, Tom Heydel, Anthony Buerkley, Barton Schott,
Rodney Brosz, Wesley Gackle, Dale Strobel, David Gackle, Youth Counselor William
Gackle.  Not present, Fred Gackle, Donald Brandenburger, Pamela Brandenburger,
Doughlas Herman, Ronald Schlauch, Edwin Nill, Carol Hilscher, Donald Hilscher,
Robert Hilscher, LaVerne Smalley, Morris George, Sonja Isaak, Linda Kinzler,
Dwight Konrad.]

The Christian Endeavor Youth Fellowship are the youth of the Christian Endeavor
that has been an organization of the First Congregational Church for many years
and have always had their meetings with Sunday evening services.  For some time,
they met in the home of Mrs. Ida Smith (now Mrs. Paulson).

It was then that the young people felt they would like a young people society
and meet once a month, that way they would have more time for special
discussions.  Education and Fellowship have a more spiritual attitude toward
their lives and their fellow friends.  About thirteen years ago, under direction
of Rev. C. Martin and Counselor Mr. Erwin Gruneich, the young people set up a
program in which they would have charge of the service every other Sunday
evening and meet the 3rd Thursday evening.  There is a youth choir which sings
in the Worship service regularly.  Rev. F. Heydel also organized a band, which
renders its service of music at services.

They make contribution to many charitable institutions and support the
missionary work of the church and home and abroad.

We feel that the CEYF is a good training school for leadership.  With their
Pastor Rev. Heydel, Counselors, Mr. William Gackle and Mr. H.J. Herrmann, they
feel they have a part in serving Christ in their church and community.


CONGREGATIONAL DORCAS SOCIETY

[picture:  Mrs. Ben Hauff, Mrs. Lee Hollan, Mrs. Gottlieb Hartman, Mrs. Otto
Herrmann, Mrs. Rudolph Herman, Mrs. Gottlieb Herman, Mrs. Mathilda Brost, Mrs.
Christ Kruse, Mrs. Ted Netz, Mrs. Emil Schott, Mrs. LeRoy Oberlander, Mrs. Emil
Janke, Mrs. Richard Herman, Mrs. Fred Heydel, Mrs. Elmer Hildebrand, Mrs. Irvin

The Dorcas Society has at the present time 34 members.

Mrs. John Janke and Mrs. Mathilda Brost are the oldest members.

The meetings are held every third Wednesday of the month, the previous hostess
had charge of the devotional program.

Charter members of the Society are Mrs. Mathilda Brost, Mrs. John Janke, Mrs.
Deopold Herman, and Mrs. Rudolph Herman.

The present officers are president-Mrs. Walter Kusler, vice president-Mrs.
Robert Keller, secretary-Mrs. Wm. Kleingartner, and treasurer-Mrs. Harry Isaak.

KULM LIONS CLUB

[picture:  Tail Twister Roland Jans, Director Deopold Herman, Second Vice
President Lyle Bethke, Lion Tamer Oscar Lange, President Sharpe Preutz,
Secretary-Treasurer Gottlieb Schock, Third Vice President Oswald George,
Director John Blumhardt, Director Cleon Hoggarth, First Vice President Dennis
Buchholz, Ernest Gackle, Max Buechler, Harry Nelson, Harold Luck, Reinhold
Beich, Dr. J.E. Grenz, Owen Wallace, John Borth, Wilbert Brost, Elmer
Hildebrand, Fred Brost, Guest Jim Lundgren, Clifford Hildebrand, Wilbert Konrad,
William Gackle, Dr. Allen Hill, Hilmer Brost, Ben Koenig, Edwin Lundgren,
William Brauer, Arthur Krein, Jacob Steinwandt, and Fred Strobel.  Absent were:
Henry Gackle, Director Reinholt Harr, Lee Hollan, Harry Isaak, Maynard Johannas,
Irvin Mogck, John Peters, Richard Stadler, Harold Wolff, and Fred Vogel.]

[picture:  LION TAMER OSCAR LANGE in action with helpers, Mrs. Lange and Max
Buechler.  The team led community singing and other organized Lions activities
for many, many years.]

Kulm Lions Club has served the community as an active fraternal organization
whose prime function has been aiding the blind, and as a commercial club for 25
years, observing their 25th anniversary at a special banquet April 21, 1967.

The club was founded April 21, 1942, and the Lions have remained the sole
survivor of various commercial and civic clubs started through the years.
District Governor Walter M. Loomer, Grand Forks, has credited the local club's
ties with Lions International for its longevity.

Many city improvements and projects were started by the Lions over the years,
though assistance from other organizations and individuals were gratefully
received before completion.

Thirty three charter members gathered on April 21, 1942 to organize the group,
including six that are still members of the club: Sharpe Pruetz, Oscar Lange,
Ernest Gackle, Reinhold Beich, Wilbert Brost and Fred Strobel.

Twelve charter members have passed on since formation of the Club: Emil
Anderson, Edwin Hieb, Martin Gackle, F.C. Smith, G.E. Chambers, Ed H. Grosz,
C.W. Ware, Dave Brost, Adolph Smith, C.C. Buechler, Fred Fergusson and L.G.
Hixon.

Other charter members, no longer with the club, included W.J. Hildebrand, John
A. Rath, Dan Hoffman, G.M. Stephens, John A. Flegel, John Zimmerman, N.G. Isaak,
Arthur Mueller, Wesley Fredenburg, Otto Beich, Joe Melicher, Peter Eberhard,
Harris Miller, J.D. Smith and John J. Gackle.

At the banquet hild April 21, 1967, awards went to six charter members still
with the club and a special award went to Oscar Lange for 25 years faithful
service as Lion Tamer.  Oscar has led the group, at their monthly supper
meetings, in group singing for the full period, with the assistance of Mrs.
Lange at the piano for most of those 25 years, and the assistance of Max
Buechler on the violin.  Max joined the group about 1952.

Receiving awards were former Zone Chairmen Sharpe Pruetz, R.L. Jans, W.E. Brost
and Gottlieb Schock.  Schock also received an award for being deputy district
governor, a post he now holds.

Special mention goes to Dr. J.E. Grenz, who has been a member for 35 years,
joining at Napoleon 10 years before organization here.

Past presidents of the club include:

NEW KULM POSTOFFICE

[picture of new post office]

October, 1961 saw great advances in postal service in Kulm with the grand
opening of the new post office.  The 25x70 foot building has a brick face, and
all modern postal equipment and inside decor.  There is a 9x10 patron service
area, and an L-shaped outer lobby for box patrons.  Modern fluorescent lighting
provides plenty of light.  There are 285 boxes, concrete loading apron in back,
where early morning delivery of mail from the Jamestown Sectional Center gives
the postal clerks time to have all first class mail sorted into boxes by opening
time at 8 a.m.  Metro pick up of mail after closing time, overnight sorting and
the early morning deliveries give overnight service for first class mail to
almost any point in North Dakota.


KULM VOLUNTEER FIRE DEPARTMENT

[picture:  Chief Robert Gackle, Assistant Chief William Brauer, President Irvin
Mogck, Secretary-Treasurer Reuben Hehr, John Borth, W.E. Brost, John J. Gackle,
Oscar Lange, John Jenner, Otto Wendland, Reinhold Beich, John Stroehl, Harold
Hettich, Fred Schlenker, William Remien, Albert Buerkley, Ernest Gackle, Maynard
Johannes, Arthur Mueller, Max Buechler, R.G. Herman, Lyle Bethke, Dennis
Buchholz, LeRoy Gackle, Ruben Goehring, Fred Brost, Wilbert Konrad, Gottlieb
Schock, Oscar Konrad, Dr. Allen Hill, Donald Kramlich, Cleon Hoggarth, Oswald
George, Gerald Buerkley and Harold Luck.  Absent: H.A. Brost, E.E. Burk, Russell
Gackle, Wayne Hieb, Oscar Hoffer, Roland Jans, Art Okerlund, Dell Palm, John
Peters, Sharpe Pruetz, Fred Rossman.]

[picture:  Kulm's Rural Tanker and the five men who organized the rural fire
department, Harold Hettich, LeRoy Gackle, Robert Gackle, Wilbert Konrad and John
Borth.]

A major addition by the Kulm Volunteer Fire Department brought local fire
fighting methods and equipment up to one of the finest in the state in 1961.  In
October of that year, the new 1000 gallon rural tanker was purchased and fire
coverage extended to several complete townships around Kulm.  The new truck
sparked a building program, financed by the Civic and Improvement Association,
for an addition to the fire hall.  The three-stall building, 34x36 feet, now
houses both the city and rural truck and the city pickup.

The new truck boasts glass lined tank, 30 feet of 2 1/2 inch suction hose that
has come in handy many times drawing water from sloughs at rural locations.
Also, a 150 ft. reel of 1" hose, and several ladders.  A mounted platform and a
light hose up front help spray water while driving for field fires.

The new truck has made the Kulm Fire Department one of the finest equipped in
the State, and allows firemen to answer alarms a long way out, with city
protection still in force, using the other truck.  The individuals that signed
notes for the new truck, on the strength of additional revenue from added
territorial protection, must be given credit for a great deal of foresight.

BOY SCOUTS

[picture:  James Fandrich, Anthony Buerkle, BartonSchott, Tom Heydel, Paul Jans,
Mike Herman, Darcy Herman, Paul Elhard, David Gackle, Dweight Konrad, Kelly
Flegel, Reinhold Harr, Gary Himmerich, Curtis Bethke and Warren Gackle.]

First Boy Scout troop was started in Kulm in 1916.  Mr. Harry E. Emery was the
scout leader and Sharpe Pruetz was assistance scout leader.  Mr. Emery, at that
time, was Superintendent of the Kulm Public School.  The troop consisted of
seven boys.

Mrs. Oscar Lange and Mrs. Otto Gackle, through help of the Civic League, were
active leaders for a number of years.

Outside of a few local camping and outing trips, the boys were taken down to
Lake Cormorant, in Minnesota, for a week of recreation consisting of boating,
fishing and swimming.

Don Gackle and R.J. Jans reorganized troop 146, after World War II, operated
three or four years.

The present cubs and boy scouts have been organized about 4 1/2 years, with
membership in the cubs running about 13 to 17.  During these years, 10 cubs have
graduated to boy scout ranks.  The scout movement, in recent years, was mainly
through the efforts of Gilbert Holle, now superintendent of schools at Napolean,
Rueben Elhard, Harold Kjos, Reinholt Harr, Art Hollan, Oscar Konrad, Wayne Hieb,
Rev. Lawrence Pratt, now in Muskegon, Mich., and the den mothers.  Den Mothers,
over the 4 1/2 years, have been Mrs. Roland Jans, Mrs. Reinholt Harr, Mrs. Lyle
Bethke, Mrs. Richard Stadler, Mrs. Dale Brown, Mrs. Art Hollan, Mrs. Clifford
Carlson, Mrs. Arno Krueger and Mrs. Harold Wolf.

ROD AND GUN BUFFALO SUPPER

Kulm Rod and Gun Club served their First Annual Buffalo Supper at city
auditorium on November 19, 1958.  The fall events have brought many people to
Kulm each year, setting a record attendance in 1966.


KULM PUBLIC LIBRARY

Kulm Public Library closed November 5, 1958.  It had operated in the old
American Legion Hall with Mrs. Lydia Orth, librarian.


KULM ATHLETIC FIELD

A new lighted ball park was dedicated at Kulm, July 14, 1958, with Mayor Dan
Hoffman throwing out the first baseball.  The park has been used in the fall by
the high school football teams for home games and by the Independent baseball
team of Kulm during its regular season schedule each summer.



OUR SCHOOLS -

Probably the biggest single improvement in our Kulm School System came in early
1960 when, after state re-organization of school districts, Kulm found their
building falling short of requirements.  Bids were called early that year for a
new elementary school building and the construction of a shop addition to the
existing building.

Bids were awarded in June of 1960 with construction to start soon, so that fall
of 1961, pupils could be taught in the new building.

The new structure was dedicated on September 27, 1961, with G.B. Nordum, state
superintendent of public instruction the featured speaker.

The facilities were expected to be adequate for many years, yet today, in 1967,
thoughts are turning toward an addition to school buildings to take care of more
students.

Gilbert Holle, superintendent of Kulm Public School District at the time of the
1957 Jubilee, has moved to Napoleon, where he is superintendent, and he has been
replaced in Kulm by Owen Wallace, formerly high school principal.  Wallace
served a year at Mott before coming back to Kulm as superintendent.

[picture:  KULM ELEMENTARY SCHOOL]

[picture:  SCHOOL OFFICIALS.  High School Principal George Scauer; Board member
Edna Okerlund; Board member Lyonel Flegel; Board President Dennis Buchholz;

Lundgren and Art Franz.

BUSINESS CHANGES -

Time has passed since 1957, and some businesses have passed from the scene with
time.  Those that stayed have made many changes and improvements, keeping up
with the technological revolution on the farm, and in the home.  An updating of
businesses listed in the 1957 book and major changes and improvements, or
suspensions, are presented here for your information.


STANDARD SERVICE STATION

[picture]  What has long been known as John Stroehl's Standard Service Station
was sold by John to Boyd's Service in Jamestown during April of 1965, but before
Mr. Boyd took possession, it was purchased by Robert Buerkley, present owner.
Bob took over in June of 1965, and has remodeled the garage and station part,
adding an all-weather garage for his AAA and Standard Motor Club service truck.
John, who had been operating the station since 1932, (Refer to 1957 book), is
devoting full time to Stroehl Auto Wrecking.  Bill Delzer, referred to in the
1957 book, has long since discontinued the repair service.

Bob Buerkley has up to date equipment and methods to take care of your auto.
The part time staff consists of Marvin Ost and Larry Himmerich.  Julie Wolf is
bookkeeper.


KULM LUMBER

[picture]  Formerly Salzer Lumber Company, Harold Luck, the manager for Salzer,
bought the year during May of 1965.  (Refer to 1957 book).  The yard has a large
business of home and farm building contracting, building granaries and cattle
feeders.  James Lundgren is second man.


GEORGE'S ELECTRIC

[picture]  Ossie George does electrical contracting, and motor repair, at his
home in Kulm, working on various home, businesses, and governmental projects in
the Kulm area.  Recently, Mr. and Mrs. George petitioned for annexation to the
city and were annexed.  Oswald is presently a city councilman.


GUST REPAIR SHOP

[picture]  The A.A. Gunst Repair Shop still functions under the hand of Mr.
Gunst at the same location in Kulm.


KRAMLICH LOCKER SERVICE

[picture]  Reinhold Kramlich and family took over the locker plant in the A.J.
Smith Business Block, July 1, 1962, and since have served an excellent brand of
local sausage and all meat products available.  The Kramlichs had a large
slaughtering and meat cutting business on their farm west of Kulm, and moved
into the locker when Signar Lundgren and John Ruff stopped.  Current staff for
Kramlich's is Mr. and Mrs. Reinhold Kramlich, Mrs. Fred Grosz, Mrs. Herbert
Schlauch, and Donald Kramlich.

The Kramlichs do custom meat work, taking care of individuals who have their own
meat, as well as sell and process their meat.  In the fall of the year,
Kramlichs take care of processing for many of the deer shot in Kulm area during
hunting season.


SCHLAUCH PLBG. & HTG.

[picture]  Oscar Schlauch worked for Brauer Plumbing and Heating until March of
1963, when he formed his own firm, doing plumbing and heating repair and
installation work.


FARMERS CO-OPERATIVE LUMBER COMPANY

[picture]  The Co-op Lumber Co. became independent from the Farmers Co-
Operative Elevator Company in 1961, and has steadily improved their facilities
by building a new wood Quonset, for storing building materials, in 1963.  In
1966, further facilities were constructed in the form of an open- faced shed.
The company has purchased and uses a delivery truck for better service to
customers.

Managers of the company, since its separation from the elevator, have been Henry
soli, William Busch, and the present manager, Maynard Johnnes.  Allan Presler is
second man.

Gottlieb Hartman, Henry Miller, Edwin Malm, Walter Puhlman, Fred Vogel and Ted
Buerkle have served as directors of the company since 1961.  The present board
members are Denvy Malm, chairman of the board; Art Hollan, vice chairman; Lloyd
Hildebrand, secretary; William Staeck, Wilbert Kinzler, Oscar Geiszler and
Erland Blomquist directors.

[picture of family]  Maynard Johannes, son of the late Milton Johannes and Mrs.
R.C. Cleveland, was born in Underwood, October 10, 1925.  He was married to
Joyce Liedholm, Washbur, N. Dak., daughter of the late Mr. and Mrs. Arthur
Liedholm, on September 15, 1948.  Their children are Sally Ann 8 years old,
Joann Marie 12 years, Lyle Maynard 17, all at home, Michael Wade 19, is in the
U.S. Army, stationed at Fort Jackson, South Carolina.  Maynard Johannes has
worked for the Farmers Co-op Lumber Co. since April 1963.


C & H SERVICE

[picture]  Chris Kruse and Herbert Hilscher started C & H Service when Dan
Hoffman sold out the Kulm Super Service in September of 1961.  They stayed in
the Super Service building until May of 1963, when they moved to the Old Schott
Farm Implement Building, their present location.  In addition to Chris and Herb,
Leo Kalmback does mechanic work.  The boys are pretty busy, especially during
farm rush periods, with repair work, and during the repair season.  The boys do
excellent work.


PALM'S DEPARTMENT STORE

[picture]  Taking up one of the stores in the A.J. Smith Business Block (Refer
to 1957 book), Palm's is still under the managership of Dell and Annetta Palm.
Darlene Tiede works full time in the store and Mrs. J.D. Netz and Mrs. Allan
Presler help out part time.


GACKLE BROTHERS GRAIN COMPANY

[picture]  The Grain Company continued from 1957 under the managership of George
Gackle, one of the brothers, until September of 1965, when George passed on.
Since then, the company has hired Rueben Goehring as manager.  Rudolph Herman
has been a longtime employee of the firm.


GACKLE BROS. GARAGE

[picture]  Gackle Bros. Garage has changed little since the last Jubilee, except
for the new technology required to repair and service the modern farm
implements.  Robert Gackle is still in charge of the garage, with Hugo Schulz,
Herbert Johnson, Jacob Wolff and Albert Elhard doing repair work.


BORTH PRODUCE

[picture]  John Borth started for North American Creamery in 1948, and commenced
buying for Armours in Bismarck.  (Refer to 1957 book).  At the present time,
John buys for Gackle Creamery.  Changes in farm milking habits have left few
farmers selling mile, cream or eggs.  John now is cashier at the Fredonia Paying
and Receiving Station for Kulm State Bank, and the cream station is operated on
a part time basis.


CARLSON AND SJOSTROM

[picture]  Harley Carlson and Morris Sjostrom opened up their present implement
business at the north end of Main Street in February of 1959.  They acquired the
Case distributorship previously, and since have added New Holland, and many
other brands of implements.

During 1966, the partners added a new steel addition to the front of their
Quonset building, giving much new display space and more room for mechanical
work and parts.

Presently, hauling, repair and yard work, at Carlson and Sjostrom, is Harold
Podoll and Veryle Bjur.


ART'S SUPER VALU

[picture]  Still occupying a part of the A.J. Smith Business Block is Art
Krein's Super Valu Store.  Mr. and Mrs. Krein have been in continuous business
at the location since 1948.  Their present staff, in addition to Mr. and Mrs.
Krein, are: Hattie Krueger, Mrs. Robert Keller and Jerry Elhard.


STANDARD OIL BULK

[picture]  Cleon Hoggarth bought the bulk business from Gottlieb Schock in 1960,
and services Standard outlets in town as well as direct bulk sales to homes and
farms.  In 1966, Cleon began selling and applying weed sprays for cattle, weeds,
and trees.  He has a 4-wheel drive mounted weed sprayer and applies the products
as well as sells them.


SOO LINE

[picture]  One of Kulm's old landmarks, the Soo Line Depot, still stands along
the right-of-way through the city, with Harry Nelson continuing as agent.
Bernard Rasch, assistant section fireman and Clarence Hehr live in Kulm and are
employed by the Soo Line.

Modern paved roads have diminished the way freight volume and many stations,
smaller than Kulm, have been closed by the Soo.  But a large volume of grain is
shipped by rail and freight service is still almost daily.

Passenger service east and west was halted in July of 1964, bringing to an end
the days that old timers talk of, when they rode the Soo to Minneapolis with
cattle, for medical care, vacation or what not.


FRED STROBEL DRAYAGE

[picture]  Nowadays in Kulm, when drayage is necessary, we call on Fred Strobel.
Fred has trucks to do local hauling and handles coal for the city, and way
freight that comes in by Soo Line.  Other hauling jobs are handled for
individuals and businesses in town by Fred.  Fred also sells insurance.  He has
been handling freight since 1962.


FARMERS CO-OPERATIVE ELEVATOR CO.

[picture; refer to 1957 book]  Farmers Co-Operative Elevator of Kulm has long
been progressive and made regular improvements in their buildings and service as
the 1957 Jubilee book attests.  Since 1957, seeing the need again for more
storage, the stockholders voted to build a 100,000 bushel annex in 1958.  Then,
in 1960, the grain co-op was separated from the lumber co-op to protect the tax
status of the grain co.

March 31, 1964, the Farmers Elevator celebrated its fiftieth anniversary.  This
was attended by 400 stockholders and friends.  Pres. Hartman introduced Emil
Loriks, Pres. Of G.T.A. board of directors, who presented the elevator with a
plaque in recognition of a half century of cooperative building and service by
farmers to their community.  Ed. Smith, Pres. of North Dakota Farmers Union,
spoke and congratulated patrons, managers, and board of directors for 50 years
of successful operation and growth.  Many bouquets of flowers were received from
local business establishments for this occasion.

This organization started in 1904 with only a few stockholders, who bought an
elevator for $8,000, has grown to 563 stockholders, who have $180,950.00 in
local earnings, and received $102,842.50 in cash dividends and stock retirement
for the period of January, up to 1967.  The total sales for and to patrons
amounted to $1,154,896.97 and handled 909,000 bushels of grain for the year of
1966.

The elevator started shipping part of their grain to G.T.A., but, in 1945, the
directors voted to ship 100% of them for which the stockholders have received an
additional $123,050.00 in stock credits.

Albert Buerkle has been employed by the elevator since 1939, and has been
manager for the last 16 years.  Albert Hehr is assistant manager, and Glen
Goehring as third man.

The present board consists of Gottlieb Hartman, president; Herman Hermann, vice
president; Edwin Malm, secretary; Henry Miller, Lawrence Vogel, Clayton
Zachrison, and William Kleingartner directors.

The board of directors feel this organization has been a great asset to this
community and to the city of Kulm.


AUCTIONEERS

[picture]  Early auctioneers in Kulm were Fred and Martin Gackle.  At the
present time, Harold Fregien, Albert Heidinger and D.J. Harter are licensed to
conduct sales.


GACKLE BROS., INC.

[picture]  Gackle Bros., Inc. has continued on since 1957, selling hardware,
furniture, automobiles and implements.  (Refer to 1957 book).  Since 1957,
active management of the store has been in the hands of Ernest Gackle and Robert
Gackle.  Ernest is the surviving brother of the Gackle family and Robert is the
son of Otto Gackle.

Martin W. Gackle died in September, 1958; Otto in June of 1958; and George in
September of 1965.  John and Fred had died previous to 1957.

The store affiliated with Gamble-Skogmo in April of 1961, bringing the chain's
lines of hardware and furniture into Kulm.

The present staff at Gackle Bros. Includes Ernest and Bob, plus Russell Gackle,
LeRoy Gackle, Harold Hettich, Mrs. Fred Strobel and Mrs. Alice Gackle.


HOFFER GARAGE

[picture]  Oscar Hoffer opened his own garage in Kulm after the Kulm Mill
Company burned down in the spring of 1963.  Oscar had worked for the Mill
Company and Brost Bros. In recent years.

Mechanical work is his specialty, and "Dingle" makes some pretty good ice augers
for winter fishing.


KULM CREDIT UNION

[picture:  Manager-Treasurer Irene Hehr, President Fred Vogel, Director Eugene
Anderson, Director and Secretary Walter Kusler, Directors William Staeck and
Edwin Lundgren.]

The Kulm Credit Union was organized March 23, 1950.  (Refer to 1957 book).  It
is a self-help financial organization, owned and operated by its members, for
its members for their savings and loans.  The Kulm Credit Union has grown to a
membership of 686 and total assets of $481.915.52.

The present Board of Directors are: Fred Vogel, president; William Staeck, vice
president; Walter Kusler, secretary; Edwin Lundgren and Eugene Anderson,
directors; Mrs. Albert Hehr, treasurer; supervisory committee: Lee Hartman, Lyle
Bethke, and Oscar Giesler; credit committee: LeRoy Olson, Herman Herrmann, and
Reuben Hehr; educational committee: Mrs. Clayton Zachrison, Mrs. G.G. Herman,
Mrs. James Herman, Ronald Brost, and Wilbert Kinzler.


DOCTORS

[picture]  Dr. J.E. Grenz, dentist in Kulm at the time of the 1957 Jubilee,
still serves Kulm and surrounding area, living here with Mrs. Grenz.  His son
Lester worked for the Kulm Messenger and has since become apprenticed at the
Grand Forks Harold, and now works there as a journeyman printer.  (Refer to 1957
book.  Dr. Aarma no longer serves Kulm.)

Dr. Cameron ended his service in Kulm in the early months of 1958.  Dr. Kenneth
Harris, Gackle, served Kulm also for a short time in 1958.  In September of
1960, Dr. Eugene Bolliger came to Kulm and served until early 1964.  Kulm was
without a medical doctor until July, 1964, when Dr. Allen Hill came.

Mrs. Anna Bjornson is the present secretary and treasurer of the Health Center
Board and has been since M.W. Gunter resigned in 1959.


NELSON RADIO AND TV

[picture]  Albert Nelson's radio and television shop is still in business, doing
radio and t.v. repair and service.  See 1957 book.


SWEDE TOWNSHIP TELEPHONE COMPANY

[picture:  Victor Ogren, Denvy Malm, Erland Blomquist, Lester Berntson, Edwin
Malm.]

The Swede Township Telephone Company was organized October 1st, 1908.
Incorporated and granted Charter on April 7th, 1909, with G.H. Davidson, Rudolph
Ogren, Jacob Bjur and J.A. Tjernlund as the original board of directors.

It has furnished rural telephone service to Swede Township and adjacent area
continuously since that date.  Present board of directors are: D.C. Malm,
president; Victor Ogren, vice president; Erland Blomquist, secretary- treasurer;
and Edwin Malm and Lester Bertnson, directors.


KULM CAFE

[picture]  Jim and Hermoine Weatherly operated the cafe in the brick annex to
the Anderson store, which had been used as a restaurant for a number of years.
In October of 1961, they opened in the new building they now occupy, with first
class restaurant services and a fine banquet room used for meetings, family
occasion suppers, organization banquets, etc.

On the staff at the cafe are Mrs. Amelia Speidel and daughter Loretta, Mrs.
Evelyn (Marvin) Ost, Mrs. Annetta Hauff, Mrs. Eleanore Buerkley, and Mrs.
Marlene Schuett.

The old cafe building is vacant.  Mrs. Weatherly does a fine job of catering
large groups, and served the 25th anniversary banquet for the Lions Club a fine
meal.  There were approximately 170 present.


KULM STATE BANK

[picture]  Kulm State Bank is noting its 60th year of business in 1967, and has
an unblemished record of never closing, except for normal closing hours and the
forced bank holidays in 1932, decreed by President Roosevelt.  (Refer to 1957
book).

The 60th year has marked a few milestones, notably, at times during the past
year, footings (assets) reached the $3,000,000 mark; and closing assets were
$125,000 over last year.

Mr. William Jans officially retired from the bank in January, 1957, but remains
as chairman of the board of directors, with Sharpe Pruetz, president; M.C.
Buechler, vice president; R.L. Jans, cashier; Wayne M. Hieb, cashier; and John
Borth, assistant cashier in charge of the Paying and Receiving Station in
Fredonia.  Marcella Ogren and Mrs. Robert Buerkley are cashier employees of the
bank at the present time.


CIVIC & IMPROVEMENT ASSOCIATION

[picture]  Still in their building that was relatively new at the time of the
1957 Jubilee (Refer to old book), the Civic and Improvement Association operates
the only liquor store in town.  The association contributed a great deal towards
getting the rural fire truck and fire hall, and in more recent years wrote a
check out for an $11,000 reseal of the city's paved streets.

Present board members are: Wilbert Konrad, Robert Gackle, LeRoy Gackle, Harold
Hettich, William Brauer, and Wayne Hieb.

Tending bar are Sam Henke and Ralph Widmer, with a part time assist of harold
Wolff.

Otto Gackle was secretary of the board for many years, and, after his death, Ed
Hieb filled the position.  Now Wilbert Konrad is secretary.


BROST & SON, INC.

[picture]  More commonly known as Brost Bros. Until this spring when papers were
signed to transfer ownership to Wilbert Brost and son Frederick.  Hilmer Brost
bought Mason Chevrolet in Crookston, Minnesota, taking over there June 1.
(Refer to 1957 book).

Brost and Son is now under the second and third generation of management in
Kulm.  D.F. Brost passed away shortly after the 1957 Jubilee, in August of 1957,
and the brothers Wilbert and Hilmer guided the firm until this spring.

On the staff at Brost and Son, Inc., is Wilbert and Fred Brost, Nora Gackle, Ray
Garman, Ray Pillaert, Clifford Hildebrand, Arno Krueger, Ervin Gackle, Ernest
Rost, Alice Hehr, and Roger Kinzler.


NORTHWESTERN BELL TELEPHONE CO.

W.A. Anderson manages the Bell System for Kulm.  The dial system, installed in
1954, is now part of the Direct Distance Dialing system of the telephone
company.  While equipment isn't installed allowing Kulmites to dial out
automatically, relatives and friends in other parts of the country can dial
directly into Kulm from their home phone without contacting operator.  The 7-
digit numbering system is preparatory to DDD service, but the necessary billing
information equipment is not installed.

[Picture:  (1) The first telephone, invented by Alexander Graham Bell in 1876.
(2) First commercial telephone went into service in 1877; round opening served
as transmitter and receiver.  (3) Magne to wall set, in which you turned the
crank to reach the operator, made its appearance in 1882, was used for many
years.  (4) First dial "desk" telephone was designed in 1898.  (5) This dial
phone was introduced in 1919.  (6) People began using this hand-set model in
1925.  (7) Desk set, introduced in 1949, still is in popular use today.  (8)
Wall model put into service in 1956.  During the 1950's, the telephone industry
began marketing color phones.  (9) A 1967 model of the push button instrument.]


DICKEY RURAL TELEPHONE MUTUAL AID CORPORATION

Robert Hasse is lineman for the rural telephone company stationed in Kulm.  The
country phone lines serve a lot of folks on the farms in this country and,
thought subject to weather, these phones are appreciated a great deal.


MONTANA DAKOTA UTILITIES CO.

[picture]  Montana-Dakota Utilities still serves Kulm with power and has Art
Okerlund stationed here, a position he has filled for 14 years.  In September of
1962, the city contracted for new modern street lights that have spruced up the
business district quite a bit.  This spring, the city renewed Montana- Dakota's
franchise.


NICKISCH FUNERAL HOME

[picture]  In January 1960, the Nickisch Funeral Home of Wishek bought the Old
Assembly of God Church and remodeled it for their Kulm funeral home.  Fred
Schlenker is in charge of arrangements for the Nickisch Brothers.  Fred also is
an insurance agent and is a distributor for a motor oil company.  See 1957 book
for Fred's history.


KULM DRUG

[picture]  Everett Brovold, pharmacist at Kulm Drug, left in 1958, and, at the
present time, Mary Steinwandt, Annie LaBrensz and Mrs. Buchholz work in the
store.  Manager and pharmacist is Dennis Buchholz.


BEN'S BARBER SHOP

[picture]  Ben is still cutting hair and using the shop as his office.  (see
1957 book)  During some remodeling of the false front in recent years, a bullet
was dug from the old wood of the building.  It possibly hit the building during
the gun fight after the discovery of the burglars in the old bank building.  Ben
is an independent insurance agent for all types: auto, home, farm, life and
hospitalization.  Ben also buys and sells real estate and handles rental
property.


FARMERS UNION OIL COMPANY

[picture]  Farmers Union Oil Company is operating with the same manager as in
1957 (Refer to 1957 book), Irvin E. Mogck, but additions and new lines have been
added.  Their latest addition, next to the Hettich Shoe Store, was built in
1964, giving more room for offices and a separate office for the Credit Union.
In the fall of 1965, a fertilizer warehouse was built, giving bulk fertilizer
delivery right into farm trucks or wagons.

Station attendants are Reuben Hehr, Bernard Fandrich, and Lyle Scott; Gordon
Irion operates the bulk truck; LeRoy Kramlich has charge of the fertilizer
warehouse; and bookkeepers are Mrs. Erwin Gackle and Mrs. Irvin Mogck.


GACKLE GROCERY

[picture]  Owned by Barbara and Henry Gackle, the grocery was formerly a Red Owl
Agency.  Now the Gackles buy independently many quality products that grace Kulm
tables.


STADLER HOTEL

[picture]  Mr. and Mrs. Richard Stadler operate the Stadler Hotel, having
transient rooms and apartments for housekeeping.  Many teachers stay at the
hotel.  See 1957 book.


ISAAK ELEVATOR

[picture]  Isaak Elevator, under the hand of Harry Isaak, has improved his
services to farmers in the seed cleaning and processing line, and selling
certified farm seeds.  They no longer buy grain and ship.  A new seed cleaning
machine was installed in recent years.

Current staff at the elevator are Victor Billigmeier and Richard Hauff.


ERNST RECREATION PARLOR

[picture]  This present Kulm business was part of the Anderson Store until 1959,
when Albert Ernest started his recreation parlor in the corner building.  Albert
has pool tables, cards and light refreshments.

Born on a farm near Kulm in 1904, Albert is married to the former Marie Haase of
Merricourt.  They started farming in 1925 on the Martin Haase homestead.


THE KULM MESSENGER

[picture]  The Kulm Messenger, local newspaper, was purchased by the present
owners, Mr. and Mrs. John Peters, in June of 1962.

(Refer to 1957 book)  In July of 1963, Mr. Peters bought The Edgeley Mail and
printing of both papers were merged in the Kulm plant, though they remain
separate newspapers.  In October of 1964, The Kulm Messenger converted a twice-
monthly shopper in Gackle to a weekly newspaper, with mechanical work integrated
with the other two newspapers at Kulm.

Since that time, a monthly shopper is printed for Edgeley businessman, and a
monthly North Dakota Wildlife publication "Flickertales" is printed in the Kulm
print shop.  An ever expanding variety of commercial printing is produced for
local businesses, cities, county, and state government.

The present staff included Mr. and Mrs. Peters; Edna Okerlund, local
correspondent, bookkeeper and mechanical employee; Robert Zimmerman (Wishek);
Arva Dell Kinzler; Linda Buchholz.

In recent years, a great deal of research was done on North Dakota newspapers by
Carol Koehmstedt in a University of Minnesota thesis, the information that Kulm
has had 5 newspapers was unearthed.

A weekly known as the Kulm Standard was published in 1894 and 1895, other years
of existence lost to history.  The paper was operated by the Kulm Publishing Co.

The predecessor to The Kulm Messenger, the North Dakota Wind, was established in
August of 1894.  The only known copy of the North Dakota Wind is on display at
Kulm State Bank.  Volume 1, Number 1 of The Kulm Messenger appeared November 18,
1897, which was Volume 3, Number 15 of the Wind, and listed as successor to the
Wind.  A copy of the first issue is at the Historical Society in Bismarck.

A weekly paper knows as the Kulm Republican, edited by G.W. DuFrane, was in
existence here in 1904, and a German language paper called Kulm Botschafter,
edited by H.L. Kelly, published here in about 1898-99.

The following men have served Kulm as editors of The Kulm Messenger: H.L. Kelly
1897-1902; J.E. Cobb and J.W. Bingham also served during this period; George W.
DuFrane 1903; A.B. Malin 1904-05; Malin and Parker 1905-1907; Jes P. Parker
1907-1910; Oramel A. Hoskins 1910-1914; Petersen Bros. 1915-1916; Harriet E.
Semple, short period in 1917; C.T. Bolstad 1917-1920; Edwin T. Brost, two weeks
in November 1920; J.Come, November and December 1920; Edwin T. Brost early 1921;
C.C. Lowe 1921-1923; Vincent C. Lewis 1923-1927; L.H. Brown 1927-1933; Harold G.
Lewis 1933-1939; L.G. Hixon 1939-1945; Florence Hixon short period 1945;
Clarence Schuldheisz 1945-1951; Edwin T. Brost 1951- 1954; Nick J. Novakovich
1954-1962; John E. Peters 1962 on.


RECREATION PARLOR  (NOW HOME LUNCHES)

[picture]  Grosz Recreation Parlor in 1957 was converted to a restaurant in
December of 1959, and, for a number of years, Tina cooked some wonderful meals
for the public.  In later years, it has been operated as a lunch cafe and
recreation parlor.  (Refer to 1957 book)


BILL'S BARBER SHOP

[picture]  William Wolf lives and works in the main street building much as he
did in 1957.  For a short period, Arnie Buerkle operated the barber shop for
Bill.  See 1957 book.


KULM BOWLING LANES

[picture]  The bowling alley, under ownership of Reinhold Beich, is still open
for league bowling.  Mr. Beich manages the alley himself nowadays.  Automatic
pin-setters were installed in October of 1959.


STROEHL AUTO WRECKING

John Stroehl, after turning over the keys to the Standard Service Station that
he operated from 1932 (Refer to 1957 book), made a full time occupation of his
auto wrecking business.  Since 1965, he has devoted his time to salvaging parts
for cars and farm machines, sometimes badly needed because of delays in getting
parts orders or discontinuance of manufacture.

John has moved a building to his yard where he houses his large collection of
stuffed animals and birds that used to grace the walls of the station.  Many
strangers to Kulm have marvelled at the mounted specie in the station over the
years, and now the mountings are displayed for all to see.

John came to Kulm in 1928 from Lidgerwood.  For two years, he sold cars for the
Kipp Brothers in Kulm, then took over the Standard Service Station.  Mrs.
Stroehl is from Hankinson and the couple were married in Kulm in 1936.


BUSINESS NOTES FROM NEWSPAPER FILES:

1905

On August 3, 1905, Charles Lindor of Mandan, N. Dak. and Emil Anderson of Kulm
announced the opening of the Ladies and Gentlemen's GOODS, boots, and shoes,
also GROCERIES at the Meurling building.  Mr. and Mrs. G.F. Meurling, former
owners moved to Worthington, Min..

On August 3, 1905, the firm of JENNER AND MOGCK DRAY LINE dissolved partnership.
Christ Mogck bought Mr. Mogck's interest.

On August 17, 1905, Gottfried Johanson of Forman rented the Zellmar building to
open up a first class BAKERY.

On October 12, 1905, J.P. Parker, who has been the editor of the DICKEY
REPORTER, became the business manager and associate editor of THE KULM
MESSENGER.  A.B. Malin is editor.

J.H.C. Michaels, who has had active management of the KULM CREAMERY for the past
two years, has resigned his position.  He will leave for New Salem where he will
be employed in a creamery.  Otto Groh assumed the business management.  This
took place on October 12, 1905.

On November 16, 1905, J.C. Nippolt purchased the MEAT MARKET, formerly owned by
George Renscheller.

On November 16, 1905, Gottlieb Miller bought hotel furnishings from F.F. Hixon
and has assumed management of the REAL ESTATE HOTEL property.  Gottlieb worked
at the Union Hotel for a year.  Mr. Hixon will go to Forbes where he will engage
in the livery business.

On November 16, 1905, J.F. Snyder moved his stock of DRUGS into his building.

On November 16, 1905, A.B. Malin and THE KULM MESSENGER moved into their new
building.

On November 16, 1905, the FIRST STATE BANK moved into its new building.

On November 16, 1905, J.A. Peterson constructed a new building on his lot to be
used as a MEAT MARKET.

On November 23, 1905, J. Smith of Monango purchased the RESTAURANT,
CONFECTIONERY of Otto Becker and moved his family here and assumed control of
the business.  Mr. Smith had been engaged in the hotel business in Monango.

1906

About January 11, 1906, Gottfried Johnson's new RESTAURANT AND BAKERY opened for
the first time.

About January 11, 1906, John A. Stolt became the owner of the STORE AND GENERAL
MERCHANDISE business of Schuldheisz Brothers.

On February 15, 1906, Bender, Broneske and Klettke became the new proprietors of
the CITY DRAY AND TRANSFER LINE.

On February 8, 1906, Stuhr and Mackin assumed management of the MEAT MARKET
formerly owned by J.C. Nippolt who will move to Wishek and manage the hotel.

On April 5, 1906, W.T. Stolt took charge of the FARMERS ELEVATOR.

On April 5, 1906, W.W. Douthit and Andrew Dok formed a partnership in the
CONTRACTING AND BUILDING BUSINESS.

On April 13, 1906, C. Flegel purchased a half interest in G.J. Dobler's GRAIN
ELEVATOR.

On May 2, 1906, Jacob Nacht and Son opened up their new store in the building
south of Brauer and Schneider's blacksmith shop.  They had a complete stock of
DRY GOODS, hats, some clothing and goods.

On May 10, 1906, C. Hieb, Chas. Lindor and Emil Anderson purchased the building
and hardware and furniture stock of Brost Bros.  Brost Bros. Retained the farm
machinery and implements, buggies, wagons, pumps, windmills, wire, fencing
material, etc.  The store was called HIEB, LINDOR, AND ANDERSON.

On May 31, 1906, J.M. Kelly of New Hampton, Iowa and A.B. Malin purchased the
LIVERY BARN and lots, buggies, horses, harness and utensils used in the barn
from the KLAUDT-KNECHT LIVERY CO.

On June 21, G.J. Dobler, C. Flegel and F. Flegel purchased the KULM ROLLER MILL
from O.W. Kerr Co., of Fargo.

On August 30, 1906, Kelly and Malin bought the LIVERY BARN from John Kosanke and
Tom Brady.

1907

On January 3, Charles Lindor, of the firm Hieb, Lindor and Anderson, sold his
interest in the business.

On January 10, 1907, David Sperling and Carl Johnson purchased the DRAY LINE
business which has been conducted by Elstad and Steen.

On January 31, 1907, Christ and Fred Flegel purchased the interest of their
partner, G.J. Dobler.

On February 7, 1907, Dr. F.G. Benn and A.P. Gordon formed a partnership and
engaged in the DRUG business, having rented the building formerly occupied by
SNYDER'S PHARMACY.

On February 21, 1907, Chas. Lindor opened a new GROCERY STORE.

On February 21, 1907, E.O. Gordon sold his BARBER SHOP AND POOL ROOM business to
Geo. C. Sempel.

On June 7, 1907, Jacob Motz formed a partnership with Chas. Lindor in the
GENERAL MERCHANDISE business.

On November 7, 1907, A.B. Malin sold his interest of the Kulm-Edgeley STAGE LINE
to Gottfried Klaudt.

1908

On January 9, 1908, A.D. Gordon sold his interest in the DRUG STORE to his
partner Dr. F.G. Benn.

On February 13, 1908, Fred M. Brost and Mathesis Dobler formed a partnership and
conducted the business formerly conducted by Brost Bros.

1909

KELLY-MURRAY CO'S STOCK of Kulm sold out their stock of dry goods, clothes,
hardware, etc., on January 14, 1909

A.B. Malin sold his LIVERY BARN and horses, buggies, etc., on March 22, 1909.

KULM CREAMERY CO. sold out in April, 1909.

C. Hieb sold the firm of HIEB AND ANDERSON to Oscar E. Anderson.  The firm will
now be known as ANDERSON BROS.

GACKLE AND CO. purchased the farm emplement business and buildings of F.M. Brost
in Kulm and Fredonia.

1910

JOHN GROSZ AND SONS sold out to GROSZ BROS. In 1910

G.J. DOBLER AND CO. sold out in 1910.

1911

NO CHANGES OF CONSEQUENCE.

NO CHANGES OF CONSEQUENCE.

Geiszler on October 6, 1913

1914

A.R. Riese resigned his position as agent for the SALZER LUMBER CO. and has
formed a partnership with J.A. Melicher, who owns the firm known as J.A.
MELICHER AND SONS, dealing in farm implements, harness, saddlery, and autos, on
January 1, 1914.

The NORTH STAR LUMBER CO. changed their name to WISCONSIN VALEY LUMBER CO.
around May 21, 1914.

R.J. Sherman, a graduate attorney, will do business under the firm name of
BURFENING AND SHERMAN.  He started around August 20, 1914.

J.A. Berg became the proprietor of the PETERSON RESTAURANT on October 1, 1914.

Joe Brady of Edgeley purchased the POOL HALL from T.E. Strickler on March 30,
1915.

Christ Dobler accepted the position as BARBER around April 8, 1915.  He took
Will Wolff's place.

Mr. J.A. Peterson sold his interest in the local MEAT MARKET to William Lange
around June 4, 1915.

Gottlieb Wendland purchased the WIRCH STORE, located about 18 miles south of
Kulm, around June 18, 1915.

1916

O.O. Groh purchased the MEAT MARKET from William Lange around August 10, 1916.

E.W. and A.G. Peterson, editors and publishers of THE KULM MESSENGER for the
past 2 years, left.

1917

1918

WARTIME, NO CHANGES OF CONSEQUENCE.

Gackle Bros. Purchased the GROSZ STORE building around January 10, 1919.

Around February 21, 1919, R.A. Schrieber took over the position of special agent
for the SALZER LUMBER CO.  Jack Haffner remained as an assistant.

Hugo Ogren took possession of the PETERSON RESTAURANT on July 16, 1919.

1920

Mrs. Mary Renz bought the PETERSON RESTAURANT on January 27, 1920.

1921

Edwin T. Brost is the editor and manager of THE KULM MESSENGER.

C.C. Lowe became the new editor of THE KULM MESSENGER about May 6, 1921.

C.L. Kenis, of Artas, purchased the stock of goods formerly owned by J. Buchman,

1922

NO CHANGES OF CONSEQUENCE.

C.C. Lowe, publisher of THE KULM MESSENGER, sold THE KULM MESSENGER to Vincent
C. Lewis on May 23, 1923.

Adolph Spitzer and Jacob Straub are joint owners of a MEAT MARKET.

1924

On September 18, 1924, M.E. Kipp leased the largest garage in Kulm, formerly
owned by a corporation known as the SERVICE MOTOR CO.

1925

On February 26, 1925, John Isaak bought the GIFT SHOP stock from J.E. Haag.

On May 1, 1925, T.W. Swanke bought the MOVIES, purchasing same from Vincent C.
Lewis.

On June 27, 1925, J.A. Melicher and sons new GARAGE has one of the best display
rooms in the Northwest.  The Melicher firm wholesales and retails Ford repair
parts, also repairs and stores cars.

On December 24, 1925, F.W. Swanke sold the MOVIE THEATER to J.L. Schlenker.

1926

On February 18, 1926, John Isler started a new RESTAURANT in the former Jake
Haag building.

On March 4, 1926, a new CREAM STATION was opened in the old Anderson building
with Mike Borth as manager.

On March 11, 1926, John Isaak bought the building occupied by THE KULM
MESSENGER.  The Kulm Messenger bought the Jake Haag building, which had been
occupied by John Isler.

On September 13, 1926, C.B. Mogck purchased PRUETZ BROTHERS ELEVATOR.

1927

On April 25, 1927, V.C. Lewis sold THE KULM MESSENGER to L.H. Brown.

On November 10, 1927, Mr. John Schuldheisz became the manager of the FARMERS
STORE, formerly managed by Mr. larson.  Mr. Schuldheisz was one of the founders
of the PEOPLES STORE.

1928

On May 3, 1928, Melvin Juberg became HOME OIL COMPANY agent.  The position was
recently vacated by Carl Weist.

On October 4, 1928, Abbey Kempf opened a PHOTOGRAPH STUDIO.

A.G. Porter closes LAW OFFICE in September and moves to Ellendale.

[Years 1929-1932 not identified]

Week of September 26, William Isaak and Theo. Schorzman bought the Lay
RESTAURANT from Mr. and Mrs. Max Lay.  The name was the STAR CAFE.

Jacob Straub bought the CITY MEAT MARKET from Borth and Kosanke in the 1st part
of April.

W.J. Austin, son of Judge Austin of Ellendale, will locate in Kulm, where he
will open a LAW OFFICE in the very near future.

February 22, John Stroehl and Louie Kemnetz of Kulm bought the PICTURE STUDIO
owned by A.R. Kempf.  They will wreak the present building and convert it into a
STANDARD OIL STATION.

1933

A deal was made in the last week of January, 1933, that Kulm will have a store
affiliated with the famous SERVICE SYSTEM line of stores.

The KULM DRUG STORE will be moved to a new location, to north side of Gackle
Brothers Store.

In February, Eddie Bjur became new DEEP ROCK agent in Kulm.  Earl Bradley was
the former agent.

On April 20, the deal was made that H.G. Lewis, Ellendale, purchases THE KULM
MESSENGER, published last 5 years by L.H. Brown.

A BAKERY AND CREAMERY was opened in Kulm by Clarence Krogstad and N.S. Diehl,
June 26.

March 14, the BANK went into new hands.  Wm. Jans is cashier and Sharpe Pruetz
as assistant cashier.

W.L. Isensee rented the building vacated by Kulm Hardware Co., and opened for
business in April.  He will open for business with a large stock of USED CARS.

April 29, Leo Reister bought BUCK'S CAFE.  He renamed it HAMBURGER INN.

December 10, the new CAFE, owned by Otto Elhard, will open.  He rented the
Spitzer building on main street for this.

[Years 1934-1938 not identified]

RED OWL STORE, owned by Bert Grenz, was sold to E.M. Graf of Linton, who took
possession on June 26.

During the week of July 20, Art Palm bought the old FARMERS STORE.  It will be
redecorated and moved on part of the ground floor and part will be a LIQUOR
STORE and the other part a CAFE.

1939

June, 1939, L.G. Hixon was editor and owner of THE KULM MESSENGER.

October, 1939, Art Palm owns the LIQUOR STORE.

[Years 1940-1941 not identified]

July, John Zimmerman operates the LIQUOR STORE.  He purchased some of the
equipment from Art Palm.

About November 7, Edwin Heck has taken of the FAIRMONT CREAMERY formerly run by
Woodrow Edstrom.

About December 12, Richard Heidinger is the new MAIL CARRIER.  Fred Gackle was
the previous carrier.

June 26 edition, Orion Rath takes over the management of the ROXY THEATRE.
Russell Riese was the former manager.

Dr. J.E. Grenz took over the DENTAL PRACTICE of Dr. E.O. Fergusson on September
19.

1942

THE KULM MESSENGER moved to a new office on November 5, 1942.

1943

John D. Netz purchased the stock of the GAMBLE STORE formerly owned by Beich, on
January 7.

William Wolff leased the BARBER SHOP from Biech on August 1.

Henry Gackle started running the CENTRAL CAFE, formerly operated by Mr. and Mrs.
John J. Gackle, on October 28.

Hovember 11, 1943, fire broke out in the PEOPLE'S STORE.  The blaze broke out on
the second floor of the building and it was believed that it originated from
defective wiring.  There was heavy damage to the storage room.  A.J. Smith,
owner, has made no estimate of damage at this time.  He opened for business the
next day.

December 17, 1943, thieves entered the LIQUOR STORE by breaking a window.  Ten
cases of whisky were taken from the store room and 6 cases from the shelves,
which was valued at $1015.  No insurance was carred by Mr. Zimmerman, owner.

1944

Edna Ost closed her BEAUTY SHOP March 2.

1946

Mr. and Mrs. Thomas Buerkley became the new owners of the CENTRAL CAFE, which
was recently operated by Henry Gackle.

Mr. and Mrs. Ed. J. Hehr, former local residents, recently purchased a grocery
store in Frazee, Minnesota.

Jacob Mill resigned as CHIEF OF POLICE in Kulm.  Otto Rutschke has been
appointed to fill the unexpired term of Mr. Nill.

About January 3, Mrs. John J. Gackle took possession of the BUECHLER STORE for 1
year, while C.C. Buechlers went on a vacation.  She is starting a RED OWL FOOD
STORE.

1947

October 2, Clarence Schuldheisz is the publisher of THE KULM MESSENGER.

1948

About January 29, Henry Gackle and Lawrence Koenig purchased the BUECHLER STORE
building.  The former conductors were Mr. and Mrs. John J. Gackle.

About January 29, Bill Remien purchased the fixtures in the Mr. Henry Gackle and
Mr. Lawrence Koenig RECREATION PARLOR.

About January 29, a GIFT SHOP was opened by Mrs. John R. Zimmerman.

1949

Week of October 6, W.E. Brost and H.A. Brost announced that they started a
business to sell CHEVROLET cars and trucks.

About November, BUS SERVICE was started (run) between Kulm and Fargo.

1950

January 5, Dan Hoffman bought the SUPER SERVICE STATION.

April 15, Dr. Fergusson is leaving Kulm.

About March30, Walter Kramlich purchased the interests of Ed. LaBrensz.  He is
now the new BEAVER OIL CO. agent.

1953

September 17, Edwin T. Brost became the publisher of THE KULM MESSENGER.

1954

About February 25, Mr. and Mrs. Jake Steinwandt open a new business place in the
former RADIO SHOP.  It's called JAKE'S COFFEE SHOP AND DAILY BAR.

April 1, Nick J. Novakovich takes over as publisher of THE KULM MESSENGER from
the former Edwin T. Brost.

July 26, Dr. Steidl will open the DOCTOR'S OFFICE again in Kulm.

August 6, the BJORNSON DRUG STORE, operated by Mrs. Anna Bjornson, was purchased
by Milo Knudsen and Everett Brovold.  The new name, THE KULM DRUG.

1955

On November 7, the City Council, at their regular meeting, agreed to make
provision in next year's budget for a PUBLIC LIBRARY.

1956

The week of March 15, 1956, Manager Reinhold Beich , of the KULM THEATRE,
announced the closing of the theatre, after 10 continuous years of operation
under his management.

March 21, 1956, The former LIQUOR STORE was moved out of Kulm, after the
building of the new liquor store was completed.

About June 14, W.J. Hildebrand, after working in the meat and grocery business
for 22 years, sold his LOCKER PLANT to Helmuth Herman.  Herman plans to call his
business E AND H FOOD CENTER.

Adam Wagner bought the KULM CAFE from Jim Watherly in the latter part of July,
1956.

About October 11, Kulm's main street was finally paved.

Preceding week of October 18, John L. Schuldheisz purchased the ANDERSON STORE
from Emil Anderson.

1957

Week of January 3, Mr. and Mrs. Jim Weatherly purchased the KULM CAFE from Adam
Wagner.

Preceding week of July 25, work began on the rebuilding of the Federal Aid road
east of Kulm, all the way between 56 and 281.

Congregationalists build new PARSONAGE in December, 1957.

1958

May 5, 1958, it was voted that Oscar Konrad replace Fred Schlenker as CHIEF OF
POLICE and John Fischer replace John Borth as the WATER COMMISSIONER.

A big burglary occurred at E & H and FARMERS UNION OIL STATION on May 15.

Harry L. Nelson was named president of Lions Club on May 20, 1958.

Dan Schuldheisz retires after 35 years of RURAL MAIL CARRIER, in August.

Mr. and Mrs. Helmuth Herrmann, proprietors of the E & H FOOD CENTER, announced
in the latter part of September, 1958, that they are selling their groceries
with a 5% discount on all items.  The LOCKER PLANT will stay under the
management of Robert Herman.

1959

The E & H FOOD CENTER will undergo another name change.  The store has now been
sold to Mr. and Mrs. Milton Lindgren.  It will now be called the E & M GROCERY.

Harold Luck was named president of the Lions Club on May 19, 1959.

First worship services were held in the new Assembly of God Church on October
22, 1959.

1960

New FUNERAL HOME plans were made by Elmo Nickisch of Wishek and his 2 sons,
Willard and Seven, in January.  The old Assembly of God Church was bought by Mr.
Nickisch for this purpose.

KULM BANK will be remodeled, beginning on April 4th, until completion.

Week of September 1, Dr. Bollinger moved to Kulm.  He took over the MEDICAL
TREATMENT for the city.

Week of October 20, a NEW ELEMENTARY SCHOOL was being built.

1961

June 1, NEW ELEMENTARY SCHOOL was finished.

Week of August 3, work on the new POST OFFICE began.

The Weatherlys opened a new CAFE on October 9, 1961.

A new FIREMAN'S TRUCK was purchased in October, 1961.

New POST OFFICE was opened October 21, 1961.

1962

Art Krein was named Lion's president on May 15, 1962 at their regular meeting.

John Peters took over as publisher of THE KULM MESSENGER office, which was owned
by Mr. and Mrs. Nick Novakovich.  Peters arrived on June 19th.

Reinhold Kramlich takes over the KULM LOCKER on July 1st.  John Ruff had
operated until then.

Week of September 19, BRAUER PLUMBING AND HEATING moved to the old FORD GARAGE.

Week of September 19, C & H SERVICE moved (from the old Ford building) to the
old SCHOTT FARM IMPLETMENT.

1964

Week of January 30, Gottlieb Schock moved his INCOME TAX SERVICE office to the
front of the old post office.

Week of March 19, Dr. E.F. Bollinger makes plans to move to Ellendale after
school is out.

Week of May 21, Dr. Hill plans to begin practice here sometime in July.

1965

Weel of May 20, Harold Luck bought the SALZER LUMBER YARD.  He remaned it KULM
LUMBER CO.

1966

Mrs. Albert Hehr became the new manager and trasurer at the KULM CREDIT UNION.
She is replacing Mrs. Anne Mogck.



KULM MILL COMPANY

[picture]  The Kulm Mill Company sold and serviced implements at their location
in Kulm until January of 1963, when a late night fire swept the building.
Firemen fought the blaze in 27 below zero temperatures and strong winds, but
could not save the business.  (Refer to 1957 book)

After the fire, the old chimney and what was still standing of the old steam
plant were demolished.

Brost Bros. Bought the property from Reinhold Beich and, after filling in the
debris and smoothing off the lot, use it for an implement lot.


BRAUER PLUMBING & HEATING

(Refer to 1957 book)  Brauer Plumbing and Heating moved from the building it
occupied during the 1957 Jubilee, in September of 1963, to the Kulm Super
Service Building.  There they operated as plumbing contractors of large
construction projects with Bill's son-in-law, George Oberlander, and other
employees working for the firm.  Brauers went out of business the first of the
year (January 1, 1967).  The digging and septic tank pumping equipment was
bought by Lawrence Doering.

MUELLER DRAY LINE

The dray line operated by Albert Mueller went out of business in 1962, after the
truck wore out and re-investment in another wasn't practical.  (Refer to 1957
book)


KOSANKE DRAY LINE

The dray line operated by Otto Kosanke stopped when Mr. Kosanke passed away in
June of 1959.  He had operated the business for 32 years and had been in
partnership with Henry Reister in the beginning.  (Refer to 1957 book)


ANDERSON STORE

Mr. Schuldheisz continued the business of the Anderson Store for a short period
following the fire and sale to him in 1956.  (Refer to 1957 book)  He later sold
the building to Albert Ernst for a recreation parlor.


KULM PAINT AND BODY SHOP

The Kulm Paint and Body Shop, housed at the north end of town (Refer to 1957
book), closed after Gideon Hilscher was killed in a truck accident on November
30, 1958, near Fredonia.


ED HIEB REAL ESTATE

Ed Hieb, city auditor for over 30 years and the city's only real estate agent in
1957, passed away May 4, 1966.  Since that time, Gottlieb Schock has taken over
chores of the city, and Ben Koenig is licensed to handle real estate.


ED LABRENSZ CREAM STATION

(Refer to 1957 book)  The cream station operated by Ed LaBrensz closed in 1957
and the office in the old bank building was used for a short period as a beauty
shop.  One of the rooms in the old bank is used by Gottlieb Schock for a tax
accountant's office.

Ed LaBrensz passed away in March of 1963.


DENNIS ELECTRIC

Dennis Electric (Refer to 1957 book) operated in Kulm, doing wiring and
electrical contracting until August of 1962, when the Dennis' moved to Omaha.


HETTICH SHOE SHOP

David Hettich operated his shoe shop until recent years, when he was no longer
able to attend to it, and, since then, Mrs. Harold Hettich sells the stock by
appointment.  (Refer to 1957 book)  Mr. David Hettich passed away in June of
1965.


AMERICAN LEGION HALL

The old Reformed Church, converted to the American Legion Hall (Refer to 1957
book), was purchased by Harold Luck in 1959 and remodeled  into a home where the
Luck family now lives.


TRUCKING

Lyle Herman sold his trucking business to Jacob Nill, who actively handled these
chores until he became city policeman early this year (1967).


E & H GROCERY

Part of the A.J. Smith Business Block, E & H Grocery was sold to Milton Lindgren
early in 1959 (Refer to 1957 book).  Milton changed the name to E & M Grocery.
The store was sold out at auction in October of 1961, and since has been
operated as a locker plant and meat market.  Signar Lundgren and Robert Herman
ran it until John Ruff took over in June of 1962.  Reinhold Kramlich took over
in July of 1962 and still operates the store.


ISAAK JEWELRY

The Isaak Jewelry store closed in August of 1962 and John Dobler purchased the
building.  (Refer to 1957 book)


BUTTRICK'S DRY CLEANERS

The dry cleaning establishment was sold to Lyle Bethke in August, 1959, and was
operated as Bud's Cleaners until November, 1963, when the business was closed
and the equipment sold.  Bernie Entzminger, Gackle, bought out the cleaning
business and now makes house to house pickup for his establishment in Gackle.
Buttrick still owns the building, now vacant.  (Refer to 1957 book)


SCHOTT FARM IMPLEMENT

The Schott Farm Implement business ended in November of 1963, when Otto Schott
sold at auction.


CITY AUTO BODY SHOP

Clarence Meidinger and Harold Wolff opened a body repair shop, July 1, 1959, and
did a thriving business in auto repair.  They were housed in the former Frank
Richards Garage, which they remodeled to suit their business.  In April of 1965,
the business sold at auction and Clarence moved to Edgeley, where he now
operates the Edgeley Body Shop.  Harold farms and helps out at the Civic and
Improvement Association, tending bar part time.


KULM SUPER SERVICE

Dan Hoffmann closed Kulm Super Service in September of 1961, but the repair
business remained open under the capable hands of Christ Kruse and Herbert
Hilscher, known as C & H Service.  In April of 1963, William Brauer bought the
building and moved his plumbing and heating business in.  C & Hl Service moved
to the old Schott Farm Store building.  The building is now vacant.


JAKE'S COFFEE SHOP

Jake and Mary Steinwandt operated their cafe until 1958, when they sold the
equipment to the Home Cafe (Clarence and Tina Grosz).  (Refer to 1957 book)  The
Steinwandts now use the entire building for living quarters.


EMIL ANDERSON MORTICIAN

Mr. Anderson retired from his undertaking business about the time of the 1957
Julilee and lived in Kulm until his death, March 10, 1966.  (Refer to 1957 book)


K & K SERVICE

Fred Kinzler, Eugene Kinzler and Emil Klettke (Cities Service Bulk Agent)
started up K & K Service, in a Quonset building on the street leading into Kulm,
in May of 1957.  Fred was the shop man and did a fine job of mechanic work until
July of 1962, when the business was sold at auction.  Fred went to Rapid City,
S.D.  Later, Mel Dallman operated a mechanic shop there for a short time in late
1962.  Jake Nill has been using the building, to shelter his trucks and
tractors, in recent years.


HOLTE FUNERAL HOME

Holte Funeral Homes, Inc. operated a home in Kulm, for a short period, in the
front part of the home where Otto Herman now lives.  (Refer to 1957 book)


CITIES SERVICE BULK

The Cities Service Bulk sales unit was operated by Emil Klettke and Fred Kinzler
and sold to Fred Kinzler in 1958.  It has since gone out of business.


MODERN PAVED ROADS

Highway 13 to Lehr was paved shortly after last Jubilee in 1957 and, in
subsequent years, the pavement was extended to Edgeley.  In more recent times,
Highway 56 was re-graded and paved from Gackle to Fredonia and from Kulm to
Highway 11 to the south.  These modern all weather roads make travel to and from
Kulm much easier and an enjoyable experience.


BLIZZARD HALTS COMMERCE

[picture]  In the spring of 1966, a spring blizzard halted commerce for better
than four days and leaving drifts up to 30 feet deep on Kulm streets.
Considered one of the worst in Dakota history, the picture shows about eight
feet of snow on main street.  Local business did a fine job of keeping city
residents supplied with fuel and food on an emergency basis.  It took days to
clear away emergency routes, weeks to completely free cars and doors, and months
before the final snow melted off.


[picture:  GEORGE GACKLE'S FIRST STORE - - Later Gackle Bros.]


[picture:  STEINWAND AND HARR.  Contracting for buildings on farm and home are
Jake Steinwandt and Reinholt Harr.  First major construction project they did in
town was the Congregational Church Parsonage, dedicated in June of 1958.  Since
that time, many homes and businesses have contracted with them for construction
and remodeling.]
